$$\boxed{\;c_0 \leftrightarrow C_m, \qquad C_m \leftrightarrow c^+, \qquad c_0 \leftrightarrow c^+\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;c_0 \leftrightarrow C_m, \qquad C_m \leftrightarrow c^+, \qquad c_0 \leftrightarrow c^+\;}
```

**The complete bidirectional triangle (K3) is the current topology.** The old chain model ($c_0 \rightleftarrows C_m \rightleftarrows c^+$, in the sense of "no skipping") is not the current topology; the chain is a historical notation.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Cₘ is the EPISTEMIC / MEASUREMENT centre — it is NOT a TOPOLOGICALLY mandatory intermediary.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Cₘ is the EPISTEMIC / MEASUREMENT centre — it is NOT a TOPOLOGICALLY mandatory intermediary.}\;}
```

### Gate and stamp

$$\boxed{\;\text{THE GATE IS BIDIRECTIONAL ON ALL THREE EDGES. Irreversibility lives not in the phase gate but in the ACT OF SEALING.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{THE GATE IS BIDIRECTIONAL ON ALL THREE EDGES. Irreversibility lives not in the phase gate but in the ACT OF SEALING.}\;}
```

The arrow of time is associated not with the direction of transition but with **the accumulation of record and seal**: the record channel is unidirectional (Cₘ → c⁺) and the seal is fixed (author's ruling, 7.1). The physical derivation of this relation has not yet been established (7.8, 16.7).

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines the structure of connections among the three phases |
| **Why does it do it?** | The old chain model left no room for a direct relation between c₀ and c⁺ and made Cₘ a mandatory intermediate stop |
| **How does it do it?** | It connects each phase to the other two by a bidirectional gate; it takes irreversibility away from the gate and assigns it to sealing |
| **What result is expected?** | That the concepts of gate, transport and readout are established consistently on this topology (13.1a) |
| **What result is obtained?** | The complete triangle is canon and has been formally encoded; the triangle is a structure given by the author, not derived from nature |

## 3.7 Map position ≠ phase membership

$$\boxed{\;\text{MAP POSITION} \;\neq\; \text{PHASE MEMBERSHIP}\;}$$


**Example.** Fast subatomic particles are subject to **the laws of Cₘ**. That they appear in the neighbourhood of c⁺ on the phase map does not make them members of c⁺. Map position shows which regime an object **is approaching**, not of which phase it is a member.

## 3.8 $U_x$ — the holistic representation of the three-phase system

> **Historical source record.** The following relation comes from the closing record in the archive. The current topology is the complete bidirectional triangle (Section 3.5); the current definition of $U_x$ is in Section 2.7.

$$\boxed{\;U_0\;\leftrightarrow\;[c_0,\ C_m,\ c^+]_{K_3}\;\equiv\;U_x\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;U_0\;\leftrightarrow\;[c_0,\ C_m,\ c^+]_{K_3}\;\equiv\;U_x\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $[c_0, C_m, c^+]_{K_3}$ | The three phases connected by the complete bidirectional triangle |
| $\equiv$ | "Represents this holistically" — not a set-theoretic type assignment |

The minimal meaning fixed with confidence in this version is:

$$\boxed{\;U_x = \text{the holistic Dynamic Universe in which the three phases are realized together}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;U_x = \text{the holistic Dynamic Universe in which the three phases are realized together}\;}
```

$U_x$ is **the holistic representation** of the three-phase system: it is not a fourth phase or a new node in the phase triangle; it is a **global node** in the global ontology layer. Nor is it another name for $X(x)$:

$$U_x\neq U_0,\qquad U_x\neq X(x),\qquad X\notin\{c_0,C_m,c^+\}$$

```latex
U_x\neq U_0,\qquad U_x\neq X(x),\qquad X\notin\{c_0,C_m,c^+\}
```

This record does not by itself close the full ontological meaning of the relation between $U_0$ and $U_x$; $\leftrightarrow$ and $\equiv$ are not the same relation. Although the interpretation "$U_0$ is only the generating pre-layer, $U_x$ is only the generated system" is reasonable, no additional canonical definition is made in this version; the full meaning of the relation is **an open debt**.

In the core figure, placing c₀ at the top and calling Cₘ "the epistemic / measurement centre" are different layers of representation: the first is a drawing arrangement, the second a position of measurement; neither defines a new topological centre.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It represents the whole of the three phases as a single global object |
| **Why does it do it?** | It must be written explicitly that the Dynamic Universe is a global node separate from the phases |
| **How does it do it?** | It gathers the triangular structure under the name $U_x$; it separates it from $U_0$, from $X$ and from the phases |
| **What result is expected?** | The determination of the structural type of $U_x$ (inclusion or membership) |
| **What result is obtained?** | The minimal meaning is established; the type and the full meaning of the $U_0$–$U_x$ relation are open |

Cₘ is the measurement and epistemic centre; it is not a topologically mandatory intermediary. A linear notation is a historical or pedagogical shorthand |

## 3.11 The causality armour of c⁺

The relation of c⁺ to causality is protected layer by layer:

$$\boxed{\;\text{c⁺ loosens causality; it does not remove causality.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{c⁺ loosens causality; it does not remove causality.}\;}
```

| Phase | State of causality | State of measurement |
|---|---|---|
| c₀ | Pre-measurement — causality not yet established | The concept of measurement is not admissible |
| Cₘ | Causality mandatory and ordered | Measurement mandatory |
| c⁺ | Relation exists but is **timeless**; a phase of correlation | Post-measurement freedom — not a signal channel |

The locked sentence in the archive is: *"Causality is the projection, put into order by measurement in Cₘ, of the timeless correlations in c⁺."* Within this framework, entanglement is read at most as "the Cₘ projection of a phase correlation"; this is a candidate interpretation, not a derivation. In this candidate reading, c⁺-sourced dark-sector effects are handled in Cₘ through gravitational and metric channels; this does not add an internal metric or rest mass to c⁺, and it does not present "interaction only through attraction" as a derived universal mechanism. The ontological identity **c⁺ ≡ dark matter ≡ dark energy ≡ vacuum** is preserved; the quantitative coupling and the observational readout map are open.




# SECTION 4 — $X(x)$: THE DYNAMIC META-RESOLVER

## 4.1 Current definition

$$\boxed{\;X(x) \;=\; \textbf{dynamic upper / meta-RESOLVER}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;X(x) \;=\; \textbf{dynamic upper / meta-RESOLVER}\;}
```

**$X(x)$ (the Dynamic Meta-Resolver):** A staged meta-resolver that **resolves** which phase regime is valid under given conditions and which states are admissible. Scalar readings can be produced from $X$; but $X$ **does not by itself select** the actual physical law, the constitutive map or the physical coefficient. That selection is the open debt of a physical selector (24.1). The path the resolution follows until it reaches a reading in Cₘ is:

$$\text{Context} \to \text{Phase licence} \to X_{\rm resolved} \to \xi_k \to \text{Physical selector} \to J_X/\mathcal E_\phi \to \text{Cₘ state and reading}$$

```latex
\text{Context} \to \text{Phase licence} \to X_{\rm resolved} \to \xi_k \to \text{Physical selector} \to J_X/\mathcal E_\phi \to \text{Cₘ state and reading}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Phase licence | Permission concerning which structure is defined in which phase |
| $X_{\rm resolved}$ | The resolved state of $X$ |
| $\xi_k$ | The $k$-th scalar reading of $X$, $\xi_k=\pi_k(X)$ (6.5) |
| Physical selector | The not-yet-found physical principle that would make the bridge unique |
| $J_X$, $\mathcal E_\phi$ | The bridge from $X$ to physical quantities in Cₘ |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It shows what $X$ resolves and the path from the resolution to a reading in Cₘ |
| **Why does it do it?** | Reading $X$ as "the number that determines everything" would hide the debt of the physical selector |
| **How does it do it?** | It divides the chain into stages: context → phase licence → resolved $X$ → reading → physical selector → bridge → Cₘ reading |
| **What result is expected?** | That, once the physical selector is found, the chain closes all the way to observable values |
| **What result is obtained?** | The first stages of the chain are established ontologically; the physical selector and the bridge are open (14 no-go results, 24.1) |

### Properties

| Property | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Single** | There is one $X$; it is not divided into sub-$X$'s |
| **Phase-sensitive** | It knows which phase it is in |
| **Range-sensitive** | Its output may be interval-valued |
| **Context-sensitive** | It resolves differently according to conditions |
| **Variable arity** | The number of arguments may vary with phase and regime |
| **Deterministic** | The same fully specified state gives the same output |
| **May be recursive / compositional** | It may contain sub-scales |
| **Does not require time** | It does not need internal physical time |

### Guarantee of determinism

$$\boxed{\;S_1 = S_2 \;\Longrightarrow\; X(S_1) = X(S_2)\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;S_1 = S_2 \;\Longrightarrow\; X(S_1) = X(S_2)\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $S_1$, $S_2$ | Two fully specified states (the whole of the phase, regime and context information) |

The same fully specified state gives the same resolution. "State" here covers the whole of the relevant phase, regime and context information; if the provenance record or history is physically relevant, it may be included in the definition of the state. For this reason the principle is not a Markov axiom, and it does not contradict $X$ being "dynamic".

## 4.2 What $X$ is not

$$\boxed{\;X \;\neq\; \text{Force of Attraction} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{gravitation} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{metric} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{a single scalar} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{a logistic function}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;X \;\neq\; \text{Force of Attraction} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{gravitation} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{metric} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{a single scalar} \quad X \;\neq\; \text{a logistic function}\;}
```

In addition, $X$:

- **is not a row of the phase-parameter matrix**; it is the **meta-resolver** of the matrix,
- **is not the average or the sum of properties**,
- **is not confined to the interval $[0,1]$**,
- **is not merely a permission filter**; filtering is one of the things it does, not its definition,
- is **not a carrier of force or energy**.

### The historical logistic kernel

The following form is found in the archive:

$$X(u) = \frac{1}{1 + e^{-s(u - u_t)}}, \qquad \frac{dX}{du} = s\,X(1-X)$$

```latex
X(u) = \frac{1}{1 + e^{-s(u - u_t)}}, \qquad \frac{dX}{du} = s\,X(1-X)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $u$ | Historical process coordinate |
| $s$ | Steepness of the transition |
| $u_t$ | Transition point |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | In the old texts it modelled $X$ as a smooth transition curve between 0 and 1 |
| **Why does it do it?** | To represent phase transitions with a simple, computable phenomenological curve |
| **How does it do it?** | With the logistic (sigmoid) function and its differential equation |
| **What result is expected?** | Imitation of transition behaviour at the model level |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a historical / phenomenological scalar kernel; it is not the current ontological definition.** $X(u)\neq X(x)$; the two must not be confused. "The logistic fundamental $X$" is retired |

### Historical duality record — a formal candidate awaiting decision

The early main archive contains the following "matter–texture / time–geometry" duality form:

$$\Phi(c^+,C_m,c_0)=\mathrm{T}(z^+,z_m,z_0)$$

```latex
\Phi(c^+,C_m,c_0)=\mathrm{T}(z^+,z_m,z_0)
```

The dynamic-balance form in the archive is:

$$\Phi\,e^{+i\pi}=\mathrm{T}\,e^{-i\pi}$$

```latex
\Phi\,e^{+i\pi}=\mathrm{T}\,e^{-i\pi}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Phi$ | The historical "matter–texture" side; its type is incomplete |
| $\mathrm{T}$ | The historical "time–geometry" side; its type is incomplete |
| $z^+$, $z_m$, $z_0$ | Historical phase coordinates |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It proposes a duality between matter and geometry |
| **Why does it do it?** | It was an early attempt to capture the symmetry between phases in a single equation |
| **How does it do it?** | It equates the two sides; in the second form it adds opposite phase factors |
| **What result is expected?** | That it turns into a law of balance once the types are completed |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is not the current definition of $X$; $\Phi$ and $\mathrm{T}$ have no types. Since $e^{+i\pi}=e^{-i\pi}=-1$, the second form reduces algebraically to just $\Phi=\mathrm{T}$; it derives no new dynamical law. The $\Phi$ here is not identical with $\Phi_\chi$ in Section 17. The record is preserved as historical and is not elevated to a postulate |

## 4.3 A programming analogy

A single analogy is used to convey the behaviour of $X$:

```cpp
X = *Resolve(phase, regime, range, conditions, ...);
```

That is, $X$ is not a **value** but **the result of a resolution call**: it takes the context and returns what is valid in that context.

$$\boxed{\;\text{This is ONLY a programming analogy — it is not a mathematical definition.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{This is ONLY a programming analogy — it is not a mathematical definition.}\;}
```

## 4.4 The determinants of $X$ — not a closed list

The working list contains quantities such as density, curvature and entropy density. But:

$$\boxed{\;\text{The set of arguments is NOT CLOSED. } X \text{ CANNOT BE NARROWED to a fixed functional form.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The set of arguments is NOT CLOSED. } X \text{ CANNOT BE NARROWED to a fixed functional form.}\;}
```

Speed, scale, total mass, charge and structural arrangement may also play a role. Moreover, **different sets of arguments** may be meaningful **in different phases**; this is the property of variable arity.

### The final mathematical type is deliberately open

$$\boxed{\;\text{The FULL MATHEMATICAL REALIZATION of } X \text{ has been DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The FULL MATHEMATICAL REALIZATION of } X \text{ has been DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN.}\;}
```

This is not an admission of a deficiency but a **design decision**: an early choice of form would imprison the framework within the limits of that form.

## 4.4a The formal resolver interface — without locking the type early

The conceptual meaning of $X$ is clear; its final mathematical type, by contrast, is deliberately left open. These two situations must not be confused. From the point of view of types, the safest minimal interface can be written as a relation:

$$\mathrm{Resolve}_X(\phi,\mathcal S,\mathcal I;\mathcal O)$$

```latex
\mathrm{Resolve}_X(\phi,\mathcal S,\mathcal I;\mathcal O)
```

The rule of **deterministic output for the same full state** is written as:

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{Resolve}_X(s,o_1)\land \mathrm{Resolve}_X(s,o_2)\;\Longrightarrow\;o_1=o_2\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{Resolve}_X(s,o_1)\land \mathrm{Resolve}_X(s,o_2)\;\Longrightarrow\;o_1=o_2\;}
```

A future dependent-function realization could take the form:

$$X:(\phi:\mathrm{Faz})\to \mathrm{Bağlam}(\phi)\to \mathrm{Çözülmüş}(\phi)$$

```latex
X:(\phi:\mathrm{Faz})\to \mathrm{Bağlam}(\phi)\to \mathrm{Çözülmüş}(\phi)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\phi$ | Phase |
| $\mathcal S$ | Full state / context of conditions |
| $\mathcal I$ | Inputs from the relevant family of properties |
| $\mathcal O$ | The resolved output |
| $s$, $o_1$, $o_2$ | A state and two possible outputs corresponding to it |

For an incompletely defined state or a interval-valued input, the output may also be a range or a relation; for a fully defined admissible state, by contrast, the resolver behaves like a function:

```text
incomplete information                 → relation / range
fully specified admissible state       → deterministic resolution
bidirectionality of the transition     → need not be an exact inverse
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It makes $X$ formally writable without locking its final type |
| **Why does it do it?** | An early functional form limits the framework; but with no interface at all, formal checking cannot be done |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes $X$ as a relation; it sets the rule of deterministic output for a full state; it allows range output under incomplete information |
| **What result is expected?** | That a stronger realization, such as a dependent function, can be chosen later |
| **What result is obtained?** | The relational interface and the determinism rule are established; the dependent-function form is a formal candidate, not the only mandatory type of the current ontology |

### Equation set — variable arity and fixed-point closure

The arguments of $X$ (density $\rho$, curvature $\mathcal{R}$, entropy $\mathcal{S}$) **require a metric**; but the metric exists only in Cₘ. Moreover, the old structure was circular: $X$ determined the phase regime, the phase regime the metric, and the metric the arguments of $X$. This set addresses both problems together:

$$X : \bigsqcup_p \mathcal{A}_p \rightharpoonup \mathrm{Çözüm},\qquad \pi_k:\mathrm{Çözüm}\to\mathrm{Aralık}(D_k) \qquad X^* = \mathcal{F}_X\!\left(\mathcal{A}[X^*]\right)$$

```latex
X : \bigsqcup_p \mathcal{A}_p \rightharpoonup \mathrm{Çözüm},\qquad \pi_k:\mathrm{Çözüm}\to\mathrm{Aralık}(D_k) \qquad X^* = \mathcal{F}_X\!\left(\mathcal{A}[X^*]\right)
```

In the logistic toy model, the fixed point is studied as follows:

$$x = \sigma(a x + c),\quad h(x) = \mathrm{logit}(x) - a x - c,\quad x_\pm = \tfrac12\!\left(1 \pm \sqrt{1 - 4/a}\right)$$

```latex
x = \sigma(a x + c),\quad h(x) = \mathrm{logit}(x) - a x - c,\quad x_\pm = \tfrac12\!\left(1 \pm \sqrt{1 - 4/a}\right)
```

$$a \leq 4 \Rightarrow \text{a single root (sufficient condition)}; \qquad a > 4:\ \text{three roots} \iff c \in \big(g(x_+),\ g(x_-)\big),\ \ g(x) = \mathrm{logit}(x) - a x$$

```latex
a \leq 4 \Rightarrow \text{a single root (sufficient condition)}; \qquad a > 4:\ \text{three roots} \iff c \in \big(g(x_+),\ g(x_-)\big),\ \ g(x) = \mathrm{logit}(x) - a x
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal{A}_p$ | The set of arguments defined in phase $p$ |
| $\bigsqcup_p$ | Disjoint union over the phases |
| $\rightharpoonup$ | Partial resolution relation: $X$ returns not a range but a resolved state |
| $\mathrm{Çözüm}$ | The type of resolved states |
| $\pi_k$, $D_k$ | The $k$-th readout projection and its value domain; it is the reading that is interval-valued, not $X$ itself |
| $\mathcal{F}_X$ | The resolving function of $X$ |
| $X^*$ | Fixed point |
| $\sigma$ | Logistic function |
| $a = \beta k$ | Gain of the logistic feedback |
| $c = \beta(b - u_0)$ | Bias |
| $h(x)$ | The zero function of the fixed-point equation |
| $x_\pm$ | The critical points of $h$ |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It ensures that arguments requiring a metric are used only in the phase in which they are defined, and that the circular definition closes |
| **Why does it do it?** | Operating with arguments that are undefined in c₀ and c⁺, and the circle "$X$ determines the metric, the metric determines $X$", produced inconsistency |
| **How does it do it?** | It changes the argument set of $X$ according to phase (variable arity); it writes the circle as a fixed-point equation, that is, as a staged resolution; in the logistic toy model it studies how many solutions there are |
| **What result is expected?** | That undefined arguments are blocked at the type level; that the circle is brought into closed form |
| **What result is obtained?** | Variable arity is established; the staged resolver is the core preference; the logistic fixed point is a model; "the logistic fundamental $X$" and "$a\le4 \iff$ a single root" are retired ($a\le4$ is only a sufficient condition). The existence and uniqueness of a solution are separate from the convergence of the chosen algorithm: despite a single fixed point, a direct iteration may be locally unstable ($f(x)=\sigma(4-8x)$, $x_*=1/2$, $f'(x_*)=-2$). General algorithmic convergence for $X$ awaits derivation |

## 4.5 Readout faces

$X$ is a single object; it shows different faces according to how it is accessed.

| Face | What it is |
|---|---|
| **Basic state face** | The resolved face of the phase and context state; producing a physical constant or an observable value additionally requires a selector, a bridge and a readout law |
| **Domain projection** | Its reflection onto a particular physical domain |
| **Reading** | The value actually measured in Cₘ |

### Domain projections are not a closed list

Projections onto particular domains (such as nuclear, cosmological, chronometric) can be defined. But:

$$\boxed{\;\text{These projections are NOT SEPARATE FUNDAMENTAL } X\text{'s, and they do NOT form A CLOSED CANONICAL LIST.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{These projections are NOT SEPARATE FUNDAMENTAL } X\text{'s, and they do NOT form A CLOSED CANONICAL LIST.}\;}
```

**There is a single $X$.** The projections are shadows of the same $X$ in different domains.

### The limit of the readout layer

$$\boxed{\;\text{A calculation made in the readout layer CANNOT PRODUCE A FUNDAMENTAL NUMBER.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{A calculation made in the readout layer CANNOT PRODUCE A FUNDAMENTAL NUMBER.}\;}
```

For example, setting up a definitional identity between two quantities of the readout layer is not "deriving" one from the other. The unknown does not close; it **changes place**.

### The historical B-hypothesis — reducing $X$ to a reading

In the archive's discussions of an action principle there is a **B-hypothesis** of the form "$X$ is not a fundamental field but a post-solution observable map". This is a sourced model option; it is not the current core definition.

$$\boxed{\;X\text{ is not merely a reading.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;X\text{ is not merely a reading.}\;}
```

The current architecture preserves the basic-state, domain-projection and reading faces of $X$ together. The B-hypothesis could be tested later in a model comparison; but if it reduces $X$ to the readout layer alone, it conflicts with the core definition of the resolver.

## 4.6 $X$ is a supra-phase meta-coordination structure; it is not a physical carrier

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{MetaCrossPermission}(X) = \text{true},\qquad \mathrm{MetaCrossPermission} \neq \mathrm{TransportPermission}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{MetaCrossPermission}(X) = \text{true},\qquad \mathrm{MetaCrossPermission} \neq \mathrm{TransportPermission}\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| MetaCrossPermission | Permission for inter-phase meta-coordination (specific to $X$) |
| TransportPermission | Permission for a quantity to be physically transported through a gate (13.1a) |

$$\boxed{\;\text{The supra-phase status of } X \text{ is NOT DERIVED SOLELY from its being dimensionless.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The supra-phase status of } X \text{ is NOT DERIVED SOLELY from its being dimensionless.}\;}
```

Dimensionlessness is not a general theorem for physical transport; whether a quantity can be transported depends on the transport permission of 13.1a. $X$ is not a physical carrier but a meta-coordination structure; dimensionlessness may be a **consequence** of this character, not its cause.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines how $X$ can "pass" between phases |
| **Why does it do it?** | That $X$ is supra-phase could be confused with a physical quantity being transported through gates |
| **How does it do it?** | It defines meta-coordination permission as a permission separate from physical transport permission |
| **What result is expected?** | That "smuggling" Cₘ-specific quantities through $X$ is prevented |
| **What result is obtained?** | The distinction has been formally encoded: meta-crossing is not physical transport |

## 4.7 The phase landscape

The vertices of the phase map are **regime examples** of $X$; they are not fixed anchor values. Approaching a vertex means that the locking effect of that regime increases.

$$\boxed{\;\text{The vertices are NOT FIXED; they are PHASE-REGIME EXAMPLES of the dynamic } X\text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The vertices are NOT FIXED; they are PHASE-REGIME EXAMPLES of the dynamic } X\text{.}\;}
```

Most physical structures — planets, atoms, electromagnetic phenomena, biological structures — are found not at any vertex but **in the interior region under the influence of the three phases** (12.3).
## 4.8 The recursive macro-object reading

$X$ can be thought of as a **recursive descriptor** that summarizes the context in which an object is found. It is one thing for an atom; another for the molecule containing it; another again for the cell containing the molecule. At each scale it is resolved anew, and it **contains the sub-scales but is not reduced to them**. This is an **analogy**: it gives structural intuition, it does not produce results.




# SECTION 5 — THE PHASE-PARAMETER MATRIX

## 5.1 Status

$$\boxed{\;\text{The phase-parameter matrix is one of the CORE structures of the framework.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The phase-parameter matrix is one of the CORE structures of the framework.}\;}
```

This matrix is not an option, a "risky extension" or merely a tool of presentation; as a methodological decision, it belongs to the core. The **correct** side of the analogy with Mendeleev's periodic table is this: Mendeleev did not discover the elements, he **ordered** them; and the order revealed **gaps**. The phase-parameter matrix likewise orders physical quantities according to phase and makes visible the place of bridges not yet known.

## 5.2 Main architecture

$$\boxed{\;\text{PropertyFamily} \times \text{Phase} \;\longrightarrow\; \text{Range} \;\;\text{or}\;\; \bot\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{PropertyFamily} \times \text{Phase} \;\longrightarrow\; \text{Range} \;\;\text{or}\;\; \bot\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| PropertyFamily | A family of quantities such as time, metric, energy, density |
| Phase | c₀, Cₘ or c⁺ |
| Range | The band of values the quantity takes in that phase |
| $\bot$ | The mark that the quantity is not admissible in that phase ($\bot_0$ or $\bot_+$) |

Each cell gives the state of a property family in a particular phase: either a range, or the mark **not admissible** in that phase.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It arranges the state of every family of physical quantities in every phase in a single table |
| **Why does it do it?** | Without recording which quantity is defined in which phase, Cₘ concepts leak into c₀ and c⁺ |
| **How does it do it?** | To each combination of property family and phase it assigns either a range or a mark of absence |
| **What result is expected?** | That the empty cells show which bridges must be derived |
| **What result is obtained?** | The architecture is core; for most cells the numerical bridge has not yet been derived |

### Where is $X$ in the matrix?

$$\boxed{\;X \text{ is NOT A ROW of the matrix. } X \text{ is the META-RESOLVER of the matrix.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;X \text{ is NOT A ROW of the matrix. } X \text{ is the META-RESOLVER of the matrix.}\;}
```

The matrix does not contain $X$; $X$ resolves the matrix. This is why there is no $X$ row in the table of 5.4; $X$ is the resolver information above the table.

## 5.3 Cell types — five different "empties" and one state of knowledge

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $0$ | The real numerical zero |
| $[0,0]$ | A valid, zero-width physical range |
| $\texttt{None}$ | No stamp was produced in this transition ($\text{NoStamp} \neq \text{NoTransition}$) |
| $\bot_0$ | In c₀: the concept is **not yet established** — undefined |
| $\bot_+$ | In c⁺: the concept is **dissolved** — no longer present in the language of that phase |

These must not be confused with one another. For example, internal physical time in c₀ is $\bot_0$, not $0$; even saying "zero time has passed" requires a concept of time. In c⁺, time is $\bot_+$: the structure that had been established in Cₘ is dissolved there.

Gathering the two absences under a single $\bot$ loses information, because **"not yet" and "no longer" are not the same thing.** This distinction is a direct consequence of the author's ruling that time, three-dimensional space and the limit $c$ are valid only in Cₘ — undefined in c₀, dissolved in c⁺. The distinction has been formally verified and enforced by a phase-indexed value type: a Cₘ-specific value cannot be constructed as "present" for c₀ or c⁺.

$$\boxed{\;\bot_0 \neq \bot_+ \neq 0 \neq [0,0] \neq \texttt{None}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\bot_0 \neq \bot_+ \neq 0 \neq [0,0] \neq \texttt{None}\;}
```

Apart from these, there is one more situation: **missing data.** When we cannot measure or do not know a quantity, this does not mean that the quantity is ontologically absent; it is a **state of knowledge**. Missing data is none of the five "empties" above and is kept on a separate axis.

*Reading rule:* When $\bot$ appears alone in the document, it is read as $\bot_0$ in a c₀ context and as $\bot_+$ in a c⁺ context.

### 5.3a The kind of a quantity and its dimension are not the same thing

The physical dimension of a quantity (mass, length, time) and what kind of quantity it is are different things. An old record said "mass is carried in c⁺"; this sentence confuses two things. The **dimension label** of mass can be seen in every context as a phase-independent label; but **rest mass** in Cₘ is a Cₘ-specific quantity and is not carried into c⁺. Likewise, the metric is not derived from a dimension label; where the metric is defined is stated separately: only in Cₘ.

### Equation set — two absences, phase-indexed value, global nodes

The author's ruling is: *"Time, three-dimensional Euclidean–flat space, the phase boundary c and similar fundamental definitions are valid in Cₘ. These structures are undefined in c₀ and dissolved in c⁺."* A single $\bot$ could not distinguish these two **ontologically different** absences. In the formal core, the distinction is encoded as follows:

```lean
inductive CmOnlyValue (α : Type) : Phase → Type
  | undefined0    : CmOnlyValue α .c0
  | presentCm     : α → CmOnlyValue α .Cm
  | dissolvedPlus : CmOnlyValue α .cp
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\bot_0$ / `undefined0` / `preAdmissible` | c₀: the structure is **not yet established** |
| $\bot_+$ / `dissolvedPlus` / `dissolved` | c⁺: the structure is **dissolved** |
| `presentCm` | A value present in Cₘ |
| `CmOnlyValue α p` | Phase-indexed value type: it can carry a real value only in Cₘ |
| `Knowledge` | Epistemic state: `observed` · `missingData` · `imputed` |
| `GlobalNode` | $U_0$, $U_x$, $U_+$ |
| `isPole` | $U_0$ and $U_+$ are poles; **$U_x$ is not** |
| `MetricAdmissible p` | The metric is admissible only in Cₘ |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It formally separates the two ontological absences, missing data and the global nodes from one another |
| **Why does it do it?** | A single mark of absence erased the difference between "not yet established" (c₀) and "dissolved" (c⁺); missing data could also be mistaken for ontological absence |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds a phase-indexed type: in c₀ only "undefined", in c⁺ only "dissolved", and in Cₘ a real value can be constructed; it keeps the state of knowledge on a separate axis |
| **What result is expected?** | That constructing a Cₘ value in the wrong phase is impossible at the compiler level |
| **What result is obtained?** | The two ontological absences and the phase-indexed value have been formally verified as the core preference; "$U_x$ is not a pole" has been verified |

## 5.4 Extended example matrix

The following table is **not a final numerical table**. Its purpose is to show the scope of the property families and their kinds in each phase. If a cell has no numerical value, the reason is not an omission but that the numerical bridge has not yet been derived.

| Property family | Notation | c₀ | Cₘ | c⁺ | Ontological note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Internal physical time | $T(x)$ | $\bot_0$ | Range / physical time | $\bot_+$ | Time is specific to Cₘ |
| Causality / speed family | $C(x)$ | Cₘ speed language $\bot_0$ | Band $0<v<c$ (core membership ruling); $v=c$ liminal shared boundary | Cₘ speed language $\bot_+$ | c⁺ is a beyond-$c$ regime; Cₘ-type $dx/dt>c$ does not apply (3.4) |
| Force of Attraction projection family | $G(x)$, $g(x)$ | Phase regime | Measurable reading | Upper-phase regime | $g(x)\neq g_{\mu\nu}$ |
| Metric | $g_{\mu\nu}$ | $\bot_0$ | Defined | $\bot_+$ | Only Cₘ |
| Topology / dimension | $d(x)$ | Non-metric | Cₘ geometry (3D Euclidean base space) | Non-metric | The question "how many dimensions?" requires separate formalization |
| Energy | $e(x)$ | Range / phase-specific meaning | Measurable | The Cₘ energy type is not carried directly; sealed share | Accounting at the level of $U_x$ (16.7a) |
| Scale | $s(x)$ | Range | Range | Range | May be one of the context inputs of $X$ |
| Entropy / order | $S(x)$ | Pre-temporal meaning | Thermodynamic reading | Meaning as timeless record | The three are not raw-equal to one another |
| Regulation / reporting | $\Gamma(x)$ | Produces no internal time | Family of time / readout coefficients | Produces no internal time | Not identical with $X$ |
| Impedance | $Z(x)$ | Model-dependent | May be a measurable bridge | Model-dependent | Open to the channel bridge |
| Momentum | $p(x)$ | Standard Cₘ type $\bot$ / open | Standard reading | Standard Cₘ type $\bot$ / open | Not carried unchanged between phases |
| Cₘ metric length | $L_{C_m}$ | $\bot_0$ | Range | $\bot_+$ | "Diluted length" is not used for c⁺ |
| Density | $\rho(x)$ | Collective single structure; $\rho_{c_0}\le\rho_P$ | Physical density | Cₘ matter density $\bot_+$ | There is no "infinite density" for c₀ |
| Matter | $m(x)$ | Metric-gapless single structure | Differentiated matter | Cₘ matter support dissolves | One of the strongest phase distinctions |
| Empty space / vacuum | — | Metric-gapless single structure | "Empty-space" reading (including inside the atom) | c⁺ ≡ vacuum | Every place that Cₘ reads as empty space is c⁺ (3.4) |
| Information | $I(x)$ | Potential / pre-temporal | Measurable record | Meaning as sealed provenance | A global conservation theorem is open |
| Record / provenance (registry / provenance) | $R(x)$ | Not an archive | May be an event source | Role of sealed archive | Record = not an entity |
| Force / field family | $F(x)$ | Phase regime | Effective interactions | Phase regime | Projection derivation from the Force of Attraction is open |
| Potential / scaffold | $\Phi(x)$ | Model / scaffold | Cₘ effective-field-theory scaffold | Model / scaffold | $X\neq\Phi$ |
| Gauge / kinetic | $Z_B$, $Z_W$, $Z_G$ | Reserved programme | Cₘ effective-field reading | Reserved programme | Standard Model machine-1 target |
| Couplings | $\alpha_i(x)$ | Bridge open | Effective constants | Bridge open | No numerical derivation |
| Temperature | $\mathcal T(x)$ | $\bot_0$ | Thermodynamic reading | $\bot_+$ | $0$ and $\bot$ must be separated; c₀ ≠ absolute zero |
| Heat | $Q(x)$ | $\bot_0$ | Thermodynamic reading | $\bot_+$ | "No heat" is not automatically $T=0$ |
| Frequency / clock | $\nu(x)$ | Internal frequency $\bot_0$ | Measurable | Internal frequency $\bot_+$ | A physical period requires Cₘ time |
| Pressure / stress | $P(x)$ | Meaning as phase limit | Energy–momentum reading | Cₘ pressure type $\bot$ / open | Not carried by itself to the outer phases |
| Coherence | $\mathcal C(x)$ | Collective | Quantum / classical regimes | Meaning as coordination | The BEC analogy is not literal |
| Measurement | $\mathcal M(x)$ | Direct measurement $\bot_0$ | Direct measurement | Direct measurement $\bot_+$ | Cₘ is the epistemic centre |
| Archive state | $A(x)$ | Not an archive | Source of records | Sealed-provenance phase | c⁺ is not an oracle of the future |
| Accessibility / threshold | $\mathcal A(x)$ | Range | Range | Range | May be tied to transition permission |

Some of these rows have only the status of a **reserved property family**. That the matrix is core does not mean that every row has a numerical law today.

## 5.5 The list of rows is open

$$\boxed{\;\text{The METHODOLOGICAL DECISION is core; the NUMBER and ORDER OF ROWS are open.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The METHODOLOGICAL DECISION is core; the NUMBER and ORDER OF ROWS are open.}\;}
```

Which property families the matrix will contain, and in what order they will be arranged, is not closed. This is not a deficiency; it follows from the table being **a living working tool**.

### Numerical values in historical compilations

Numerical coefficients coming from archive surveys do not enter the matrix unless directly verified. Constants coming from a retired line **cannot be brought back** (Section 21).



# SECTION 6 — THE RANGE FORMALISM

## 6.1 Basic syntax

$$\boxed{\;P \in [P^-,\, P^+]\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;P \in [P^-,\, P^+]\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $P$ | Any physical quantity |
| $P^-$, $P^+$ | Lower and upper bound |

Physical numerical expressions are interval-valued. This is a rule about **how the framework writes** quantities. Measurements and model parameters alike actually come as a band; rather than hiding this band, Evertophy carries it explicitly.

## 6.2 A zero-width range is fully valid

$$\boxed{\;[P,\,P] \text{ is a FULLY VALID, STATIC and DETERMINISTIC physical range.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;[P,\,P] \text{ is a FULLY VALID, STATIC and DETERMINISTIC physical range.}\;}
```

This is the most frequently misunderstood point of the framework and must be stated explicitly:

| Misreading | What is correct |
|---|---|
| ~~"Point-valued physics is forbidden"~~ | Interval-valued syntax is used; $[P,P]$ is legitimate |
| ~~"$[P,P]$ is a special threshold case"~~ | **No** special mechanism is required |
| ~~"$[P,P]$ is a Heaviside or structural singularity"~~ | There is **no** such necessity |
| ~~"Every constant varies with $X$"~~ | A quantity **may remain** $[P,P]$ in a given regime |

$$\boxed{\;\text{Being interval-valued} \;\nRightarrow\; \text{being time-variable.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Being interval-valued} \;\nRightarrow\; \text{being time-variable.}\;}
```

Writing a quantity in range syntax **does not imply** that it varies. A static and deterministic constant is written as $[P,P]$, and this is entirely correct.

## 6.3 Three kinds of range

| Symbol | Name | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| $\Delta X_{\rm acc}$ | **Acceptance range** | The range permitted by the framework |
| $\Delta X_{\rm pred}$ | **Prediction range** | The range **derived** from the framework |
| $\Delta X_{\rm obs}$ | **Observation range** | The range coming from the data |

**The falsification rule operates only between prediction and observation:**

$$\boxed{\;\Delta X_{\rm pred} \;\cap\; \Delta X_{\rm obs} = \varnothing \;\Longrightarrow\; \text{falsified.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\Delta X_{\rm pred} \;\cap\; \Delta X_{\rm obs} = \varnothing \;\Longrightarrow\; \text{falsified.}\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\cap$ | Intersection |
| $\varnothing$ | The empty set |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines when a prediction is falsified |
| **Why does it do it?** | If it remains unclear which ranges are to be compared, a disagreement between two observations, or the width of the acceptance range, could be mistaken for falsification |
| **How does it do it?** | It compares the derived prediction band with the observation band; if the intersection is empty, the prediction is falsified |
| **What result is expected?** | An explicit, previously defined criterion of falsification |
| **What result is obtained?** | The rule is canon; disagreement between two **observation** ranges does not falsify a theory. Hard rejection is given only for an empty intersection (21.6) |

## 6.3a What is a range and what is a constant — layers

The Range Formalism does not mean "turn everything into a range". As the author emphasizes, some equations use a constant rather than a range, and this is not an error. What is correct is to know to which layer each magnitude belongs:

| Layer | Example | How it is carried |
|---|---|---|
| Structurally exact | $\pi$, the number of phases 3, counts | Exact — not turned into a range |
| Definitional point | $c_{C_m} = [c,c]$ | Zero-width range $[P,P]$ |
| Uncertain or variable physical parameter | $\sigma$, $\beta$, $k$, $\Delta E$, $\eta_{\rm clock}$; well width, couplings, energy spread | Range or joint feasibility set |
| Epistemic | Measurement uncertainty | Range or set |
| Extended | If the energy spread contains zero ($\Delta E\ni0$), the time bound extends to $+\infty$ | Extended endpoint |
| Phase-absent | Time in c₀ and c⁺ | $\bot_0$ / $\bot_+$ |

A parameter that is genuinely taken as constant remains constant, with this assumption written explicitly; if necessary, it is carried as $[P,P]$. Using a constant is not, by itself, a deficiency, a failure or grounds for partial success.

## 6.4 The prohibition of vacuity

$$\boxed{\;[0,\infty) \text{ is a valid extended range; but if it does not narrow the prior domain, it carries no predictive power.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;[0,\infty) \text{ is a valid extended range; but if it does not narrow the prior domain, it carries no predictive power.}\;}
```

The problem is not that such a range is invalid, but that **it gives no information**: it "fits" every dataset and therefore does not count as a distinctive prediction band. A prediction range must be **derived** and **meaningfully narrower than the prior range**.

## 6.4a Reliability of range arithmetic

The basic operations of range arithmetic have been formally proven **in the exact integer band layer**. Addition, negation, intersection and multiplication are safe: if the true values lie inside the ranges, the result lies inside the resulting range. For multiplication, the four-corner rule holds:

$$x\in[a,b],\ y\in[c,d] \;\Rightarrow\; xy \in \big[\min(ac,ad,bc,bd),\ \max(ac,ad,bc,bd)\big]$$

```latex
x\in[a,b],\ y\in[c,d] \;\Rightarrow\; xy \in \big[\min(ac,ad,bc,bd),\ \max(ac,ad,bc,bd)\big]
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $x$, $y$ | True values |
| $[a,b]$, $[c,d]$ | The ranges containing them |
| `Band` | $\langle lo, hi, \text{valid}: lo\le hi\rangle$ — a range with a proof of validity |
| `mem x b` | $lo\le x\le hi$ |
| `cmin`, `cmax` | The smallest and largest of the four corner products |
| `ZeroExcluded b` | $0<lo$ or $hi<0$ — an invertible range |
| `Ext` | Endpoints $-\infty$ · finite · $+\infty$ |

All three safeguards are established: a range containing zero cannot be inverted; the empty set and $[0,0]$ are different; $+\infty$ is an endpoint, not an absence.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It guarantees that calculation with ranges is safe, that is, that it never leaves the true value outside |
| **Why does it do it?** | If the validity of a range ($lo\le hi$) and the soundness of the operations are not proven, all prediction bands become unreliable |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds a band type that carries a proof of validity; it formally proves addition, negation, intersection and four-corner multiplication in the exact integer layer |
| **What result is expected?** | A complete range algebra: exact inversion and division, a full algebra with extended endpoints, tracking of dependencies between variables |
| **What result is obtained?** | The basic exact arithmetic is the core preference and is proven; full range arithmetic is partly established. This is not an ontological debt but a debt of library engineering |

## 6.5 Sensitivity

Since $X$ is not a single number, the sensitivity of a quantity to $X$ cannot be written directly in the form $d\ln c/dX$; the derivative is taken through a particular reading of $X$:

$$S_c^{(k)} \equiv \frac{\partial \ln c}{\partial \xi_k} \in [S^-, S^+], \qquad \xi_k = \pi_k(X)$$

```latex
S_c^{(k)} \equiv \frac{\partial \ln c}{\partial \xi_k} \in [S^-, S^+], \qquad \xi_k = \pi_k(X)
```

For a transition edge, the reading is written with the same discipline:

$$\xi_{ij} \in \big[\xi^{\rm crit,-}_{ij},\ \xi^{\rm crit,+}_{ij}\big]\ \ (\text{point reading}) \qquad B_{\xi_{ij}} \cap \big[\xi^{\rm crit,-}_{ij},\ \xi^{\rm crit,+}_{ij}\big] \neq \varnothing\ \ (\text{band reading})$$

```latex
\xi_{ij} \in \big[\xi^{\rm crit,-}_{ij},\ \xi^{\rm crit,+}_{ij}\big]\ \ (\text{point reading}) \qquad B_{\xi_{ij}} \cap \big[\xi^{\rm crit,-}_{ij},\ \xi^{\rm crit,+}_{ij}\big] \neq \varnothing\ \ (\text{band reading})
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $X$ | Non-scalar, staged resolver ($X_{\rm prior}\to X_{\rm resolved}\to X_{\rm read}$) |
| $\pi_k$ | The $k$-th readout projection |
| $\xi_k$ | The $k$-th scalar reading of $X$ |
| $S_c^{(k)}$ | The sensitivity of $c$ to $\xi_k$ |
| $\xi_{ij}$ | The reading specific to the edge $i\to j$ |
| $\xi^{\rm crit,\pm}_{ij}$ | The critical reading band of that edge |
| $B_{\xi_{ij}}$ | The band, if the reading itself is a band |

If $S\approx0$, the quantity is practically constant in that regime; this is fully consistent with the framework.

The core ruling for the speed of light is this: in Cₘ, $c$ is a definitional point interval, $c_{C_m}=[c,c]$; consequently, in the core the sensitivity of $c$ to any reading is zero. "A varying speed of light" can be treated only as an effective model quantity, and can be tested only through **dimensionless** observables such as the fine-structure constant; a change in the choice of units does not count as an observation.

*Reading rule:* Historical or model expressions in the document in which a scalar operation (derivative, inversion, logarithm, limit) is applied to $X$ are not core statements but historical models or not-yet-established bridges, until a reading $\xi=\pi(X)$ is explicitly defined. Merely replacing the letter $X$ by $\xi$ does not count as establishing the physical bridge. The problem is not that a vector or tensor derivative cannot be taken in general, but that **the differential structure required for the fundamental $X$ has not been given**.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It ensures that every scalar operation depending on $X$ is carried out through a defined reading |
| **Why does it do it?** | The old canon sometimes compared $X$ like a single number ($X(x)>X(0)$) and took its derivative ($d\ln c/dX$); for a multi-component object these operations are undefined |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes every operation through a projection $\xi_k=\pi_k(X)$; for transition conditions it uses the edge-specific reading |
| **What result is expected?** | That, once a physical projection is chosen, sensitivity and transition conditions acquire quantitative form |
| **What result is obtained?** | The projection discipline is a core principle; the exact type of $X$ and the physical projection are open |

## 6.6 The sector hierarchy must be derived

$$\boxed{\;\text{Which sector is CONSTANT and which is VARIABLE CANNOT BE ASSIGNED — it MUST BE DERIVED.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Which sector is CONSTANT and which is VARIABLE CANNOT BE ASSIGNED — it MUST BE DERIVED.}\;}
```

Otherwise the formalism accommodates every result and forbids nothing.

## 6.7 Range-safe sealing

$$\mathrm{Seal}\big([p^-,\,p^+]\big) \;=\; \big(\mathrm{rid},\; [p^-,\,p^+],\; \text{stamp}\big)$$

```latex
\mathrm{Seal}\big([p^-,\,p^+]\big) \;=\; \big(\mathrm{rid},\; [p^-,\,p^+],\; \text{stamp}\big)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Seal | The sealing operation |
| rid | The unique identity of the record |
| $[p^-,p^+]$ | The physical content of the record (a range) |
| stamp | The image of the transition in the stamp monoid (Section 14) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines how interval-valued content is sealed |
| **Why does it do it?** | If sealing forced the content to a single point, it would contradict the Range Formalism |
| **How does it do it?** | It gives the record a unique identity, leaves the content as a range and adds the stamp |
| **What result is expected?** | That the record identity is unique and the physical content honestly remains a band |
| **What result is obtained?** | The record identity is unique; the content may remain a range; $p^-=p^+$ is not forced |



# SECTION 7 — THE ONTOLOGY OF TIME

## 7.1 The only home of physical time

$$\boxed{\;\text{EMERGENT PHYSICAL TIME EXISTS ONLY IN Cₘ.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{EMERGENT PHYSICAL TIME EXISTS ONLY IN Cₘ.}\;}
```

c₀ is the pre-temporal (pre-emergent) potential / core phase: time there is not yet established. c⁺ is the atemporal upper / archive phase: the time established in Cₘ is dissolved there. In neither phase are the internal clock, speed, duration or physical flow variables of Cₘ used.

$$\boxed{\;t_{c_0}\text{ and }t_{c^+}\text{ are not variables of the core ontology:}\quad T(c_0)=\bot_0,\qquad T(c^+)=\bot_+\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;t_{c_0}\text{ and }t_{c^+}\text{ are not variables of the core ontology:}\quad T(c_0)=\bot_0,\qquad T(c^+)=\bot_+\;}
```

The expression "$dt=0$" may be used in both phases only as a pedagogical shorthand; the ontologically correct statement is **"physical time is not admissible in this phase"**.

**Author's ruling — the direction of time.** Time exists only in Cₘ and is unidirectional; otherwise causality would disappear, because in the matter phase everything stands in a cause–effect relation. Phase transitions are bidirectional; the channel of time sealing / record, by contrast, is unidirectional (Cₘ → c⁺), and once sealing has taken place the record is fixed (13.1, 14.1).

$$\text{gate: } c_i\rightleftarrows c_j\ \ (\text{bidirectional}),\qquad \text{record channel: } C_m\longrightarrow c^+\ \ (\text{unidirectional; seal fixed})$$

```latex
\text{gate: } c_i\rightleftarrows c_j\ \ (\text{bidirectional}),\qquad \text{record channel: } C_m\longrightarrow c^+\ \ (\text{unidirectional; seal fixed})
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $c_i$, $c_j$ | Any two of the three phases (complete bidirectional triangle, 3.5) |
| $\rightleftarrows$ | Bidirectional phase gate |
| $C_m\longrightarrow c^+$ | Unidirectional record / sealing channel |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines where time exists and why it is unidirectional |
| **Why does it do it?** | Although transitions between phases are bidirectional, the source of the unidirectional experience of time must be explained |
| **How does it do it?** | It takes irreversibility away from the transition and assigns it to the record channel: the transition is bidirectional, the record is unidirectional in the Cₘ → c⁺ direction and fixed |
| **What result is expected?** | That the thermodynamic arrow of time and causality are quantitatively related to this record structure |
| **What result is obtained?** | That time exists only in Cₘ and is unidirectional, and the direction of the record channel, are author's rulings; the quantitative derivation is open (7.8, 16.7) |

## 7.2 The production of time — the core scaffold

The most general working form preserved in the core is:

$$\boxed{\;dt \;=\; \Gamma\,\mathcal{G}_{C_m}\,d\tau\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;dt \;=\; \Gamma\,\mathcal{G}_{C_m}\,d\tau\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $dt$ | The interval of physical time read in Cₘ |
| $\Gamma$ | The coefficient of the Cₘ readout / reporting-regulation family |
| $\mathcal{G}_{C_m}$ | A not yet closed object representing the structure of measurable change and differentiation within Cₘ |
| $\tau$ | **Formal ordering / parametrization variable**; it is not a hidden fundamental physical time |

$$\boxed{\;\tau \neq \text{fundamental physical time}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\tau \neq \text{fundamental physical time}\;}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes how time is read from distinguishable change in Cₘ |
| **Why does it do it?** | Time is not a fundamental dimension but an emergent reading specific to Cₘ; a general form for this is needed |
| **How does it do it?** | It ties the time interval to an ordering parameter, as the product of the structure of change in Cₘ and a readout coefficient |
| **What result is expected?** | That time is produced quantitatively once the microphysical definition of $\mathcal{G}_{C_m}$ is established |
| **What result is obtained?** | The scaffold is preserved in the core; a unique microphysical definition of $\mathcal{G}_{C_m}$ is open |

## 7.3 Normalized realization — model level

When dimensional accounting is required, the following normalized form can be used:

$$dt \;=\; \tau_0\,\tilde\Gamma\,\mathcal{G}_{C_m}\,d\lambda$$

```latex
dt \;=\; \tau_0\,\tilde\Gamma\,\mathcal{G}_{C_m}\,d\lambda
```

$$[\lambda]=1,\qquad [\tilde\Gamma]=1,\qquad [\tau_0]=T,\qquad \Gamma\equiv\tau_0\tilde\Gamma\ \Rightarrow\ dt=\Gamma\,\mathcal G_{C_m}\,d\lambda$$

```latex
[\lambda]=1,\qquad [\tilde\Gamma]=1,\qquad [\tau_0]=T,\qquad \Gamma\equiv\tau_0\tilde\Gamma\ \Rightarrow\ dt=\Gamma\,\mathcal G_{C_m}\,d\lambda
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $[\cdot]$ | Physical dimension |
| $T$ | The dimension of time |
| $\lambda$ | Dimensionless ordering / parametrization variable; it is not physical time |
| $\tilde\Gamma$ | Dimensionless readout coefficient |
| $\tau_0$ | The time scale of the realization; it carries the dimension of time |

In a Cₘ quantum / open-system model, the structure of change can be written with a density-matrix norm:

$$\mathcal{G}_{C_m} \stackrel{\rm model}{=} \left\|\frac{d\rho_{C_m}}{d\lambda}\right\|$$

```latex
\mathcal{G}_{C_m} \stackrel{\rm model}{=} \left\|\frac{d\rho_{C_m}}{d\lambda}\right\|
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\rho_{C_m}$ | The Cₘ density matrix (a model object; not a core primitive) |
| $\|\cdot\|$ | Candidate norms: trace norm, Hilbert–Schmidt norm, operator norm |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It gives a dimensionally consistent realization of the time scaffold |
| **Why does it do it?** | If it is unclear in which term the dimension of time is carried, the equation becomes dimensionally inconsistent |
| **How does it do it?** | It gives the dimension only to $\tau_0$; it keeps $\lambda$ and $\tilde\Gamma$ dimensionless; in a quantum model it writes the structure of change with the norm of the change of the density matrix |
| **What result is expected?** | That the norm is selected by the physical bridge and by a test of parametrization independence |
| **What result is obtained?** | The dimensional accounting is correct; if $\Gamma$ is taken as dimensionless, $dt$ does not carry the dimension of time — this case is recorded in the formal type system as a negative test that does not compile. This realization is **one** realization of the core scaffold, not its only mandatory form; no norm has been selected |

## 7.4 Parametrization independence — formal candidate

In a realization, an object resembling arc length can be constructed:

$$s(\lambda)=\int \left\|\frac{d\rho_{C_m}}{d\lambda}\right\|d\lambda$$

```latex
s(\lambda)=\int \left\|\frac{d\rho_{C_m}}{d\lambda}\right\|d\lambda
```

This object can be constructed to be invariant under order-preserving reparametrizations. It provides a useful consistency test; but it does not make the chosen norm, or the ontological status of $\rho_{C_m}$, unique.

### Active achromaticity — a Lorentz-compatible readout constraint

Independence from the energy of the measurement probe is not entirely historical. Under the same physical state $\mathcal S$ and the same admissible Cₘ readout regime:

$$\boxed{\;\left.\frac{\partial X}{\partial E_{\rm probe}}\right|_{\mathcal S}=0,\qquad \left.\frac{\partial\Gamma}{\partial E_{\rm probe}}\right|_{\mathcal S}=0\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\left.\frac{\partial X}{\partial E_{\rm probe}}\right|_{\mathcal S}=0,\qquad \left.\frac{\partial\Gamma}{\partial E_{\rm probe}}\right|_{\mathcal S}=0\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $E_{\rm probe}$ | The energy of the measurement probe |
| $\mathcal S$ | The physical state held fixed |

$$\boxed{\;\text{active readout constraint}\;\neq\;\text{the fundamental definition of }X\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{active readout constraint}\;\neq\;\text{the fundamental definition of }X\;}
```

*Note on the derivative structure:* Since $X$ is not a scalar, the derivative presupposes a differentiable structure on the value space of $X$. Two levels that do not require this structure are distinguished — achromaticity **at the resolver level** and achromaticity **at the level of selected readings** — and the two are not unconditionally equivalent:

$$\underbrace{X(\mathcal S,E_1)=X(\mathcal S,E_2)\ \ \forall\,E_1,E_2}_{\text{resolver level}}\;\Longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\left.\frac{\partial\xi_k}{\partial E_{\rm probe}}\right|_{\mathcal S}=0\ \ \forall k}_{\text{reading level}}\qquad(\xi_k=\pi_k(X)\ \text{differentiable})$$

```latex
\underbrace{X(\mathcal S,E_1)=X(\mathcal S,E_2)\ \ \forall\,E_1,E_2}_{\text{resolver level}}\;\Longrightarrow\;\underbrace{\left.\frac{\partial\xi_k}{\partial E_{\rm probe}}\right|_{\mathcal S}=0\ \ \forall k}_{\text{reading level}}\qquad(\xi_k=\pi_k(X)\ \text{differentiable})
```

$$\text{The converse is invalid in general: } X(E)=(0,E),\ \ \pi_1(a,b)=a\ \Rightarrow\ \xi_1=0\ \text{constant, but } X(E)\ \text{varies}$$

```latex
\text{The converse is invalid in general: } X(E)=(0,E),\ \ \pi_1(a,b)=a\ \Rightarrow\ \xi_1=0\ \text{constant, but } X(E)\ \text{varies}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $E_1$, $E_2$ | Two different probe energies |
| $\xi_k$ | A differentiable reading of $X$ |
| $\pi_1(a,b)=a$ | The projection in the counterexample: it reads only the first component |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It also writes the achromaticity constraint in a form that does not require a derivative structure |
| **Why does it do it?** | Without defining what a derivative means for a non-scalar object, the constraint is not well defined |
| **How does it do it?** | It states that energy independence at the resolver level implies the energy independence of all differentiable readings; it shows with a counterexample that the converse is invalid in general. Equivalence in the reverse direction can be established when the family of readings separates resolutions (the projections are jointly injective) and under suitable connectedness / differentiability conditions |
| **What result is expected?** | A constraint consistent at the type level with bounds on energy-dependent delays (LHAASO / GRB 221009A) |
| **What result is obtained?** | The content of the constraint does not change; its **well-definedness** is secured. LHAASO-type observations test only selected readings; the observational bound is therefore a constraint at the reading level, and the constraint at the resolver level is its stronger (sufficient) form |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It requires that the ontological resolution of the same physical state does not shift with the energy of the measurement probe |
| **Why does it do it?** | A resolution depending on the probe's energy would produce effects that violate Lorentz invariance and contradict measurements |
| **How does it do it?** | At fixed state, it holds the derivative of $X$ and of the readout coefficient with respect to the probe energy at zero |
| **What result is expected?** | That bridge models do not produce forbidden effects such as an energy-dependent difference in the speed of light |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is preserved as an active Cₘ readout / Lorentz-compatibility constraint; it does not mean that the probe energy is irrelevant in every model; it is not the definition of $X$ |

### Equation set — time and the quantum threshold

The canon tied the "smallest meaningful step" of time to a quantum threshold. If this threshold is set up incorrectly, the definition of time itself becomes dimensionally inconsistent, and so does everything built on it.

$$\int \mathcal{G}\,d\tau \geq \frac{\hbar}{2} \qquad \text{(old — dimensional error, retired)}$$

```latex
\int \mathcal{G}\,d\tau \geq \frac{\hbar}{2} \qquad \text{(old — dimensional error, retired)}
```

$$\int \mathcal{G}_{\rm FS}\,d\lambda \geq \theta_{\min}, \qquad \Delta t\;\geq\;\Delta t_{\min}=\frac{\hbar\,\theta_{\min}}{\overline{\Delta E}},\qquad \overline{\Delta E}=\frac1{\Delta t}\int_0^{\Delta t}\Delta E(t)\,dt \qquad \text{(correction; for constant } \Delta E\text{, }\overline{\Delta E}=\Delta E)$$

```latex
\int \mathcal{G}_{\rm FS}\,d\lambda \geq \theta_{\min}, \qquad \Delta t\;\geq\;\Delta t_{\min}=\frac{\hbar\,\theta_{\min}}{\overline{\Delta E}},\qquad \overline{\Delta E}=\frac1{\Delta t}\int_0^{\Delta t}\Delta E(t)\,dt \qquad \text{(correction; for constant } \Delta E\text{, }\overline{\Delta E}=\Delta E)
```

$$dt = \frac{\hbar}{\Delta E_{C_m}}\,ds_{\rm FS} \quad \text{(C-1 bridge)}, \qquad dt_{\rm ET} = \eta_{\rm clock}\,d\tau_{\rm GR},\ \ \eta_{\rm clock} \in [\eta^-, \eta^+],\ \text{in the GR limit } [1,1]$$

```latex
dt = \frac{\hbar}{\Delta E_{C_m}}\,ds_{\rm FS} \quad \text{(C-1 bridge)}, \qquad dt_{\rm ET} = \eta_{\rm clock}\,d\tau_{\rm GR},\ \ \eta_{\rm clock} \in [\eta^-, \eta^+],\ \text{in the GR limit } [1,1]
```

$$\tau_0\tilde\Gamma\,\mathcal{G}_{C_m}=\frac{dt_{\rm ET}}{d\lambda}=\eta_{\rm clock}\,\frac{d\tau_{\rm GR}}{d\lambda},\qquad d\tau_{\rm GR}=\sqrt{-g_{00}}\;dt_{\rm koor}\ \ (\text{static observer, }v^i=0) \qquad \text{(candidate clock mapping)}$$

```latex
\tau_0\tilde\Gamma\,\mathcal{G}_{C_m}=\frac{dt_{\rm ET}}{d\lambda}=\eta_{\rm clock}\,\frac{d\tau_{\rm GR}}{d\lambda},\qquad d\tau_{\rm GR}=\sqrt{-g_{00}}\;dt_{\rm koor}\ \ (\text{static observer, }v^i=0) \qquad \text{(candidate clock mapping)}
```

$$\Delta t_{\min} \in \left[\frac{\hbar\theta^-}{\overline{\Delta E}^{\,+}},\ \frac{\hbar\theta^+}{\overline{\Delta E}^{\,-}}\right] \quad \text{(range form)}$$

```latex
\Delta t_{\min} \in \left[\frac{\hbar\theta^-}{\overline{\Delta E}^{\,+}},\ \frac{\hbar\theta^+}{\overline{\Delta E}^{\,-}}\right] \quad \text{(range form)}
```

**Retired notation:** the equality $\Gamma\cdot\mathcal G_{C_m}=1/\sqrt{-g_{00}}$ of earlier versions is **dimensionally inconsistent** in the normalized scaffold ($[\lambda]=1$, $[\Gamma]=T$), since it leaves the left-hand side with the dimension of time and the right-hand side dimensionless; it becomes dimensionally meaningful only if a scaffold parameter with the dimension of time ($[\tau]=T$) is chosen and the identifications $t_{\rm ET}\leftrightarrow t_{\rm koor}$ and $\tau\leftrightarrow\tau_{\rm GR}$ are made, and these identifications have not been proven. The clock mapping above is used in its place; which clock (coordinate time, proper time, Evertophy time) is being read is written explicitly.

**The stationary-state branch:** a state with $\Delta E=0$ does not evolve ($ds_{\rm FS}=0$); in the C-1 bridge, $\hbar\,ds_{\rm FS}/\Delta E$ becomes indeterminate ($0/0$), and this branch is defined separately. That the state distance of a subsystem is zero says that the measure of change of that subsystem is zero; **it does not mean that universal physical clocks have stopped**.

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal{G}$ | The rate of change of the phase state (a "speed" in state space) |
| $\mathcal{G}_{\rm FS}$ | The same rate measured with the Fubini–Study metric (the natural distance between quantum states) |
| $\tau$, $\lambda$ | Parameters; $\lambda$ is **dimensionless** |
| $\hbar$ | Reduced Planck constant — dimension [energy × time] |
| $\theta_{\min}$ | The smallest angle between two distinguishable states (dimensionless) |
| $\Delta E$ | The energy spread of the Cₘ system |
| $ds_{\rm FS}$ | The Fubini–Study arc-length element |
| $\eta_{\rm clock}$ | The ratio between Evertophy time and general-relativistic proper time (a range) |
| $d\tau_{\rm GR}$ | General-relativistic proper time |
| $g_{00}$ | The time–time component of the metric |
| $\overline{\Delta E}$ | The time average of the energy spread along the trajectory (the Anandan–Aharonov form for a time-dependent Hamiltonian) |
| $t_{\rm ET}$, $t_{\rm koor}$ | Evertophy (scaffold) time and coordinate time |
| $\tau_0$, $\tilde\Gamma$ | Time scale and dimensionless readout coefficient ($\Gamma=\tau_0\tilde\Gamma$) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It gives a dimensionally consistent quantum bound for the smallest meaningful step of time |
| **Why does it do it?** | In the old threshold, the left-hand side was dimensionless and the right-hand side had the dimension of action; the definition of time was dimensionally inconsistent |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the threshold as a dimensionless angle; from it, for a constant energy spread, it extracts a temporal **speed limit** (of Mandelstam–Tamm type); it builds a time bridge with the Fubini–Study metric |
| **What result is expected?** | That the time step is defined dimensionally consistently in the quantum Cₘ regime and reduces to $[1,1]$ in the general-relativistic limit |
| **What result is obtained?** | The old threshold is retired; the dimensionless threshold is a model / geometric bridge; the Fubini–Study time bridge (C-1) is a meaningful bridge only under the conditions of a pure state and unitary evolution, and **it is not the universal definition of all Cₘ clocks**: physical time is meaningful in a stationary quantum state too, whereas the bridge gives $0/0$ there; $\eta_{\rm clock}$ is a bridge / model (not core; GR limit $[1,1]$); the open-system generalization is open. $\Delta t_{\min}$ is not an attained duration but a **lower bound** (equality only for geodesic evolution). The old notation $\Gamma\mathcal G_{C_m}=1/\sqrt{-g_{00}}$ is retired because of a dimensional error; a dimensionally consistent clock mapping has replaced it |

**Historical dimensional check:** the historical clock factor $dt=\Gamma\,G_t\,d\tau$ is consistent only if $[\Gamma]=T$, $[G_t]=T^{-1}$, $[d\tau]=T$; the dimensionless form becomes $dt=\tau_0\,\tilde\Gamma\,G_t\,d\tau$, with $[\tilde\Gamma]=1$. The $G_t$ here is **not** the gravitational constant. This form carries no physical clock outside Cₘ; nor is a zero derivative defined in c₀ and c⁺. Status: archive / dimensional check; physical bridge open.

## 7.5 Two different kinds of timelessness

| Phase | Temporal role | Ontological meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **c₀** | Pre-temporal | Physical time is not yet established ($\bot_0$); potential / core regime |
| **Cₘ** | Physical time | The physical clock, the proper-time reading and measurable change are meaningful here |
| **c⁺** | Timeless record | The time established in Cₘ is dissolved ($\bot_+$); sealed records are present together without requiring temporal order |

$$\boxed{\;\text{c₀ timelessness (not yet established)} \;\neq\; \text{c⁺ timelessness (dissolved)}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{c₀ timelessness (not yet established)} \;\neq\; \text{c⁺ timelessness (dissolved)}\;}
```

## 7.6 The photon, proper time and phase membership

In special relativity, proper time is zero along a lightlike world line. This measured fact does not make the photon ontologically a member of c⁺.

$$\boxed{\;d\tau_{\rm öz}=0 \;\nRightarrow\; \text{phase}(\text{photon})=c^+\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;d\tau_{\rm öz}=0 \;\nRightarrow\; \text{phase}(\text{photon})=c^+\;}
```

The photon is on the $v=c$ boundary, in a **liminal** state (3.3). Being liminal means lying on the boundary of the membership band of Cₘ. This boundary is at the same time the boundary of the c⁺ regime: Cₘ and c⁺ share this boundary. Lying on the **shared boundary** of two regimes is not the same as being a member of either: the photon is a member neither of the massive matter of Cₘ nor of c⁺. The same reading also holds for gravitational waves (author's ruling, 3.3).

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It settles the phase status of the photon |
| **Why does it do it?** | The inference "no time passes for the photon, therefore the photon is in c⁺" is common but wrong |
| **How does it do it?** | It accepts zero proper time as a Cₘ measurement; it places the photon on the shared liminal boundary of Cₘ and c⁺ |
| **What result is expected?** | That light and gravitational waves find a consistent place in the framework |
| **What result is obtained?** | The shared-boundary reading is an author's ruling; lying on the boundary is not membership |

## 7.7 The two-clock problem — an open tension

The effective geometry of Cₘ already produces a proper time; the emergent-time scaffold offers a second language of reading. The "two-clock" problem must therefore be resolved explicitly.

| Route | Content | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | The emergent reading is identical with proper time | The new time structure may add no physical content |
| **B** | The two clocks are independent | The precise agreement between them must be explained |
| **C** | There is a dynamic or unique mapping | An additional bridge law is required |

Until this problem is closed, the emergent-time equation cannot be presented as a derived alternative to general-relativistic proper time.

### 7.7a The registry — a safe core reading

**The registry is not an independent entity.** It is a readout language used for the record trace of differentiated events and provenance information within Cₘ. The statement "there is a record" does not mean "there is a new ontological entity".

The following forms were used in the historical archive:

$$\Delta\sigma_m\neq0\;\Rightarrow\;\text{Record}\;\Rightarrow\;\text{Time},\qquad \frac{dS_{\rm Reg}}{d\tau}\ge0$$

```latex
\Delta\sigma_m\neq0\;\Rightarrow\;\text{Record}\;\Rightarrow\;\text{Time},\qquad \frac{dS_{\rm Reg}}{d\tau}\ge0
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Delta\sigma_m$ | The change in the Cₘ share (historical) |
| $S_{\rm Reg}$ | The historical "registry entropy" |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It tried to derive time from the production of records, and the registry entropy from a monotonic magnitude |
| **Why does it do it?** | It was an early attempt to tie the arrow of time to the accumulation of records |
| **How does it do it?** | By writing a chain that ties the change in phase share to a record, and the record to time, together with a law of monotonicity |
| **What result is expected?** | A fundamental derivation of time |
| **What result is obtained?** | In the current core, these forms are **not elevated** to a fundamental derivation of time or to a universal law of entropy; the time scaffold of 7.2 and the seal / archive ontology of Section 14 take precedence. The reading "c⁺ = the global / instantaneous registry of all phases" is not current canon either: the Cₘ time language of "instantaneous" is not carried into c⁺ |

## 7.8 Time and entropy

Time and entropy **are not identical**. The Cₘ arrow of time and the behaviour of record / entropy may have common structural roots; but:

$$\boxed{\;\text{physical time} \neq \text{thermodynamic entropy} \neq \text{archive order}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{physical time} \neq \text{thermodynamic entropy} \neq \text{archive order}\;}
```

The increase of one is not the definition of the other.
## 7.9 Phase memory — a timeless statement

"Phase memory" does not mean a history variable flowing inside c₀ or c⁺. The safe interpretation is this: the history of thresholds and transitions leaves **a structural residue or provenance constraint** in the subsequent Cₘ reading.

For the archive, core monotonicity is expressed not with a time variable but with a provenance order:

$$\boxed{\;A \preceq A'\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;A \preceq A'\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $A$, $A'$ | Two states of the archive |
| $\preceq$ | Append-only / provenance order; it is not physical time in c⁺ |

The expression $R_\phi(t+\Delta t)\ge R_\phi(t)$ seen in the archive can be used only as **an effective model with Cₘ time**; it is not a core axiom.

$$\Delta X_{\rm res}(x)=\int \Gamma(x,t')\,dt',\qquad X_{\rm eff}(x)=X_0(x)+\Delta X_{\rm res}(x),\qquad \Xi_{\rm iz}=\int\frac{\partial\Gamma}{\partial X}\,dX$$

```latex
\Delta X_{\rm res}(x)=\int \Gamma(x,t')\,dt',\qquad X_{\rm eff}(x)=X_0(x)+\Delta X_{\rm res}(x),\qquad \Xi_{\rm iz}=\int\frac{\partial\Gamma}{\partial X}\,dX
```

### Equation set — phase memory

$$R_\varphi(r_{n+1}) \geq R_\varphi(r_n) \qquad \text{(with respect to the provenance index — not with respect to physical time)}$$

```latex
R_\varphi(r_{n+1}) \geq R_\varphi(r_n) \qquad \text{(with respect to the provenance index — not with respect to physical time)}
```

| **Why does it do it?** | The old canon wrote this principle with respect to physical time; but there is no time in c₀ and c⁺ |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes monotonicity with respect to the provenance order of records instead of time; in effective-model versions it restricts the time integral to Cₘ only |
