The Phase Scar equations are not core laws; they do not scalarize $X$ and do not carry Cₘ time into the atemporal phases |

## 7.10 The language of speed is legitimate only in Cₘ

$$\boxed{\;\text{No internal RATE / SPEED language is used for } c_0 \text{ and } c^+\text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{No internal RATE / SPEED language is used for } c_0 \text{ and } c^+\text{.}\;}
```

For the same reason, expressions such as $dX/dt$, "the rate of phase flow" or "the expansion speed of c⁺" do not belong to the fundamental ontological layer; they can be defined only at the level of Cₘ readout or of models.

## 7.11 Comparison map with schools of emergent time

Four major positions on the status of time, and the position of Evertophy, are on record. This comparison is a positioning, not a criticism.

| School | Status of time | Source | Relation to Evertophy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Barbour** | Ordering of configurations in a timeless ontology; "time capsules" | No source | Common: time is not fundamental. Difference: Evertophy tries to **reproduce** time physically; Barbour dissolves it philosophically |
| **Rovelli** | Thermal time: modular flow determined by the state; time is derived | State → thermal flow | Common: derivedness. In Evertophy the source is regulation / sealing / phase transition |
| **Smolin** | Time is fundamental and real | Time itself | Partial closeness: a candidate bridge between a timeless ontology and realism about physical time |
| **Wheeler–DeWitt** | $H\Psi=0$ — a timeless wave equation | It stands as a problem | Evertophy reads timelessness through the phase distinction: not established in c₀, dissolved in c⁺ |

### 7.11a The Page–Wootters formalism

This is the classical structure "timeless universe + clock subsystem → time from a conditional wave function". In the Evertophy reading, the role of the clock subsystem belongs to the physical processes in Cₘ; the candidate mechanism is the "regulator flow". No formal identity has been established.

### 7.11b The holographic approach to time

This is the relation between boundary time and bulk time. Its counterpart in Evertophy is the readout relation between c⁺ and Cₘ; but holographic correspondence (of the AdS/CFT type) is not imported into the core (19.2).

### 7.11c Thermodynamic / entropic time

This is the view that the arrow of time arises from the increase of entropy. In Evertophy the distinction is sharp: time, entropy and archive order are three separate concepts (7.8, 16.3); none of them is the definition of another.

### 7.11d Relational quantum mechanics (RQM)

Measurement is interaction; the observer has no priority. In parallel, in Evertophy the observer is not a separate ontological class but the role of systems that produce records within Cₘ (1.4).

### 7.11e Wheeler–DeWitt and the literature on timelessness

The question "how timelessness is overcome" is open in the literature. Evertophy's proposal is phase-dependent production; in terms of mathematical depth the framework is still **fragmentary**: not a closed formal system, but at the level of "pattern + phase model".

### 7.11f A deeper comparison with Barbour

Four differences are on record: **(A)** timeless ontology ↔ time produced by the phase; **(B)** epistemic ↔ physical ("emergent does not mean unreal": clocks, drift and formal correlations are physical data); **(C)** the phase architecture (c₀, Cₘ, c⁺ do not exist in Barbour); **(D)** a residual trace ($\delta X$, $\Phi(x)$, $\rho(\tau)$) — the only claim by which Evertophy goes beyond Barbour is a measurable residue, and this claim has not been closed.

$$\boxed{\;\text{The plainest position: there is no fundamental time; time is produced in } C_m \text{ through regulation and sealing, and may leave a measurable trace.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The plainest position: there is no fundamental time; time is produced in } C_m \text{ through regulation and sealing, and may leave a measurable trace.}\;}
```

The caveat is locked: this sentence cannot be accepted as having been realized; its verification is an open debt.

### Equation set — $c$: the Cₘ light-cone boundary

$$\text{Core: } g(k,k) = 0,\quad c_{C_m} = [c,c] \qquad \text{(K12: } c = \sup d\ell/d\tau \text{ — retired; } \gamma v \to \infty\text{)}$$

```latex
\text{Core: } g(k,k) = 0,\quad c_{C_m} = [c,c] \qquad \text{(K12: } c = \sup d\ell/d\tau \text{ — retired; } \gamma v \to \infty\text{)}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $k^\mu$ | A lightlike (null) vector |
| $g(k,k)=0$ | The null-cone condition |
| $c_{C_m}=[c,c]$ | The speed of light in Cₘ: a definitional point interval |
| $d\ell/d\tau$ | Path speed with respect to the particle's proper time |
| $\gamma$ | Lorentz factor |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It determines the place of the speed of light in Evertophy |
| **Why does it do it?** | Defining $c$ as a phase-independent universal constant would import an internal speed into c₀ and c⁺ |
| **How does it do it?** | It defines $c$ as the null-cone boundary in Cₘ; it writes it in Cₘ as a zero-width range |
| **What result is expected?** | That there is no internal $c$ in c₀ and c⁺; that in the core the sensitivity of $c$ to any reading is zero |
| **What result is obtained?** | The null-cone definition is the core preference and is in the canonical record; the proper-time definition (K12) is retired, because $\gamma v$ grows without bound |



# SECTION 8 — THE ONTOLOGY OF ATTRACTION

## 8.1 Unfolding Postulate 0

$$\boxed{\;\text{The Force of Attraction is the fundamental ontological postulate; gravitation is one of its projections in Cₘ.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The Force of Attraction is the fundamental ontological postulate; gravitation is one of its projections in Cₘ.}\;}
```

Because the distinction of Section 1.2 is critical, it is repeated here:

$$\text{Force of Attraction}_{\rm ontoloji} \;\neq\; \text{Gravitation}_{C_m} \;\neq\; X \;\neq\; g_{\mu\nu} \;\neq\; G_{\rm eff}$$

```latex
\text{Force of Attraction}_{\rm ontoloji} \;\neq\; \text{Gravitation}_{C_m} \;\neq\; X \;\neq\; g_{\mu\nu} \;\neq\; G_{\rm eff}
```

### 8.1a The positive regulation reading — narrowed current form

The positive content of Postulate 0 requires that the Force of Attraction not be left described only by "what it is not": in Evertophy, the Force of Attraction is read as **the fundamental ontological constrainer and regulator of phase organization**.

But the identity in the historical record, "$X$ = a gravitation-sourced index of permission / order", **is not current canon**. The current $X$ is the universal, phase-sensitive resolver defined in Section 4; the Force of Attraction may be one of the determinants of $X$, but it does not by itself exhaust $X$.

$$\boxed{\;\text{The Force of Attraction is the fundamental regulator}\;\not\Rightarrow\;X\equiv \text{attraction index}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The Force of Attraction is the fundamental regulator}\;\not\Rightarrow\;X\equiv \text{attraction index}\;}
```

## 8.2 The Force of Attraction ≠ observed gravitation

General relativity **is not wrong**: it is the effective language of gravitation, which is the metric projection of the Force of Attraction in Cₘ. The ontological postulate and its projection in Cₘ are different layers; all the measured results of general relativity are accepted unconditionally.

## 8.3 Effective coupling — model level

A bridge form that can be written in scalar–tensor language is:

$$G_{\rm eff} = \frac{G_N}{F}, \qquad\qquad \frac{\dot G}{G} = -\frac{\dot F}{F}$$

```latex
G_{\rm eff} = \frac{G_N}{F}, \qquad\qquad \frac{\dot G}{G} = -\frac{\dot F}{F}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $G_{\rm eff}$ | The effective gravitational coupling measured in Cₘ |
| $G_N$ | Newton's gravitational constant |
| $F$ | The coupling function of the scalar–tensor theory (its form is unknown) |
| $\dot G/G$ | The relative rate of change of the gravitational constant (tightly bounded by measurements) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It ties the effective gravitational coupling to a model function |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make contact with observational bounds on a possible variation of $G$ |
| **How does it do it?** | In the standard form of scalar–tensor theories, it scales $G_{\rm eff}$ with the inverse of $F$ |
| **What result is expected?** | A prediction for $\dot G/G$, if $F$ is derived from $X$ |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is not core ontology but a model realization.** The form of $F$ is unknown and its relation to $X$ has not been established; the observational bounds are constraints placed **on** Evertophy, not predictions that follow from it |

## 8.4 Local strength of attraction

The local strength of attraction is a quantity **related** to the phase position; it is **not equivalent** to it. The same value may correspond to different phase positions in different structural arrangements.

### Gradient formulas

Forms of a law of motion of the following kind appear in the archive:

$$\mathbf{a} \sim -c^2\nabla\ln X \qquad (\text{historical form})$$

```latex
\mathbf{a} \sim -c^2\nabla\ln X \qquad (\text{historical form})
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It tried to derive acceleration from the logarithmic gradient of $X$ |
| **Why does it do it?** | It was an early attempt to tie gravitational acceleration directly to the resolver |
| **How does it do it?** | By using $X$ as if it were a scalar field |
| **What result is expected?** | That the Newtonian limit follows from $X$ |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is not a core law.** Since $X$ is not necessarily a scalar, such a gradient form is not required by the ontology; if a reading $\xi=\pi(X)$ is defined and derived, it is evaluated in the model or bridge layer |

## 8.5 The other forces

The electromagnetic, strong and weak forces — together with gravitation — are projections of the Force of Attraction in Cₘ (1.2). The coupling constants are in principle determined by the scale-, mass- and phase-dependent form of this projection; but the form of this dependence is not known.

### The three-layer readout architecture — model candidate

| Layer | What it describes | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Maxwell / field language** | The electromagnetic field in Cₘ and measurable field readings | Established effective physics |
| **CMT / channel language** | Modes, scattering, impedance and directional channel behaviour | Experimental / model bridge language |
| **Evertophy / phase-constraint language** | The upper constraint structure that resolves which phase, regime and reading are admissible | Ontological / programme layer |

$$\boxed{\;\text{Maxwell}\neq\text{CMT}\neq\text{Evertophy};\quad \text{the layers are not used in place of one another.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Maxwell}\neq\text{CMT}\neq\text{Evertophy};\quad \text{the layers are not used in place of one another.}\;}
```

The historical development records also contain the following phenomenological candidate assignments for the forces:

| Cₘ interaction | Historical / candidate Evertophy reading |
|---|---|
| Strong | A binding structure resembling "crystal / phase integrity" (old name; To Be Addressed Later) |
| Electromagnetic | A persistent Cₘ signature and readout programme related to the limit $c$ (charge); the projection form of repulsion and of charge To Be Addressed Later |
| Weak | Transformation from an unstable to a stable structure |

This table **is not a derivation**; it does not count as having explained the gauge structure or the couplings of the Standard Model. In particular, the row concerning $c$ does not claim a superluminal process that physically "breaks" the kinematic and consistency limit of Cₘ.

## 8.6 Attraction and the outer phases

$$\boxed{\;\text{The Force of Attraction is phase-independent: } c_0 \text{ and } c^+ \text{ are also subject to its constraint coherence.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The Force of Attraction is phase-independent: } c_0 \text{ and } c^+ \text{ are also subject to its constraint coherence.}\;}
```

The metric, matter support and the language of acceleration are **readout tools** belonging to Cₘ; the Force of Attraction does not depend on these tools. The attractive effect of c⁺, observed as dark matter and dark energy (Section 11), is read in Cₘ in the language of the metric; but the source of the effect is c⁺'s own phase regime, not Cₘ matter. The mathematical language of "how" attraction works in the outer phases is deliberately open, and it is the natural subject of the cycle-mechanism layer of V24.

This reading does not bring a metric or Cₘ matter support back into c⁺; the distinction **identity ≠ mechanism** is preserved, and no quantitative derivation is claimed here.

## 8.7 Gravitational impedance — a Cₘ general-relativity model reading

In the language of weak-field general relativity, to avoid confusion with the $\tau$ of the ontology, let us denote local proper time by $\tau_{\rm GR}$ and coordinate time by $t_{\rm koor}$. The following relations are for **an observer at rest in the coordinates** ($v^i=0$); the last approximation holds in the weak field and in the notation $g_{00}=-(1+2\Phi_N/c^2)+\dots$:

$$d\tau_{\rm GR}\simeq\left(1+\frac{\Phi_N}{c^2}\right)dt_{\rm koor}$$

```latex
d\tau_{\rm GR}\simeq\left(1+\frac{\Phi_N}{c^2}\right)dt_{\rm koor}
```

$$\boxed{\;Z_g(x)\equiv\frac{dt_{\rm koor}}{d\tau_{\rm GR}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g_{00}}}\simeq1-\frac{\Phi_N}{c^2}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;Z_g(x)\equiv\frac{dt_{\rm koor}}{d\tau_{\rm GR}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{-g_{00}}}\simeq1-\frac{\Phi_N}{c^2}\;}
```

$$\mathbf a\simeq-c^2\nabla\ln\sqrt{-g_{00}}=+c^2\nabla\ln Z_g\simeq-\nabla\Phi_N$$

```latex
\mathbf a\simeq-c^2\nabla\ln\sqrt{-g_{00}}=+c^2\nabla\ln Z_g\simeq-\nabla\Phi_N
```

For a general timelike world line ($x^0=ct$, $v^i=dx^i/dt$):

$$Z_g = \frac{dt}{d\tau_{\rm GR}} = \Big[-\big(g_{00} + 2g_{0i}\,v^i/c + g_{ij}\,v^iv^j/c^2\big)\Big]^{-1/2}$$

```latex
Z_g = \frac{dt}{d\tau_{\rm GR}} = \Big[-\big(g_{00} + 2g_{0i}\,v^i/c + g_{ij}\,v^iv^j/c^2\big)\Big]^{-1/2}
```

The candidate dictionary that can be investigated with the channel model is:

$$\Phi_g\;\longleftrightarrow\;\Phi_{\rm ch}^{\rm gen}=\lambda\ln\left|\frac{Z_{\rm eff}}{Z_{\rm ref}}\right|$$

```latex
\Phi_g\;\longleftrightarrow\;\Phi_{\rm ch}^{\rm gen}=\lambda\ln\left|\frac{Z_{\rm eff}}{Z_{\rm ref}}\right|
```

The complex channel potential $\lambda\ln(Z_{\rm eff}/Z_{\rm ref})$ is preserved separately as a model object carrying independent phase information (17.1a) |

The ratio $dt_{\rm koor}/d\tau_{\rm GR}$ is not identical with the ontological scaffold of 7.2; nor is $\Phi_{\rm ch}$ regarded as identical with $\Phi_\chi$ of Section 17 unless a calibration is shown. The clock mapping of 7.4 ($\eta_{\rm clock}$; for a static observer $d\tau_{\rm GR}=\sqrt{-g_{00}}\,dt_{\rm koor}$) does not remove this distinction: the mapping is in the readout layer, the identity in the ontological layer |

### The processing-delay reading

In the operational reading of the archive, $n$ is **an index of processing order**, not a physical duration; it attributes no physical time to c₀. The link between the index and the duration measured in Cₘ requires a scale and a mapping:

$$dt_{\rm gösterge} = \tau_*\,f\,dn$$

| **What result is obtained?** | Gravitational time dilation is read as a **processing delay**, and the Shapiro delay as the delay of passage through a high-impedance region. This **renames** the numerical results of general relativity and does not count as deriving them; the choice of $f$ is not derived |

## 8.8 The derivation chain from CMT to attraction (model candidate)

The candidate chain from the language of coupled-mode theory (CMT) to a reading of attraction is:

$$(\Gamma_{\rm CMT}, \kappa, \nu) \;\Rightarrow\; S(\omega; \mathcal C) \;\Rightarrow\; \chi_S \;\Rightarrow\; \Xi = \ln\chi_S \;\Rightarrow\; \mathbf g_\chi = -\Lambda(\xi)\nabla\ln\chi_S-(\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda(\xi)$$

```latex
(\Gamma_{\rm CMT}, \kappa, \nu) \;\Rightarrow\; S(\omega; \mathcal C) \;\Rightarrow\; \chi_S \;\Rightarrow\; \Xi = \ln\chi_S \;\Rightarrow\; \mathbf g_\chi = -\Lambda(\xi)\nabla\ln\chi_S-(\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda(\xi)
```

### Conditions required for breaking reciprocity (model record)

Single-mode CMT does not yield a breaking of reciprocity. At least one of five classes of mechanism is required:

| Class | Example |
|---|---|
| Magneto-optic bias | Ferrite, magneto-optic medium |
| Time-dependent modulation | Parametric driving |
| Multi-mode + circulation | Ring resonator |
| Background path + interference | Interferometer arrangement |
| Nonlinear active medium | — |

The correct statement is not "single mode + parameter $\nu$" but "a concrete physical class that breaks reciprocity + port configuration + reference plane". Passivity and time invariance alone do not guarantee reciprocity: ferrite systems under static magnetic bias are passive but non-reciprocal examples. Nor are multiple modes, a ring resonator or an interference path by themselves proof of a breaking of reciprocity (17.1).

### The gravitational impedance chain (archive record)

Step by step: (1) an analogy between electromagnetism and general relativity; (2) $d\tau\approx(1+\Phi/c^2)dt$; (3) $Z_g=dt/d\tau=1/\sqrt{-g_{00}}\approx1-\Phi/c^2$; (4) $\mathbf a\approx+c^2\nabla\ln Z_g=-c^2\nabla\ln\sqrt{-g_{00}}\to-\nabla\Phi$ (sign correction: $\ln Z_g\approx-\Phi/c^2$); (5) the bridge candidate $\Phi_g\leftrightarrow\Phi_{\rm ch}^{\rm gen}=\lambda\ln\lvert Z_{\rm eff}/Z_{\rm ref}\rvert$ (real amplitude form; the complex form $\lambda\ln(Z_{\rm eff}/Z_{\rm ref})$ is a separate model object carrying phase information, 17.1a).

$$\boxed{\;Z_g \text{ is a general-relativistic readout magnitude defined only in Cₘ; it is not carried into } c_0 \text{ and } c^+ \text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;Z_g \text{ is a general-relativistic readout magnitude defined only in Cₘ; it is not carried into } c_0 \text{ and } c^+ \text{.}\;}
```

## 8.9 The Shapiro delay — an operational-delay reading

The Shapiro delay of general relativity is a measured phenomenon and is accepted as it is. The Evertophy reading is operational:

$$X_{\rm clock}(r) \equiv \pi_{\rm clock}[X](r), \qquad t_{\rm göz} = \int \frac{d\tau}{X_{\rm clock}(r)} \qquad (X_{\rm clock} > 0)$$

```latex
X_{\rm clock}(r) \equiv \pi_{\rm clock}[X](r), \qquad t_{\rm göz} = \int \frac{d\tau}{X_{\rm clock}(r)} \qquad (X_{\rm clock} > 0)
```

A random residual candidate that could be added to the bridge is also recorded in the archive:

$$\delta t = \delta t_{\rm GR} + \eta,\qquad \mathbb E[\eta] = 0,\qquad \mathrm{Var}(\eta) \propto \int F(\rho,Z)\,dx$$

```latex
\delta t = \delta t_{\rm GR} + \eta,\qquad \mathbb E[\eta] = 0,\qquad \mathrm{Var}(\eta) \propto \int F(\rho,Z)\,dx
```

| $\pi_{\rm clock}$ | The clock readout projection of $X$ |
| $X_{\rm clock}(r)$ | Position-dependent clock reading |
| $t_{\rm göz}$ | The observed travel time |
| $\delta t_{\rm GR}$ | The delay predicted by general relativity |
| $\eta$ | A random residual with zero mean |

The critical safety constraint is $\partial X/\partial E_{\rm probe}=0$ (active achromaticity, 7.4): agreement with the bounds on energy-dependent delays from observations such as LHAASO / GRB 221009A requires that the bridge not produce Lorentz violation |

## 8.10 The reading of cosmic expansion and collapse

At the cosmic scale, the expansion branch is related to c⁺ and the collapse branch to c₀ (3.1). The quantitative expansion–collapse dynamics and the balance equation of the three phases have the status **Awaiting Derivation**; they have not been locked as a one-line principle of inertia. This heading is preserved historically; its counterpart in the current core is the double-limit seed of Section 2.



# SECTION 9 — THE ONTOLOGY OF THE METRIC

## 9.1 The only domain of the metric

$$\boxed{\;g_{\mu\nu} \text{ is an admissible object ONLY in Cₘ.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;g_{\mu\nu} \text{ is an admissible object ONLY in Cₘ.}\;}
```

The metric is an **output** of Cₘ, not its input. The language for measuring length, duration and angle is born in the phase in which matter is differentiated and measurement is established; using this language to define the phases themselves would put the readout tool in place of the thing being read.

## 9.2 The phase barrier and geometric realization within Cₘ

The ontological core criterion is:

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{MetricAdmissible}(\phi)\Longleftrightarrow \phi=C_m\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{MetricAdmissible}(\phi)\Longleftrightarrow \phi=C_m\;}
```

The tetrad $E^A{}_\mu$ (four frame vectors) is a tool of representation that can be used **only after the Cₘ geometry has been established**. Within Cₘ, suitable tetrad rank conditions for a non-degenerate Lorentzian metric can be studied; but the statement

$$\boxed{\;\operatorname{rank}(E)=4\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\operatorname{rank}(E)=4\;}
```

**is not** the definition of the phase barrier of the outer-phase ontology.

### The tetrad is not inserted into the outer phases

$$\boxed{\;\text{The tetrad is NOT A DEFINED OBJECT in } c_0 \text{ and } c^+ \text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The tetrad is NOT A DEFINED OBJECT in } c_0 \text{ and } c^+ \text{.}\;}
```

For this reason one does not say "the rank drops in the outer phase"; the correct statement is that **the tetrad and metric types are not admissible in the outer phase**. A drop in rank is still speaking in the language of Cₘ; inadmissibility says that this language has not been established there at all.

### Equation set — the metric phase barrier

$$g = E^{\mathsf T}\eta E,\quad \eta = \mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1),\quad E \in \mathbb{R}^{4\times4}:\qquad \det g = -(\det E)^2,\qquad \operatorname{rank} E = 4 \iff g \text{ is a non-degenerate Lorentzian metric}$$

```latex
g = E^{\mathsf T}\eta E,\quad \eta = \mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1),\quad E \in \mathbb{R}^{4\times4}:\qquad \det g = -(\det E)^2,\qquad \operatorname{rank} E = 4 \iff g \text{ is a non-degenerate Lorentzian metric}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $g$, $g_{\mu\nu}$ | The metric tensor |
| $E$, $E^A{}_\mu$ | The tetrad matrix (frame components) |
| $\eta$ | The Minkowski metric, signature $(-,+,+,+)$ |
| $\det$ | Determinant |
| $\operatorname{rank}$ | The number of independent rows / columns of a matrix |
| $\theta^a$ | Frame 1-forms |
| $\mathrm{MetricAdmissible}(\phi)$ | The predicate stating whether the metric is admissible in phase $\phi$ |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It constructs the metric through the tetrad and ties the condition of non-degeneracy to the full rank of the tetrad |
| **Why does it do it?** | To tie the condition under which the metric can exist to an explicit mathematical criterion |
| **How does it do it?** | Through the identity $\det g = -(\det E)^2$, it makes the non-degeneracy of $g$ equivalent to $E$ having full rank |
| **What result is expected?** | That in phase transitions the tetrad can be rebuilt from $X$ and phase information (9.4) |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is correct as a **conditional identity** within Cₘ. The rank condition is the condition for the realization of the metric in Cₘ; it is not the definition of the outer phases. Reconstruction of the metric from the ontology is **To Be Addressed Later** |

*Notation note:* rank 4 is the condition for this construction to give a **non-degenerate** Lorentzian metric; when the rank is lower, the matrix expression can still be written but is degenerate.

## 9.2a Three-dimensional Euclidean flat space and emergent time

**Author's ruling:** The base space of Cₘ is the flat, three-dimensional Euclidean space made up of the familiar $x$–$y$–$z$ coordinates; there is no fourth or additional dimension. Physical time **emerges** in this phase; it is not a fundamental dimension. In c₀, Cₘ-type space and time are not yet established; in c⁺ they are dissolved.

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{Space}_{\rm temel}(C_m) = E^3,\qquad {}^{(3)}R_{ijkl}=0\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{Space}_{\rm temel}(C_m) = E^3,\qquad {}^{(3)}R_{ijkl}=0\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathrm{Space}_{\rm temel}(C_m)$ | The base (ontological) space of Cₘ |
| $E^3$ | Three-dimensional Euclidean space |
| ${}^{(3)}R_{ijkl}$ | The Riemann curvature tensor of three-dimensional space; its vanishing means flatness |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It fixes the base space of Cₘ as flat, three-dimensional Euclidean space |
| **Why does it do it?** | To separate measured curvature effects from the structure of the base space: curvature is a reading, not a bending of the base space |
| **How does it do it?** | It assigns zero three-dimensional curvature to the base space; it places the 3+1 Lorentzian metric language in the measurement and model layer as the effective readout geometry of Cₘ |
| **What result is expected?** | That all the observational results of general relativity are recovered from this effective geometry |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is an author's ruling. Measured curvature effects such as lensing, the Shapiro delay and gravitational waves are accepted unconditionally and are understood as **readings** of the metric projection of the Force of Attraction. Recovering the observational results from the effective geometry is **an open bridge problem**. Not every curved space-time admits a flat spatial slicing (for example, there are spherically symmetric families for which Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates cannot be constructed); therefore it must be defined explicitly which families of general-relativistic solutions the map $g^{\rm eff}_{\mu\nu}[E^3,X,\Delta]$ covers |

This has an important consequence: the **four-dimensional curved space-time** of general relativity is not a fundamental structure, but the emergent effective readout geometry of Cₘ. As context — not counted as evidence —: cosmological data, under certain models, bound the large-scale spatial curvature to near zero; this is not proof of exact zero. It is also known in physics that general relativity can be written as a field theory on a flat background.

## 9.3 Two phases, two different reasons

| Phase | Why there is no metric |
|---|---|
| **c₀** | It is pre-temporal; the metric is **not yet established** |
| **c⁺** | Cₘ matter support is **dissolved**; $g_{\mu\nu}^{(C_m)} \to \bot_+$ |

$$\boxed{\;\text{The two phases are NOT metric-free FOR THE SAME REASON.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The two phases are NOT metric-free FOR THE SAME REASON.}\;}
```

In c₀ the metric **has not yet been born**; in c⁺ the support that carries it **has dissolved**. These two "absences" are not written with the same symbol: the undefinedness of c₀ is $\bot_0$, that of c⁺ is $\bot_+$, and neither is zero ($\bot_0\ne\bot_+\ne0$).

## 9.4 Reconstruction of the metric — two separate routes

In a transition from a phase into Cₘ, the old metric **is not carried over**; the new Cₘ state rebuilds its own metric.

$$\mathcal{E}_m^{(0)} : (\Delta_{0m},\, X,\, \Theta_m) \to E^A{}_\mu, \qquad\qquad \mathcal{E}_m^{(+)} : (\Delta_{+m},\, X,\, \Theta_m) \to E^A{}_\mu$$

```latex
\mathcal{E}_m^{(0)} : (\Delta_{0m},\, X,\, \Theta_m) \to E^A{}_\mu, \qquad\qquad \mathcal{E}_m^{(+)} : (\Delta_{+m},\, X,\, \Theta_m) \to E^A{}_\mu
```

In both cases:

$$g_{\mu\nu} = \eta_{AB}\,\mathcal{E}^A{}_\mu\,\mathcal{E}^B{}_\nu, \qquad\qquad \text{success condition: } \operatorname{rank}\mathcal{E}_m = 4$$

```latex
g_{\mu\nu} = \eta_{AB}\,\mathcal{E}^A{}_\mu\,\mathcal{E}^B{}_\nu, \qquad\qquad \text{success condition: } \operatorname{rank}\mathcal{E}_m = 4
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal{E}_m^{(0)}$ | The map that builds the tetrad in the c₀ → Cₘ transition |
| $\mathcal{E}_m^{(+)}$ | The map that builds the tetrad in the c⁺ → Cₘ transition |
| $\Delta_{0m}$, $\Delta_{+m}$ | The phase difference / transition information of the corresponding transition edges (Section 5) |
| $X$ | The dynamic meta-resolver |
| $\Theta_m$ | The admission conditions of Cₘ (gate / permission parameters) |
| $\eta_{AB}$ | The Minkowski metric in frame space |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes, as two separate maps, how the metric is rebuilt on arrival in Cₘ from two different entries (from c₀ and from c⁺) |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make mathematically explicit that the old metric cannot be carried across the transition and that the two entries are not the same event |
| **How does it do it?** | Each map takes its own edge information, $X$ and the Cₘ admission conditions and produces a tetrad; the metric is built from this tetrad |
| **What result is expected?** | That arrival from c₀ **builds the geometry anew**, and arrival from c⁺ **restores matter support**; explicit forms of the two maps |
| **What result is obtained?** | Only the **structure** has been written; the two maps need not be the same, and their explicit forms are not known (9.5) |

## 9.5 The remaining gap

$$\mathcal{E}_m^{(0)}[\cdot] = ?, \qquad \mathcal{E}_m^{(+)}[\cdot] = ?$$

```latex
\mathcal{E}_m^{(0)}[\cdot] = ?, \qquad \mathcal{E}_m^{(+)}[\cdot] = ?
```

How the tetrad is to be derived from $X$ and from the phase transition **is open**. This gap is left honestly; the framework does not use the metric without saying where the metric comes from.

## 9.6 No action is written for the outer phases

$$\boxed{\;\text{NO four-dimensional action / Lagrangian density IS WRITTEN for } c_0 \text{ and } c^+\text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{NO four-dimensional action / Lagrangian density IS WRITTEN for } c_0 \text{ and } c^+\text{.}\;}
```

The standard Cₘ-type action integral uses the measure $\sqrt{-g}\,d^4x$; this measure is not defined in the outer phases. Metric-free or topological forms of action are mathematically possible; in the outer phases Evertophy excludes only the Cₘ-type four-dimensional metric action. The actions Evertophy uses **belong to Cₘ**.

# SECTION 10 — THE ONTOLOGY OF MATTER

## 10.1 Matter in the three phases

| Phase | The state of matter |
|---|---|
| **c₀** | Differentiation has disappeared; matter is in the form of **a single integrated structure**, contiguous to a degree that converges to the metric-gapless state at the Planck scale |
| **Cₘ** | **Differentiated matter**: particles, atoms, structures |
| **c⁺** | Cₘ matter support is **dissolved**; the Cₘ kind of matter is not admissible. Every region that Cₘ reads as "empty space" — even the space between the particles that make up the atom — is the Cₘ reading of c⁺ |

## 10.2 Two transitions, two different events

$$\boxed{\;C_m \to c_0: \text{ differentiated matter} \to \text{METRIC-GAPLESS SINGLE STRUCTURE}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;C_m \to c_0: \text{ differentiated matter} \to \text{METRIC-GAPLESS SINGLE STRUCTURE}\;}
```

$$\boxed{\;C_m \to c^+: \text{ Cₘ matter support} \to \text{DISSOLUTION / INADMISSIBILITY}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;C_m \to c^+: \text{ Cₘ matter support} \to \text{DISSOLUTION / INADMISSIBILITY}\;}
```

These are **not the same event**. In the first, matter merges and distinction is lost: the metric gap between particles closes and matter becomes a single form. In the second, the basis of matter in Cₘ disappears: the structure dissolves and its energy is sealed in c⁺. One is a gathering, the other a dissolution.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines the transition of matter from Cₘ to the two outer phases as two separate events |
| **Why does it do it?** | The expression "matter disappears" would confuse two opposite processes — gathering into a single structure and dissolution — under the same word |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the c₀ direction as the formation of a contiguous single structure, and the c⁺ direction as matter support becoming inadmissible, separately |
| **What result is expected?** | Separate quantitative bridges for the two transitions ($\rho\le\rho_P$ and capacity scaling for c₀; dissolution and seal for c⁺) |
| **What result is obtained?** | The ontological distinction is canon; the single-structure reading of c₀ is an author's ruling (Section 3). The dynamical laws of the transitions are open |

## 10.3 The concept of a particle

A particle is a **projection** produced in Cₘ by particular readout conditions. That two observers count different numbers of particles is not an ontological contradiction.

**Honest note:** This largely coincides with the operational reading of standard quantum field theory. Evertophy produces no new position here; it **joins** an existing position.

# SECTION 11 — c⁺ AND THE DARK SECTOR

## 11.1 Internal ontological identity

$$\boxed{\;c^+ \;\equiv\; \text{DARK MATTER} \;\equiv\; \text{DARK ENERGY} \;\equiv\; \text{VACUUM}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;c^+ \;\equiv\; \text{DARK MATTER} \;\equiv\; \text{DARK ENERGY} \;\equiv\; \text{VACUUM}\;}
```

In Evertophy's internal ontology these are **the same entity**. Dark matter, dark energy and the vacuum are not three separate things but faces of the same phase that look different when seen from Cₘ. The vacuum calculations of quantum field theory are the computational language of this identity within Cₘ (Section 3).

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It ties dark matter, dark energy and the vacuum to a single phase identity |
| **Why does it do it?** | Uniting what appear to be three separate unknowns as three readings of the same phase is the centre of the framework's ontological economy |
| **How does it do it?** | It sets the distinction not as an energetic difference but as a difference of density, topology and readout environment (11.1a) |
| **What result is expected?** | A separate quantitative bridge for each appearance: rotation curves and lensing, accelerating expansion, vacuum effects |
| **What result is obtained?** | The identity is canon; the quantitative mechanism for each observational phenomenon is **open** (11.2) |

## 11.1a Dark matter and dark energy: clustered and expansion-sustaining c⁺

Dark matter and dark energy are not two separate mysterious components; they are **two different appearances of the same c⁺ structure**. The distinction is not energetic; it is a difference of **density and topology**. At laboratory scale, the same c⁺ is read as the vacuum.

| Appearance | Its reading in Cₘ |
|---|---|
| **Clustered c⁺** → dark matter | A c⁺ effect read in association with clusters of matter. The observed additional gravitational effect — galactic rotation curves, gravitational lensing — is the Cₘ reading of this appearance |
| **Expansion-sustaining c⁺** → dark energy | A c⁺ effect associated with expansion, read in regions of low matter density, far from the pressure of matter |
| **c⁺ at laboratory scale** → vacuum | c⁺ read as "empty space" in the measurement apparatus and inside the atom; vacuum phenomena such as the Casimir effect are this reading |

The distinction between identity and reading is written at the type level as follows:

$$\mathrm{Okuma}_{C_m}(c^+;\mathcal C)=\begin{cases}\mathrm{DM}_{\rm etkin}, & \mathcal C=\text{galaxy / cluster}\\ \mathrm{DE}_{\rm etkin}, & \mathcal C=\text{cosmological expansion}\\ \mathrm{Vak}_{\rm etkin}, & \mathcal C=\text{laboratory / inside the atom}\end{cases}$$

```latex
\mathrm{Okuma}_{C_m}(c^+;\mathcal C)=\begin{cases}\mathrm{DM}_{\rm etkin}, & \mathcal C=\text{galaxy / cluster}\\ \mathrm{DE}_{\rm etkin}, & \mathcal C=\text{cosmological expansion}\\ \mathrm{Vak}_{\rm etkin}, & \mathcal C=\text{laboratory / inside the atom}\end{cases}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathrm{Okuma}_{C_m}(c^+;\mathcal C)$ | The Cₘ reading of c⁺ in the observation context $\mathcal C$ |
| $\mathcal C$ | Cₘ observation context |
| $\mathrm{DM}_{\rm etkin}$, $\mathrm{DE}_{\rm etkin}$, $\mathrm{Vak}_{\rm etkin}$ | The effective appearances read as dark matter, dark energy and vacuum |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes at the type level that a single ontological object gives three different Cₘ readings in three different observation contexts |
| **Why does it do it?** | To prevent the sign "≡" from being read as an energy–momentum equation or as an equality of observed density / pressure |
| **How does it do it?** | It keeps the identity in a single source; it loads the difference onto the readout context |
| **What result is expected?** | The establishment of a physical selector that gives the density, pressure and perturbation dynamics for each context |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a type distinction**; it does not divide c⁺ into three separate phases. The quantitative mechanism of the readings is open (11.2, APPENDIX K.1) |

In the archive the names "bound" and "free" also appear for the first two appearances; the primary terms are those above.

This reading has five subtleties:

- **The "outer rim" is not a spatial edge.** "Outer" is used in an ontological sense: the unclustered appearance, far from the pressure of matter. Since three-dimensional space is dissolved in c⁺, c⁺ itself being located at a spatial edge is not defined. The quantitative agreement of the reading with measurements of isotropy and homogeneity has not yet been shown; this is a condition to be tested by model and observation.
- **Measurement and reading are separate.** What is measured is the **acceleration** of the expansion, and it is accepted unconditionally. That distant galaxies recede faster than light is also a result of standard cosmology and is not a local superluminal motion. The link between the beyond-c character of c⁺ and the expansion is an Evertophy reading.
- **Source and appearance are separate.** In its own phase c⁺ has no Cₘ-type rest mass and no metric; but the appearance in Cₘ of a c⁺-sourced effect may be a gravitational or metric reading. The distinction Force of Attraction ≠ gravitation is applied between the source phase and the measurement language in Cₘ (1.2). The gate lock does not prevent this reading (13.1a).
- **Identity does not mean equal density.** The identity c⁺ ≡ dark matter ≡ dark energy ≡ vacuum does not mean that the observed dark-matter density is equal to the dark-energy density; the readout notation describes the observation layer, not the identity.
- **The quantitative mechanism is open.** The quantitative bridge from c⁺ to the different pressure, clustering and expansion behaviours of dark matter and dark energy is an open debt (APPENDIX K).

## 11.2 Identity and mechanism are separated

$$\boxed{\;\text{ONTOLOGICAL IDENTITY IS NOT A QUANTITATIVE OBSERVATIONAL DERIVATION.}\;}$$

The following are at the model / bridge level **unless derived individually**:

- "Clustered c⁺ is exactly the dark-matter mechanism"
- "Expansion-sustaining c⁺ is exactly the dark-energy mechanism"

The identity claim is ontological; **a separate derivation is required for each observational phenomenon**, and none of these has yet been done.

### Author canon — the dark-sector appearances of c⁺ and the closed cycle

The joint birth of the three phases by $U_0$ in the same constitution is preserved. In this architecture, c⁺ is the source of the reading of **dark matter and dark energy as two Cₘ appearances of the same phase entity**:

$$\boxed{\;c^+\equiv{\rm DM}\equiv{\rm DE}\equiv\text{Vacuum}\quad\text{(ontological identity; different Cₘ appearances)}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;c^+\equiv{\rm DM}\equiv{\rm DE}\equiv\text{Vacuum}\quad\text{(ontological identity; different Cₘ appearances)}\;}
```

**The clustered appearance:** the **non-particle** structuring regime of c⁺ that becomes gravitationally prominent at galactic and cluster scale. The type-safe reading of the archive expression "having exceeded the speed-of-light limit through gravitation" is:

$$\boxed{\;\text{the matter-membership band } 0<v<c \text{ of Cₘ is no longer applicable}\;\neq\;\text{a claim of internal speed of the form } v_{c^+}>c\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{the matter-membership band } 0<v<c \text{ of Cₘ is no longer applicable}\;\neq\;\text{a claim of internal speed of the form } v_{c^+}>c\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $0<v<c$ | The matter-membership band of Cₘ (author's ruling) |
| $v=c$ | Not a member of the band, but the liminal shared boundary of Cₘ and c⁺ (boundary ≠ membership) |
| $v_{c^+}$ | An internal speed attributed to c⁺ — **not** defined |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It turns the archive expression "having exceeded the speed of light" into a type-safe form that assigns no speed to c⁺ |
| **Why does it do it?** | Carrying the speed type of Cₘ into c⁺ would be a category error and would create an unnecessary conflict with the measured results of relativity |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes explicitly the difference between "the band not being applicable" and "a speed value greater than the band" |
| **What result is expected?** | A quantitative reading of the clustered appearance that is independent of speed and rests on the phase regime |
| **What result is obtained?** | The ontological part — that c⁺ is a beyond-c regime and that a Cₘ-type speed cannot be applied there — is the locked formulation of 3.4. The **final mathematical representation** of the archive expression is an interpretation awaiting approval |

This record therefore does not bring back "superluminal matter"; it preserves the undefinedness in c⁺ of the Cₘ speed type, of the metric and of matter support. The gravitational effect seen in Cₘ observation is **the Cₘ reading** of the c⁺ regime.

**The expansion-sustaining appearance:** the expansion / dark-energy appearance, at cosmic scale, of the same ontological c⁺ regime. The clustered and expansion-sustaining appearances are not two separate substances but different Cₘ readout regimes of the same phase identity.

**The closed-cycle connection:** This identity is read together with the dilution branch of the double-limit structure kept as a core seed in Section 2.4; the full law of the cycle and the mathematical cycle mechanism are set aside for V24.

$$\boxed{\;c^+ \text{ HAS EFFECTS; it is NOT a controllable channel or an internal-speed sector.}\;}$$

This is a paradigm critique, not a rejection of experimental results. The variety of dark-matter candidates (the record of the "dark-matter zoo") is preserved as observational context indicating that any single particle candidate has weakened — but context is not evidence.

## 11.9 The reading of phantom-divide crossing

The crossing of the line $w=-1$ by the dark-energy equation of state (the phantom region) is a candidate for being read in the framework as "a change of regime of the c⁺ share in the Cₘ reading". In the literature, observations of phantom crossing are followed as supporting context; there is no quantitative correspondence.

*Redshift domain:* the physical domain is $z>-1$; the unbounded future of the expansion is $z\to-1^+$. $z\to-\infty$ is only a mathematical extension.

*Equation-of-state domain:* $w=-1$ corresponds to the cosmological constant; $w<-1$ is the phantom region. The phantom region violates the null energy condition, but it is not observationally excluded; therefore $w\ge-1$ is not written as "the physical domain".

$$\boxed{\;\text{"Compatible reading"}\neq\text{"solved / verified" — the ruling of 11.7 also applies here.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{"Compatible reading"}\neq\text{"solved / verified" — the ruling of 11.7 also applies here.}\;}
```

### Equation set — the A1 logistic readout model

This is a **model** that tries to derive the dark-energy equation of state $w(z)$ from a logistic reading of $X$ as a function of redshift:

$$w = -1 + \frac{(1+z)s}{3\Delta}\cdot\frac{(T - X_{\min})(X_{\max} - T)}{T(1-T)}\cdot\big[p(1-T) - qT\big]$$

```latex
w = -1 + \frac{(1+z)s}{3\Delta}\cdot\frac{(T - X_{\min})(X_{\max} - T)}{T(1-T)}\cdot\big[p(1-T) - qT\big]
```

Counterexample ($X_{\min}=0$, $X_{\max}=1$, $s=p=q=1$, $z_t=0$):

$$w = -1 + \tfrac{(1+z)(1-2X)}{3},\qquad w(10) = -4.66633374896$$

```latex
w = -1 + \tfrac{(1+z)(1-2X)}{3},\qquad w(10) = -4.66633374896
```

$$z \to +\infty:\ w/z \to -\tfrac13, \qquad z \to -\infty:\ w/z \to +\tfrac13, \qquad \text{at both ends } w \to -\infty$$

```latex
z \to +\infty:\ w/z \to -\tfrac13, \qquad z \to -\infty:\ w/z \to +\tfrac13, \qquad \text{at both ends } w \to -\infty
```

The correct condition ($0 \leq u < v \leq 1$, $s>0$, $p,q$ finite; $u=X_{\min}$, $v=X_{\max}$):

$$(u > 0 \lor p = 0) \wedge (v < 1 \lor q = 0) \;\Rightarrow\; w \to -1 \text{ at both ends}$$

```latex
(u > 0 \lor p = 0) \wedge (v < 1 \lor q = 0) \;\Rightarrow\; w \to -1 \text{ at both ends}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $X(z)$ | Logistic reading: $1/[1 + e^{-s(z - z_t)}]$ (not $X$ itself, but a projection of it) |
| $T$ | $X_{\min} + \Delta\,X(z)$ |
| $\Delta$ | $X_{\max} - X_{\min}$ |
| $X_{\min}$, $X_{\max}$ ($u$, $v$) | Lower and upper bounds of the reading |
| $s$, $z_t$ | Steepness and transition redshift |
| $p$, $q$ | Exponential weighting parameters |
| $z$ | Redshift; $1+z=a_{\rm ref}/a$, $a>0$ |
| $w$ | Dark-energy equation-of-state parameter (pressure / energy density) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It computes $w(z)$ from a redshift-dependent logistic reading of $X$ |
| **Why does it do it?** | To look for a testable model that makes contact with cosmological measurements of $w$ |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds the reading as a logistic curve between lower and upper bounds; it ties it to the equation of state through weighting parameters |
| **What result is expected?** | A curve, consistent with observation, that gives $w\to-1$ at both ends |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a conditional model.** The unconditional claim of a double asymptote is **wrong**: in the counterexample $w\to-\infty$ at both ends; $w\to-1$ is obtained only if the correct condition above is satisfied. The model is not part of the K1v2 core; its source derivation (MINILAB-4) is **To Be Addressed Later** |



# SECTION 12 — THRESHOLD AND STRUCTURE BANDS

## 12.1 The two-channel threshold

The orientation of a structure from Cₘ towards one of the outer phases is read through two separate channels: the mass channel operates towards c₀, the energy channel towards c⁺.

### The mass channel

$$\text{stellar mass} \;\to\; \text{critical-mass threshold} \;\to\; \text{saturation} \;\to\; \text{black-hole formation}$$

```latex
\text{stellar mass} \;\to\; \text{critical-mass threshold} \;\to\; \text{saturation} \;\to\; \text{black-hole formation}
```

Black-hole formation is an established phenomenon of external physics. In Evertophy, the end of this channel is the transformation of matter into a single contiguous structure, to a degree that converges to the metric-gapless state at the Planck scale; the density cannot exceed the limit $\rho\le\rho_P$, and as mass increases the structure does not become denser but larger (Section 3). The quantitative threshold and transition law for the mass channel, however, have not yet been derived. Black hole ≡ c₀ is not an inference presented as the result of this mechanism, but the author's ontological definition (3.2).

### The energy channel

$$\text{radiation} \;\to\; \text{convergence to the } c^+ \text{ band} \;\to\; \text{exhaustion of energy} \;\to\; \text{dilution}$$

```latex
\text{radiation} \;\to\; \text{convergence to the } c^+ \text{ band} \;\to\; \text{exhaustion of energy} \;\to\; \text{dilution}
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{The energy channel is at the MODEL / CONCEPTUAL level.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The energy channel is at the MODEL / CONCEPTUAL level.}\;}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It divides the orientation of Cₘ structures towards the outer phases into two channels: towards c₀ through the accumulation of mass, towards c⁺ through radiation and exhaustion |
| **Why does it do it?** | To show that the two directions operate through different physical processes and cannot be read with the same threshold |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes each channel as a chain of successive stages; it builds both not as a sharp boundary but as **a transitional band** |
| **What result is expected?** | Quantitative thresholds for both channels depending on scale and amount of mass (13.2) |
| **What result is obtained?** | The last stop of the mass channel is an ontological definition (black hole ≡ c₀); the quantitative threshold laws are open. The energy channel is at the conceptual level |

## 12.3 The structure band

Planetary, atomic, electromagnetic and biological structures can be represented within the phase-parameter landscape with different relations and constraints. This does not mean that objects are members of three phases at the same time.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Map position}\neq\text{phase membership}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Map position}\neq\text{phase membership}\;}
```

Cₘ is the phase of direct measurement and readout; the existence of an ontological relation with the other phases does not produce a mandatory mixture ontology of the form "Cₘ is not pure". That the space between particles inside an atom is the Cₘ reading of c⁺ (Section 3) does not make the atom a member of c⁺ either: the atom is a Cₘ structure, the empty space is the reading of c⁺.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Planets, atoms, electromagnetic and biological structures, while remaining members of Cₘ, may carry continuous constraints of relation and interaction with the three phases.}\;}$$

# SECTION 13 — PHASE TRANSITIONS

## 13.1 Gate and stamp

$$\boxed{\;\text{THE GATE IS BIDIRECTIONAL} \qquad \text{THE STAMP IS UNIDIRECTIONAL}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{THE GATE IS BIDIRECTIONAL} \qquad \text{THE STAMP IS UNIDIRECTIONAL}\;}
```

Irreversibility comes not from the phase gate but from **the act of sealing**. A gate can be passed in both directions; but the seal written into c⁺ of an event that has occurred in Cₘ cannot be undone. The unidirectionality of time is a property not of the gate but of the seal.

## 13.1a Gate, transport and lock

A gate states that a transition between two phases is possible. But the existence of a gate does not mean that every quantity can pass through that gate. These two things must be kept separate:

$$\boxed{\;\text{Gate} \;\neq\; \text{Transport permission}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Gate} \;\neq\; \text{Transport permission}\;}
```

The gate structure is a complete triangle: each phase stands in a gate relation with the other two (c₀ ↔ Cₘ, Cₘ ↔ c⁺, c₀ ↔ c⁺). For a quantity to be transportable through a gate, however, it must be natively defined **in the phases at both ends** of the gate.

From this follows an important formally proven result — within the scope of the encoded formal system, as a theorem of the core ontology. Time, three-dimensional space, the metric, internal speed, the speed-of-light limit and rest mass are defined only in Cₘ; for this reason **they cannot be transported through any gate**. The general form is:

$$\boxed{\;q \text{ is gate-locked} \;\Longleftrightarrow\; q \text{ is natively defined in at most one phase}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;q \text{ is gate-locked} \;\Longleftrightarrow\; q \text{ is natively defined in at most one phase}\;}
```

That a quantity is not on the list of Cₘ-specific quantities does not determine its native definition in other phases, or its transport permission for a particular pair of phases; formal permission and physical realization are separated further. This lock does not prevent a c⁺-sourced effect from being **read** in Cₘ. Transport and reading are different things: no quantity is transported from c⁺ to Cₘ, but a c⁺-sourced effect can be measured in Cₘ, in Cₘ's own language (15.1a). Only phase-independent labels pass through the gates. The coordination of $X$ between phases, by contrast, is a separate meta-crossing and does not count as physical transport.

*Reading rule:* In the other sections of the document, the expressions "transition", "effect" or "passing through the gate" are read according to three separate concepts: **gate** (structural permission), **transport permission** (specific to a quantity) and **readout** (the observation in Cₘ of a c⁺-sourced effect — not transport).

$$\boxed{\;\text{Gate}\neq\text{Transport}\neq\text{Readout}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Gate}\neq\text{Transport}\neq\text{Readout}\;}
```

### Equation set — gate, transport permission and gate lock

$$\text{GateLocked}(nat) \iff \text{AtMostOneNative}(nat) \qquad \text{(under the complete K3)}$$

```latex
\text{GateLocked}(nat) \iff \text{AtMostOneNative}(nat) \qquad \text{(under the complete K3)}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `GateRelated a b` | $a \neq b$ — **complete K3**: every distinct pair of phases stands in a gate relation |
| `NativeOK q p` | Whether the quantity $q$ is natively defined in phase $p$ |
| `TransportPermission q a b` | `Gate ∧ NativeOK q a ∧ NativeOK q b` — transport permission |
| `CmOnlyKind` | Kinds defined only in Cₘ: rest mass, time, three-dimensional space, metric, internal speed, the limit $c$ |
| `GateLocked nat` | $\forall a\,b,\ \neg\,\text{Transport}$ — cannot be transported through any gate |
| `AtMostOneNative nat` | $\forall x\,y,\ nat\,x \to nat\,y \to x = y$ — native in at most one phase |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It formally separates transition between phases (gate) from the transport of a quantity between phases, and characterizes the gate lock |
| **Why does it do it?** | The old `adjacent` function ignored the c₀ ↔ c⁺ gate; the old K2 also said "mass is carried in c⁺". Both contradicted the current ontology |
| **How does it do it?** | It ties transport permission, in addition to the existence of the gate, to the quantity being natively defined at both ends; from this it proves the equivalence "defined in at most one phase ⇔ gate-locked" |
| **What result is expected?** | That leakage of Cₘ-specific quantities (time, metric, rest mass…) into the outer phases is formally prevented |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Proven** (★ gate ≠ transport, gate lock). The old "witness-existence" generalization **is wrong** and has been removed. This is an algebraic theorem following from the definitions of the complete K3: it does not establish that K3 is realized in nature, nor does it determine the amplitudes, durations, probabilities or energy flows of transitions |

## 13.2 The transition criterion

Since $X$ need not be a single scalar, the general transition condition is not defined by a scalar equality $X_{\rm crit}$. The type-safe core statement is:

$$\boxed{\;(X,\Theta_{ij}) \in \mathcal D_{ij}^{\rm adm} \;\Longleftrightarrow\; \mathcal A_{ij}(X,\Theta_{ij})=1\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;(X,\Theta_{ij}) \in \mathcal D_{ij}^{\rm adm} \;\Longleftrightarrow\; \mathcal A_{ij}(X,\Theta_{ij})=1\;}
```

In models in which a scalar or band realization is chosen, the edge-specific reading $\xi_{ij}$ is used instead of $X$ itself:

$$\xi_{ij} \in [\xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,-},\ \xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,+}] \ \ (\text{point reading}) \qquad B_{\xi_{ij}} \cap [\xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,-},\ \xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,+}] \neq \varnothing \ \ (\text{band reading})$$

```latex
\xi_{ij} \in [\xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,-},\ \xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,+}] \ \ (\text{point reading}) \qquad B_{\xi_{ij}} \cap [\xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,-},\ \xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,+}] \neq \varnothing \ \ (\text{band reading})
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $i$, $j$ | The phases at the two ends of the edge |
| $\Theta_{ij}$ | The transition-specific family of context / conditions |
| $\mathcal D_{ij}^{\rm adm}$ | The admissible domain of the edge in question |
| $\mathcal A_{ij}$ | Admission indicator: if it is 1, the transition is permitted |
| $\xi_{ij}=\pi_{ij}(X)$ | The edge-specific reading |
| $\xi_{ij}^{\rm crit,\pm}$ | Lower and upper bounds of the critical range (a single-point range of the form $[P,P]$ is also valid) |
| $B_{\xi_{ij}}$ | The band (range) form of the reading |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines under which condition a phase transition is **permitted** |
| **Why does it do it?** | A scalar threshold would contradict the non-scalar nature of $X$; the transition condition must depend on the whole of the resolver and on the context |
| **How does it do it?** | In the general case it requires $X$ and the context to lie in the admissible domain; at the model level this becomes concrete as a reading falling within the critical range, or its band intersecting the range |
| **What result is expected?** | Concrete critical ranges for each edge, depending on scale and amount of mass |
| **What result is obtained?** | The type-safe definition is canon; point and band readings are **special cases**, not the ontological definition of $X$. The emphasis is on **"permitted"**: permission does **not make** the transition **mandatory** |

**Author's ruling:** Phase-transition thresholds are evaluated according to scale and amount of mass; the projections of the Force of Attraction depend on the same variables (1.2).

## 13.3 Classification of edges

| Edge | Stamp-carrying? | Observable in Cₘ? |
|---|---|---|
| $\Delta_{0m}$ | Yes | Yes |
| $\Delta_{+m}$ | Yes | Indirectly |
| $\Delta_{0+}$ | No | No |

$\Delta_{0+}$ produces no stamp, because it does not touch Cₘ — the record opens on the edges that touch Cₘ.

### Edge carriage ≠ directional production

$$\boxed{\;\text{That an edge is STAMP-CARRYING does NOT MEAN that it produces stamps IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{That an edge is STAMP-CARRYING does NOT MEAN that it produces stamps IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.}\;}
```

The meaning of direction is a separate matter. In the formal package, the stamp-carriage record of the form `stampOf(c⁺ → Cₘ) = κ₊ₘ` and the reading of the direction of writing into the ontological archive as Cₘ → c⁺ need not be the same layer of meaning. The protective sentence in this version is:

$$\boxed{\;\text{The direction in which }\kappa_{+m}\text{ is carried is deliberately open; stamp carriage does not by itself determine the meaning of direction.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The direction in which }\kappa_{+m}\text{ is carried is deliberately open; stamp carriage does not by itself determine the meaning of direction.}\;}
```

The author's ruling (7.1) fixes the direction of the ontological record channel as **Cₘ → c⁺**. Aligning the carriage direction of $\kappa_{+m}$ in the formal package with this ruling is an open debt. The code is not silently reversed to close this debt; nor is the ontology derived solely from the direction of the existing code.

## 13.4 Three different "absences" — and the correct formalization

| Concept | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **No transition** | The edge does not exist |
| **Stampless transition** | The edge exists, the transition exists, no stamp was produced |
| **Algebraic unit** | The neutral element in the stamp algebra |

### The correct distinction

At the level of meaning, "no stamp" and, at the algebraic level, "unit" are **different** things; they must therefore be expressed with two separate maps:

$$\boxed{\;\text{emittedStamp}(T_{0+}) = \texttt{None} \qquad\qquad \Sigma(T_{0+}) = e\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{emittedStamp}(T_{0+}) = \texttt{None} \qquad\qquad \Sigma(T_{0+}) = e\;}
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{NO TYPE-LEVEL IDENTITY of the form } e = \texttt{NoStamp} \text{ IS SET UP.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{NO TYPE-LEVEL IDENTITY of the form } e = \texttt{NoStamp} \text{ IS SET UP.}\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $T_{0+}$ | A transition on the c₀ ↔ c⁺ edge |
| $\text{emittedStamp}$ | The map giving the stamp produced by a transition; its result type is `Option Stamp` |
| `None` | The value "no stamp was produced" |
| $\Sigma$ | The algebraic image map from transitions to the stamp monoid |
| $e$ | The unit (neutral) element of the stamp monoid |
| $M$ | The stamp monoid |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates the concepts "no stamp was produced" and "algebraic unit" into two separate maps |
| **Why does it do it?** | $\Sigma : \text{Transition}\to M$ goes into a monoid, and the monoid does not contain `None`; merging the two concepts into one map would be a type error |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the **production** of a stamp with a separate map that returns `Option Stamp`, and the **algebraic image** of the stamp with the monoid map |
| **What result is expected?** | That the stampless edge is expressed consistently without breaking the stamp algebra |
| **What result is obtained?** | This is the correct formalization; no type-level identity is set up between $e$ and `NoStamp` |

## 13.5 A safe candidate for Δ₀₊

The safest formal candidate for this edge is:

$$\text{ArchiveEffect} = \text{No change}, \qquad\qquad \text{CoordinationEffect} = X\text{-coordination}$$

```latex
\text{ArchiveEffect} = \text{No change}, \qquad\qquad \text{CoordinationEffect} = X\text{-coordination}
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{Direct RAW ARCHIVE writing / reading is NOT IMPORTED for } c_0 \leftrightarrow c^+\text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Direct RAW ARCHIVE writing / reading is NOT IMPORTED for } c_0 \leftrightarrow c^+\text{.}\;}
```

Direct access to the archive would make c₀ history-sensitive; this contradicts its pre-temporal character. This coordination structure **is not identified with $U_0$**.

The weakest and safest interface of the formal candidate is a compatibility relation:

$$\boxed{\;\mathcal R^X_{0+}\subseteq \mathcal X_0\times\mathcal X_+\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathcal R^X_{0+}\subseteq \mathcal X_0\times\mathcal X_+\;}
```

$\mathcal X_0$ and $\mathcal X_+$ here are not two separate fundamental $X$'s; they are the phase-indexed admissible presentation spaces of the single meta-resolver $X$. For the internal realization, a span / fibre candidate may be used:

$$\boxed{\;\mathcal X_0 \xleftarrow{p_0}\mathfrak F^X_{0+}\xrightarrow{p_+}\mathcal X_+\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathcal X_0 \xleftarrow{p_0}\mathfrak F^X_{0+}\xrightarrow{p_+}\mathcal X_+\;}
```

If a single target per source is proven in the relation, a partial function can be obtained. But the bidirectional gate does not by itself yield the result

$$F^X_{+0}=(F^X_{0+})^{-1}$$

```latex
F^X_{+0}=(F^X_{0+})^{-1}
```

an exact inverse or a bijective correspondence requires separate proof.

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal R^X_{0+}$ | The $X$-compatibility relation between the c₀ and c⁺ presentations |
| $\mathcal X_0$, $\mathcal X_+$ | The admissible presentation spaces of the single $X$ for c₀ and c⁺ |
| $\mathfrak F^X_{0+}$ | Transition witness / matching structure (fibre) |
| $p_0$, $p_+$ | Projections from the fibre to the two presentation spaces |
| $F^X_{0+}$, $F^X_{+0}$ | Partial functions in the two directions, if they exist |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It gives the stampless c₀ ↔ c⁺ edge a meaning of compatibility only at the level of $X$, without transporting archive or matter |
| **Why does it do it?** | The edge exists (complete K3) but does not touch Cₘ; it must be given a meaning, and that meaning must not make c₀ history-sensitive |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the relation as a subset of the Cartesian product of the two presentation spaces; it builds the internal realization with a fibre and two projections |
| **What result is expected?** | A partial function if a single target per source is proven; an exact inverse if separately proven |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a formal candidate.** $\mathfrak F^X_{0+}$ is not a new phase, it is not $U_0$, and it carries no physical time. The bidirectional gate does not automatically give an exact inverse |
