## 13.6 The formal candidate of X-coordination for c₀ ↔ c⁺

*Status:* This section is a **formal candidate** for the particular relation and fibre realization between c₀ and c⁺. The general meta-coordination of $X$ (4.6) is definite.

The current safe meaning of the direct edge is not raw archive transport or matter transport, but **compatibility / coordination at the level of $X$** (the relation and fibre of 13.5). In this structure:

- $\mathfrak F^X_{0+}$ **is not a new phase**;
- it is not $U_0$;
- it carries no physical time or rate parameter;
- it can be upgraded to a partial function if a single target is proven for each source state;
- the bidirectional gate does not automatically give the exact correspondence $F_{+0}=F_{0+}^{-1}$.

The deterministic resolution rule of $X$ under the same fully determined state supports **the functionality of the resolver output**; but it does not prove the reversibility of the phase transition.

### Note on the model layer — the direct c₀ ⇄ c⁺ edge lock

In a thought-experiment rate model, the author approved the following rule for the direct c₀ ⇄ c⁺ edge (2026-10-14): the potential of the edge is written as the ratio of the expansion ratio to the gravitational ratio; the stampless edge does not include the quantum localization ratio.

$$\Psi_{0+}(\ell,M)=\ln\frac{\Xi_\Lambda}{\Xi_g}=\ln\frac{\Lambda c^4\ell^4}{6G^2M^2},\qquad \Xi_g=\frac{2GM}{c^2\ell},\quad \Xi_\Lambda=\frac{\Lambda c^2\ell^3}{3GM},\quad \Xi_q=\frac{\hbar}{Mc\ell}$$

```latex
\Psi_{0+}(\ell,M)=\ln\frac{\Xi_\Lambda}{\Xi_g}=\ln\frac{\Lambda c^4\ell^4}{6G^2M^2},\qquad \Xi_g=\frac{2GM}{c^2\ell},\quad \Xi_\Lambda=\frac{\Lambda c^2\ell^3}{3GM},\quad \Xi_q=\frac{\hbar}{Mc\ell}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Psi_{0+}$ | The direct c₀ ⇄ c⁺ edge potential (model) |
| $\ell$, $M$ | The scale considered and the mass within it — Cₘ readout magnitudes |
| $\Xi_g$ | The gravitational projection ratio (the inverse of the ratio of the scale to the Schwarzschild radius) |
| $\Xi_\Lambda$ | The expansion / attraction ratio |
| $\Xi_q$ | The quantum localization (Compton) ratio; the stampless edge does not include it |
| $\Lambda$ | Cosmological constant |
| $G$, $c$, $\hbar$ | Gravitational constant, speed of light, reduced Planck constant |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It parametrizes, with a single potential, at which scale and mass the stampless c₀ ⇄ c⁺ edge is effective |
| **Why does it do it?** | To express in magnitudes readable in Cₘ the relative weight between the two poles of the edge — attraction (the c₀ direction) and expansion (the c⁺ direction) |
| **How does it do it?** | It divides the expansion ratio by the gravitational ratio and takes the logarithm; it deliberately leaves the quantum ratio out, because the edge does not touch Cₘ and produces no stamp |
| **What result is expected?** | That the line $\Psi_{0+}=0$ gives the threshold at which the two model indicators ($\Xi_\Lambda$ and $\Xi_g$) become equal; the mass dependence of the cycle affinity. This threshold is not the scale at which the accelerations balance (see the note below) |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is **a model rule**, not an ontological definition; it does not change the ontological meaning of 13.4–13.6: the edge is stampless ($\Sigma(T_{0+})=e$), imports no raw archive writing / reading and carries no physical time. The rate (speed) language in the model belongs only to the model's Cₘ readout accounting. The $\Xi$ ratios here are unrelated to $\Xi=\ln\chi_S$ in 8.8 and 17 |

**Distinguishing the threshold from the balance of accelerations.** $\Xi_\Lambda$ is, in a Newtonian-type reading, the ratio of the expansion acceleration to the gravitational acceleration. The balance of accelerations is the condition $\Xi_\Lambda=1$; $\Psi_{0+}=0$, by contrast, is the condition $\Xi_\Lambda=\Xi_g$, and in general gives a different scale:

$$a_g=\frac{GM}{\ell^2},\quad a_\Lambda=\frac{\Lambda c^2\ell}{3},\quad \frac{a_\Lambda}{a_g}=\Xi_\Lambda;\qquad \ell_{\rm denge}=\left(\frac{3GM}{\Lambda c^2}\right)^{1/3},\qquad \ell_{\Psi=0}=\left(\frac{6G^2M^2}{\Lambda c^4}\right)^{1/4}$$

```latex
a_g=\frac{GM}{\ell^2},\quad a_\Lambda=\frac{\Lambda c^2\ell}{3},\quad \frac{a_\Lambda}{a_g}=\Xi_\Lambda;\qquad \ell_{\rm denge}=\left(\frac{3GM}{\Lambda c^2}\right)^{1/3},\qquad \ell_{\Psi=0}=\left(\frac{6G^2M^2}{\Lambda c^4}\right)^{1/4}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $a_g$, $a_\Lambda$ | Gravitational and expansion acceleration readings |
| $\ell_{\rm denge}$ | The scale at which the accelerations become equal ($\Xi_\Lambda=1$) |
| $\ell_{\Psi=0}$ | The scale at which the edge potential is zero ($\Xi_\Lambda=\Xi_g$) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates the model threshold from the physical balance of accelerations |
| **Why does it do it?** | The earlier explanation defined $\Psi_{0+}=0$ as "the scale at which attraction and expansion balance"; the equation does not give this |
| **How does it do it?** | It solves the two conditions separately and finds two different scales |
| **What result is expected?** | That the correct scale is used in the physical interpretation of the edge lock |
| **What result is obtained?** | The model rule approved by the author **is preserved unchanged**; only its explanation is corrected. For one solar mass, $\ell_{\rm denge}\approx3.4\times10^{18}$ m ($\approx111$ pc), $\ell_{\Psi=0}\approx5.9\times10^{14}$ m |

Under the rule, the cycle affinity of the model depends only on mass and **approaches** zero for $M\gg m_P$. The parameter domain is $w>0$, $M>0$ (undefined at $w=0$; the sign changes for $w<0$); in this domain the affinity is positive at every finite mass:

$$\mathcal A=\frac{1}{w}\ln\!\left(1+\frac{m_P^2}{2M^2}\right)$$

```latex
\mathcal A=\frac{1}{w}\ln\!\left(1+\frac{m_P^2}{2M^2}\right)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal A$ | Cycle affinity; if it is zero, the model is in detailed balance |
| $w$ | Threshold softness (model band, $w>0$; not to be confused with the equation-of-state $w$ of 11) |
| $m_P$ | Planck mass |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It measures whether there is a net cycle flux among the three phases in the model |
| **Why does it do it?** | To see at which mass scale departure from detailed balance matters |
| **How does it do it?** | Under the edge rule, it sums the potential differences around the cycle; the result depends only on the ratio $M/m_P$ |
| **What result is expected?** | That balance is seen at macroscopic masses and net flux at small masses |
| **What result is obtained?** | $\mathcal A$ is positive at every finite mass and approaches zero as $\mathcal A\simeq m_P^2/(2wM^2)$ for $M\gg m_P$; there is no exact vanishing. The affinity alone does not determine the size of the flux: together with the transition rates of the model (V24 §732), the region in which the net current exceeds the threshold $10^{-3}$ is only $\log_{10}(M/\mathrm{kg})\in[-42,-27]$, that is, **subatomic masses** (a model result within the model and parameter band examined). An upper bound on the subatomic cycle rate from entropy production is an open debt (APPENDIX K). The numerical bands and the derivation are in the V24 rolling master record (§732) |



# SECTION 14 — THE STAMP ALGEBRA AND THE ARCHIVE

## 14.1 Sealing

$$\boxed{\;\text{SEALING} = \text{the IRREVERSIBLE writing of a record.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{SEALING} = \text{the IRREVERSIBLE writing of a record.}\;}
```

Sealing takes place not inside Cₘ, but **at the threshold interfaces that touch Cₘ**. The record channel is unidirectional (Cₘ → c⁺); once sealing has taken place, the record is fixed (author's ruling, 7.1). This section builds the algebraic language of this fixity: if something can be written but cannot be erased, the mathematics of that structure is not a group but a **monoid**.

## 14.2 The stamp monoid

$$M = \big\langle s,\; s',\; \kappa_{0m},\; \kappa_{+m} \;\big|\; \kappa_{0m}^2 = \kappa_{0m},\;\; \kappa_{+m}^2 = \kappa_{+m} \big\rangle$$

```latex
M = \big\langle s,\; s',\; \kappa_{0m},\; \kappa_{+m} \;\big|\; \kappa_{0m}^2 = \kappa_{0m},\;\; \kappa_{+m}^2 = \kappa_{+m} \big\rangle
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $M$ | The stamp monoid |
| $s$, $s'$ | Free transition generators (free stamps) |
| $\kappa_{0m}$ | The lock stamp of the edge Δ₀ₘ |
| $\kappa_{+m}$ | The lock stamp of the edge Δ₊ₘ |
| $\langle\ \cdot\ \mid\ \cdot\ \rangle$ | Presentation: generators on the left, relations on the right |
| $\kappa^2=\kappa$ | Idempotence: stamping the lock a second time does not change the algebraic image |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines the algebraic trace in the archive of transitions between phases as a monoid with four generators and two relations |
| **Why does it do it?** | The smallest algebraic structure is needed that simultaneously preserves the order, provenance and non-erasability of records |
| **How does it do it?** | It leaves the free stamps free; it makes only the two locks idempotent. Two separate lock generators preserve the information of which edge was crossed |
| **What result is expected?** | A full classification of the idempotents, uniqueness of normal forms, the structure of the group completion |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Proven as the core archive algebra** (subsections below) |

### Why a monoid, and why not a group

$$\boxed{\;\text{There is NO } s^{-1} \text{ — because there is NO PRIMITIVE OPERATION OF ERASURE. The prohibition of invertibility IS ITSELF the reason } M \text{ is not a group.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{There is NO } s^{-1} \text{ — because there is NO PRIMITIVE OPERATION OF ERASURE. The prohibition of invertibility IS ITSELF the reason } M \text{ is not a group.}\;}
```

In a group, every element has an inverse; a record that has an inverse is an erasable record. Since the seal cannot be erased in Evertophy, the algebra of the archive cannot be a group. This is not a deficiency; it is the ontology itself.

### Idempotents

$$\mathrm{Idem}(M) = \{e,\; \kappa_{0m},\; \kappa_{+m}\}$$

```latex
\mathrm{Idem}(M) = \{e,\; \kappa_{0m},\; \kappa_{+m}\}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It lists all the elements of the monoid that are unchanged when multiplied by themselves |
| **Why does it do it?** | To know exactly which stamps show the lock behaviour of "being complete once stamped" |
| **How does it do it?** | In the free sector the square of a word is longer than the word itself; hence $ww=w$ is impossible. What remains are the unit and the two locks |
| **What result is expected?** | That apart from the locks no stamp is idempotent by itself |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Formally proven:** $w\cdot w = w$ if and only if $w \in \{e,\ \kappa_{0m},\ \kappa_{+m}\}$ |

### Forbidden relations

| Relation | Why forbidden |
|---|---|
| $ss' = s's$ | Word order is event order; order cannot be erased |
| $\kappa s = \kappa$ | Records could no longer be added after a lock |
| $s^N = \kappa$ | A free stamp cannot turn into a lock by itself |

### Group completion — and its interpretation

Since a group has invertibility, the locks go to the unit in the completion; what remains is a free group.

$$\boxed{\;\text{THIS DOES NOT MEAN PHYSICAL INVERTIBILITY. Group completion is an ALGEBRAIC TOOL.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{THIS DOES NOT MEAN PHYSICAL INVERTIBILITY. Group completion is an ALGEBRAIC TOOL.}\;}
```

That the locks become trivial in the algebraic completion **does not imply** that a record in the archive can be erased. Moreover, collapsing the locks to a single absorbing zero **is rejected**; this would erase provenance and order information.

On the assumption that the presentation remains closed with only the idempotent relations above, the idempotents go to the unit in the universal group completion, and the following results follow:

$$\boxed{\;G_{\rm univ}(M)\cong F_2\;}\qquad\qquad \boxed{\;K(M)\cong \mathbb Z^2\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;G_{\rm univ}(M)\cong F_2\;}\qquad\qquad \boxed{\;K(M)\cong \mathbb Z^2\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $G_{\rm univ}(M)$ | The universal group completion of $M$ |
| $F_2$ | The free group on two generators ($s$, $s'$) |
| $K(M)$ | The commutative (abelian) universal group completion |
| $\mathbb Z^2$ | The two-dimensional integer lattice |
| $\cong$ | Isomorphism |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It computes what remains when inverse elements are artificially added to the stamp monoid |
| **Why does it do it?** | Completion is a powerful algebraic tool for counting and accounting; but the limit of its physical meaning must be drawn explicitly |
| **How does it do it?** | When inverses are added, the relation $\kappa^2=\kappa$ collapses to $\kappa=e$; what remains is the free group on $s$ and $s'$, and, in the commutative case, two integer counters |
| **What result is expected?** | That the net count of free stamps can be kept with two integers |
| **What result is obtained?** | The results are **conditional on the presentation remaining closed**; if new relations are added later, they are checked again. Completion is not physical invertibility |

### There is no counter-stamp

The "negative" of a stamp **does not exist** as a physical object. It can be derived in the algebraic completion; it is **an accounting tool**.

### Proof status of the stamp algebra

The basic properties of the stamp algebra have been proven in general, beyond finite sampling: associativity, that the rewriting process always terminates, that every word reaches a unique normal form, and that different reduction paths reach the same result (confluence).

One subtlety must be noted: confluence does not mean that **events can be interchanged**. When the order of events is held fixed, different groupings and reduction paths reach the same normal form; but in general $ab \neq ba$, and the order of events changes the result. The archive preserves the order of history.

### Equation set — the stamp monoid and the archive algebra

$$u\,\kappa\,\kappa\,v \;\to\; u\,\kappa\,v \qquad (\kappa \in \{\kappa_{0m}, \kappa_{+m}\})$$

```latex
u\,\kappa\,\kappa\,v \;\to\; u\,\kappa\,v \qquad (\kappa \in \{\kappa_{0m}, \kappa_{+m}\})
```

$$w \cdot w = w \iff w \in \{e,\ \kappa_{0m},\ \kappa_{+m}\}$$

```latex
w \cdot w = w \iff w \in \{e,\ \kappa_{0m},\ \kappa_{+m}\}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $u$, $v$ | The parts of the word before and after (arbitrary sequences of generators) |
| $w$ | A word (sequence of generators) |
| $\cdot$ / `mulW` | Concatenation reduced to normal form |
| $\to$, $\to^*$ | Single-step / multi-step rewriting |
| $e$ | The empty word (unit) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It gives the rewriting rule that reduces two consecutive identical locks to one, and the classification of idempotents |
| **Why does it do it?** | So that the archive counterpart of the same history is not ambiguous: with the order of events fixed, different bracketings and orders of reduction must reach the same normal form |
| **How does it do it?** | It merges only identical locks that stand side by side; it does not touch the free stamps or the order |
| **What result is expected?** | Termination, unique normal form and confluence |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Proven** (★ core archive algebra). The group completion $G(M)\cong F_2$, $K_{ab}(M)\cong\mathbb Z^2$ holds for the stated closed presentation. Moreover `None ≠ Some(e)`: **no stamp being produced** and **the unit stamp** are different. This is not independence from the interchange of events |

## 14.3 The weight function

How much a stamp narrows can be measured by a ratio of ranges. For consecutive stamps the ratios multiply, and in the logarithm they add — that is, a homomorphism is obtained.

### Consequence for the lock

For a state-independent additive character $\omega$, the relation $\kappa^2=\kappa$ gives $\omega(\kappa)=0$; this is not assigned by hand but **derived**. But this result does not require that the coverage range built from independent range endpoints be $[0,0]$, or that a physical lock produce no cost at its first application. Three objects are separate:

| Object | Its property |
|---|---|
| **Exact character** | State-independent and additive; $\omega(\kappa)=0$ |
| **Coverage range** | Built from independent endpoints; if it validly covers the exact value, $0\in\Omega_{\rm enc}(\kappa)$, but it need not be $[0,0]$ |
| **State-dependent physical narrowing cost** | May be non-zero at the first application |

For example, $P(W)=\min(W,1)$ is idempotent; but in the state $W=2$ the first application produces a cost $\ln 2$, the second does not. A mathematical cost model can be written as follows — it is not assigned as the physical law of sealing:

$$C(T_2\circ T_1,s)=C(T_1,s)+C(T_2,T_1(s)),\qquad C(T,s)=\ln W(s)-\ln W(T(s)),\quad W>0$$

```latex
C(T_2\circ T_1,s)=C(T_1,s)+C(T_2,T_1(s)),\qquad C(T,s)=\ln W(s)-\ln W(T(s)),\quad W>0
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $T$, $T_1$, $T_2$ | State transformations (applications of stamps) |
| $s$ | State |
| $W(s)$ | The width of the state (the size of the permitted range), strictly positive |
| $C(T,s)$ | The narrowing cost produced by $T$ in state $s$ |
| $\circ$ | Composition (first $T_1$, then $T_2$) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It measures, as a logarithmic cost, how much a transformation narrows the state, and shows that costs add under composition |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make explicit that, even if the algebraic character of the lock is zero, its first application may have a physical price |
| **How does it do it?** | It defines the cost as the difference of the logarithms of the width before and after; in composition it takes account of the intermediate state ($T_1(s)$) |
| **What result is expected?** | A cost that is zero at the second application of an idempotent transformation and may be non-zero at its first |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is a mathematical **cost model**; it is not assigned as the physical law of sealing |

### Character ≠ physical lock

$$\boxed{\;\text{That the weight is zero does NOT MEAN that the lock is PHYSICALLY INEFFECTIVE.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{That the weight is zero does NOT MEAN that the lock is PHYSICALLY INEFFECTIVE.}\;}
```

It says only that the lock lies in the kernel of the additive character. The lock can still change subsequent compositions and coarsen the state space.

**The direction of the ratio must be preserved:** the weight is defined in the direction "width before / width after"; if inverted, the sign breaks. For positive widths, the basic notation is:

$$\boxed{\;\Omega(m)=\log\frac{W_{\rm önce}}{W_{\rm sonra}}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\Omega(m)=\log\frac{W_{\rm önce}}{W_{\rm sonra}}\;}
```

At a common state $s$, the **exact character** preserves dependence and is exactly additive:

$$\boxed{\;\omega_{AB}(s)=\ln W_A(s)-\ln W_B(s)\;}\qquad \omega_{AB}(s)+\omega_{BC}(s)=\omega_{AC}(s)$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\omega_{AB}(s)=\ln W_A(s)-\ln W_B(s)\;}\qquad \omega_{AB}(s)+\omega_{BC}(s)=\omega_{AC}(s)
```

A range built from independent endpoint values, by contrast, is not the exact character but a **coverage range** ($W_A$ "before", $W_B$ "after"):

$$\boxed{\;\Omega_{\rm enc}(m)=\left[\log\frac{W_A^-}{W_B^+},\ \log\frac{W_A^+}{W_B^-}\right]\;}\qquad \Omega_{\rm enc}(uv)\subseteq\Omega_{\rm enc}(u)\oplus\Omega_{\rm enc}(v)$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\Omega_{\rm enc}(m)=\left[\log\frac{W_A^-}{W_B^+},\ \log\frac{W_A^+}{W_B^-}\right]\;}\qquad \Omega_{\rm enc}(uv)\subseteq\Omega_{\rm enc}(u)\oplus\Omega_{\rm enc}(v)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $m$ | A stamp or a transition |
| $W_{\rm önce}$, $W_{\rm sonra}$ | The width before and after the stamp |
| $\Omega(m)$ | The weight of the stamp (measure of narrowing) |
| $\omega_{AB}(s)$ | The exact character from A to B at the common state $s$ |
| $W_A^{\pm}$, $W_B^{\pm}$ | The range endpoints of the widths (lower / upper) |
| $\Omega_{\rm enc}$ | Coverage range |
| $\oplus$ | Independent range sum (Minkowski sum) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines the weight of a stamp both as an exact number (at a common state) and as a range (with independent endpoints) |
| **Why does it do it?** | In the Range Formalism, when endpoints are taken independently, dependency information is lost; confusing the exact value with the coverage range leads to wrong results |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds the exact character as a difference of logarithms at the same state, and the coverage range with the worst combinations of endpoints |
| **What result is expected?** | Exact additivity for the exact character; only an inclusion relation for the coverage range |
| **What result is obtained?** | For example, for $W_A(s)=W_B(s)=s$, $s\in[1,2]$, at a common state $\log(W_A/W_B)=0$, while the coverage range is $[-\log2,\ \log2]$. Exact additivity and vanishing on idempotents hold **only for the exact character**; this coverage result does not carry over automatically to the range of state-dependent physical cost. The logarithm requires the widths to be strictly positive |

The lock effect can be carried by a separate idempotent representation or projector:

$$\Pi_\kappa:\mathcal B\to\mathcal B,\qquad \Pi_\kappa^2=\Pi_\kappa$$

```latex
\Pi_\kappa:\mathcal B\to\mathcal B,\qquad \Pi_\kappa^2=\Pi_\kappa
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Pi_\kappa$ | The projector representation of the lock |
| $\mathcal B$ | The space of states |

Thus the vanishing of the exact character on an idempotent ($\omega(\kappa)=0$) does not remove the physical effect of the lock: the effect is carried not by the character but by the projector.

### Computational note

Range arithmetic must be done **with exact numbers**. In floating-point arithmetic, associativity breaks under rounding.

### Sealing cost — historical bridge, candidate reading

Two accounts of the sealing cost $C_s$ stand together in the archive; both are here in the **candidate-reading** mode. To avoid confusing the quantity whose source symbol in the historical model is $\Gamma$ with the $\Gamma$ of the current time scaffold, let us write it as $\Gamma_{\rm hist}$:

$$\Gamma_{\rm hist}=\Gamma_0(1+\lambda D_+)^{-1},\qquad C_s\propto\frac{1}{\Gamma_{\rm hist}}\propto(1+\lambda D_+),\qquad \nabla C_s=\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S \quad (\Lambda \text{ constant})$$

```latex
\Gamma_{\rm hist}=\Gamma_0(1+\lambda D_+)^{-1},\qquad C_s\propto\frac{1}{\Gamma_{\rm hist}}\propto(1+\lambda D_+),\qquad \nabla C_s=\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S \quad (\Lambda \text{ constant})
```

The historical causal chain is:

$$D_+\;\to\;\Gamma_{\rm hist}\downarrow\;\to\;C_s\uparrow\;\to\;\chi_S\neq1\;\to\;\mathbf g_\chi$$

```latex
D_+\;\to\;\Gamma_{\rm hist}\downarrow\;\to\;C_s\uparrow\;\to\;\chi_S\neq1\;\to\;\mathbf g_\chi
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Gamma_{\rm hist}$ | The record / dissolution rate in the historical model (not to be confused with the $\Gamma$ of 7.2) |
| $\Gamma_0$ | Reference value |
| $D_+$ | A measure of density / occupancy in the c⁺ direction (historical model quantity) |
| $\lambda$ | Coupling coefficient |
| $C_s$ | Sealing cost (candidate reading) |
| $\Lambda$ | Channel scale coefficient (not the cosmological constant) |
| $\chi_S$ | Channel asymmetry ratio (17.1) |
| $\mathbf g_\chi$ | Channel-sourced candidate acceleration field (8.8) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It proposes a chain tying the sealing cost to c⁺ density, and from there to channel asymmetry |
| **Why does it do it?** | To look, if sealing has a physical price, for whether it leaves a measurable trace in Cₘ |
| **How does it do it?** | It assumes that as density increases the record rate falls, the cost rises, and this can be read as a non-reciprocal channel asymmetry |
| **What result is expected?** | A laboratory-measurable signature $\chi_S\neq1$ |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a candidate reading**; it does not define the time scaffold of 7.2 or the stamp algebra of this section. $D_+$ and $C_s$ are not fundamental quantities of the current core. Whether the $\Gamma$ of the source notation and the $\Gamma$ of 7.2 are two names for the same physical role, or independent quantities, is open. The double-counting debt of 24.2 **does not count as resolved** by this block. The retired criterion "$\nabla\ln\chi_S\neq0$ for dark matter" is not brought back into this chain |

## 14.4 The two-layer archive

| Layer | Structure | Property |
|---|---|---|
| **Raw archive** | Free monoid on records | **Append-only**; full fidelity |
| **Stamp projection** | Coarse projection to $M$ | The idempotent locks are here |

$$\boxed{\;\text{Raw archive} \;\neq\; M\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Raw archive} \;\neq\; M\;}
```

The critical difference is:

$$\kappa^2 = \kappa \quad\text{(in the stamp monoid)} \qquad\qquad A + [r_1] + [r_2] \;\neq\; A + [r_1] \quad\text{(in the raw archive; } \mathrm{damga}(r_1)=\mathrm{damga}(r_2)=\kappa,\ \mathrm{rid}(r_1)\neq\mathrm{rid}(r_2))$$

```latex
\kappa^2 = \kappa \quad\text{(in the stamp monoid)} \qquad\qquad A + [r_1] + [r_2] \;\neq\; A + [r_1] \quad\text{(in the raw archive; } \mathrm{damga}(r_1)=\mathrm{damga}(r_2)=\kappa,\ \mathrm{rid}(r_1)\neq\mathrm{rid}(r_2))
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $A$ | The raw archive (a sequence of records) |
| $[r_1]$, $[r_2]$ | Two one-record sequences carrying the same stamp, with different identities |
| $+$ | Appending (concatenation of sequences) |

**The event source is not collapsed.** Even if the same stamp is stamped twice, two separate records are formed — with two separate identities ($r_1$, $r_2$): in the algebraic image the lock is counted once, but in the raw archive two events remain two events.

**Distinguishing the ambient structure from the valid archive.** The free monoid contains all finite sequences of records, including sequences in which the same identity is repeated. Valid archives are the subset of these without repeated identities, and this subset is not closed under unrestricted concatenation; therefore appending is guarded by a condition:

$$\mathcal A_{\rm geçerli}=\{A\in\mathrm{FreeMonoid}(\mathrm{Record}):\ \mathrm{NoDup}(A.\mathrm{map}\;\mathrm{rid})\},\qquad \mathrm{append}(A,r)\ \text{only if}\ \mathrm{rid}(r)\notin\mathrm{IDs}(A)$$

```latex
\mathcal A_{\rm geçerli}=\{A\in\mathrm{FreeMonoid}(\mathrm{Record}):\ \mathrm{NoDup}(A.\mathrm{map}\;\mathrm{rid})\},\qquad \mathrm{append}(A,r)\ \text{only if}\ \mathrm{rid}(r)\notin\mathrm{IDs}(A)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal A_{\rm geçerli}$ | Raw archives without repeated identities (valid archives) |
| $\mathrm{IDs}(A)$ | The set of record identities in $A$ |
| $\mathrm{append}$ | The guarded append operation |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates the free monoid as the ambient structure, and sequences without repeated identities as valid archives |
| **Why does it do it?** | Mistaking the invariant $\mathrm{NoDup}$ and the free monoid for the same closed structure produced invalid examples such as $[r]+[r]$ |
| **How does it do it?** | It ties appending to the condition of a new identity |
| **What result is expected?** | A formal proof of the uniqueness of `rid` and of `WellFormedArchive` (23.10) |
| **What result is obtained?** | **The type distinction is established**; the algebraic results of the stamp monoid do not change |

### Record structure

$$r = \big(\mathrm{rid},\; \text{edge},\; \text{stamp},\; \text{content range}\big)$$

```latex
r = \big(\mathrm{rid},\; \text{edge},\; \text{stamp},\; \text{content range}\big)
```

- $\mathrm{rid}$ **is unique** and is **not** a physical parameter
- Erasure operations are **not** in the set of operations
- The archive order is **a provenance order**; it is not physical time
- The time label in the Cₘ reading is **not inserted back** into the archive

The time label is **Cₘ provenance information**; c⁺ has no time coordinate of its own ($dt_{c^+}=0$ is a pedagogical reading; $\bot_+$ is the ontological state).

For a well-formed raw archive, the two basic candidate formal invariants are:

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{NoDup}(A.\mathrm{map}\;\mathrm{rid})\;}\qquad\qquad \boxed{\;A\preceq A'\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{NoDup}(A.\mathrm{map}\;\mathrm{rid})\;}\qquad\qquad \boxed{\;A\preceq A'\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathrm{NoDup}$ | No repeated element in the list |
| $A.\mathrm{map}\;\mathrm{rid}$ | The list of identities of all records in the archive |
| $A\preceq A'$ | $A$ is a prefix of $A'$ (append-only order) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes the two basic invariants of a well-formed raw archive |
| **Why does it do it?** | The uniqueness of record identities and the fact that provenance information can only grow are the formal counterpart of the non-erasability of the seal |
| **How does it do it?** | The first invariant requires identities to be non-repeating; the second requires every new archive to contain the old one as a prefix |
| **What result is expected?** | Monotone growth of provenance information without any primitive operation of erasure or undoing |
| **What result is obtained?** | **They are candidate formal invariants**; they are fully consistent with the ontological ruling (the record is fixed) |

### The free-monoid realization of the raw archive

The smallest suitable structure for raw provenance information is:

$$\mathcal A_{\rm raw}=\mathrm{FreeMonoid}(\mathrm{Record}),\qquad \pi_{\rm stamp}:\mathcal A_{\rm raw}\to M$$

```latex
\mathcal A_{\rm raw}=\mathrm{FreeMonoid}(\mathrm{Record}),\qquad \pi_{\rm stamp}:\mathcal A_{\rm raw}\to M
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal A_{\rm raw}$ | The raw archive |
| $\mathrm{FreeMonoid}(\mathrm{Record})$ | The free monoid on records (finite sequences of records) |
| $\pi_{\rm stamp}$ | Stamp projection: coarsely reduces a sequence of records to a stamp word |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It builds the raw archive as the free monoid of records and connects it to the stamp monoid by a projection |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make precise the relation between the full-fidelity record and the coarse algebraic summary |
| **How does it do it?** | The projection reduces each record to its stamp; `rid`, the content range and the multiplicity of events need not be preserved in this reduction |
| **What result is expected?** | That algebraic results are not confused with the meaning of the raw archive |
| **What result is obtained?** | The projection **loses information**; therefore raw archive ≠ $M$ |

Erasure operations are kept out:

```text
erase, pop, undo ∉ Ops(Archive)
```

### Warning against ideal / Rees collapse

Words containing locks may form a class resembling an ideal in the algebra; but collapsing all lock content to a single absorbing zero destroys provenance information. Consequently, the approach "reduce everything locked to a single zero class" is not used in the current formal programme. This distinction prevents the group completion or a quotient result from being mistaken for the physical meaning of the archive.

### Archive count and monotonicity

The raw archive grows only by appending. A definite result follows: when a record is appended to the archive, the number of records increases by exactly one.

$$N_{\rm arşiv}(n+1) \;\geq\; N_{\rm arşiv}(n)$$

```latex
N_{\rm arşiv}(n+1) \;\geq\; N_{\rm arşiv}(n)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $N_{\rm arşiv}(n)$ | The number of records after the $n$-th append step |
| $n$ | Index of provenance order (order of appending) — not physical time |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It states that the number of records in the archive does not decrease in the order of appending |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make precise the counting consequence of an archive without a primitive operation of erasure |
| **How does it do it?** | It shows that each append operation increases the count by exactly one |
| **What result is expected?** | Strict monotonicity in provenance order |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Formally proven**, but it holds in provenance order; it is not a derivative with respect to physical time, and a continuous "archive growth rate" does not follow by itself. The record count in entropy models is a separate model quantity; a decrease in occupancy in a model does not mean that the archive has been erased |

The principle that phase memory is monotone in provenance order ($R_\varphi(r_{n+1}) \geq R_\varphi(r_n)$) has a different status: the use of provenance order instead of physical time is definite, but monotonicity itself is not a formally proven theorem; it is **an architectural rule**. Its status depends on the author's decision.
## 14.5 The correct form of "the whole past is present"

The correct form of the intuition "the whole past is present in c⁺" is **a static relation of compatibility** over the set of records — not a flow. This preserves the intuition of the archive without breaking the principle of timelessness: in the archive there is no "before" and "after"; there are only the provenance order and the relations between records.

## 14.6 The two-layer q/Σ reading

Two separate maps carry different information:

$$q:\mathcal C_{\rm full}\to G_{\rm bare},\qquad\qquad \Sigma:\mathcal C_{\rm full}\to M$$

```latex
q:\mathcal C_{\rm full}\to G_{\rm bare},\qquad\qquad \Sigma:\mathcal C_{\rm full}\to M
```

In the bare layer the reverse direction can be represented; in the stamp monoid, in general, it cannot:

$$\boxed{\;q(T_{ji})=q(T_{ij})^{-1}\;}\qquad\qquad \Sigma(T_{ji})=\Sigma(T_{ij})^{-1}\ \ \text{(CANNOT be written in general)}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;q(T_{ji})=q(T_{ij})^{-1}\;}\qquad\qquad \Sigma(T_{ji})=\Sigma(T_{ij})^{-1}\ \ \text{(CANNOT be written in general)}
```

On the stampless direct edge, the layer of meaning and the algebraic layer are kept separate:

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{emittedStamp}(T_{0+})=\texttt{None},\qquad \Sigma(T_{0+})=e\;}\qquad \boxed{\;\text{NoStamp}\neq \text{NoTransition},\qquad \texttt{None}\neq\texttt{Some}(e)\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{emittedStamp}(T_{0+})=\texttt{None},\qquad \Sigma(T_{0+})=e\;}\qquad \boxed{\;\text{NoStamp}\neq \text{NoTransition},\qquad \texttt{None}\neq\texttt{Some}(e)\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathcal C_{\rm full}$ | The category of transitions with all their information (stamp, provenance, direction) |
| $G_{\rm bare}$ | The bare phase-transition groupoid, in which stamp and provenance information are forgotten |
| $q$ | Forgetful map: gives the bare phase transition |
| $\Sigma$ | Stamp map: the image of the transition in the stamp monoid |
| $T_{ij}$, $T_{ji}$ | The transition from $i$ to $j$ and the one in the reverse direction |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates into two maps the information "from which phase to which" and the information "what it wrote into the archive" |
| **Why does it do it?** | The gate is bidirectional, the stamp unidirectional (13.1); a single map cannot carry both correctly |
| **How does it do it?** | In the bare layer it represents the reverse transition as a group inverse; in the stamp layer, since it is a monoid, it writes no inverse |
| **What result is expected?** | That the bidirectionality of the gate and the unidirectionality of the seal are written together without contradiction |
| **What result is obtained?** | The protective rules hold: **stampless transition ≠ no transition**, **`None` ≠ `Some(e)`** |

## 14.7 The C layer — disentangling the meaning of a transition

In the formal model, the following fields must not be compressed into a single symbol:

$$\boxed{\;\mathfrak C(T)=(Gate,\,EdgeClass,\,Direction,\,StampClass,\,StampEmission,\,SealEffect,\,ArchiveEffect,\,CoordinationEffect,\,ProjectionEffect)\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathfrak C(T)=(Gate,\,EdgeClass,\,Direction,\,StampClass,\,StampEmission,\,SealEffect,\,ArchiveEffect,\,CoordinationEffect,\,ProjectionEffect)\;}
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $Gate$ | Gate (structural permission) |
| $EdgeClass$ | Edge class (Δ₀ₘ, Δ₊ₘ, Δ₀₊) |
| $Direction$ | Direction of transition |
| $StampClass$ | Stamp class (free / lock) |
| $StampEmission$ | Stamp production (`Option Stamp`) |
| $SealEffect$ | Sealing effect |
| $ArchiveEffect$ | Effect on the archive (append / no change) |
| $CoordinationEffect$ | $X$-coordination effect |
| $ProjectionEffect$ | Readout / projection effect in Cₘ |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It divides the meaning of a transition into nine independent fields |
| **Why does it do it?** | Merging these fields into a single symbol would silently identify concepts that are kept separate in the ontology |
| **How does it do it?** | It keeps each field as a separate component; in this way the following identifications are prevented |
| **What result is expected?** | That the formal code preserves the ontological distinctions one to one |
| **What result is obtained?** | The distinction between appending to the archive in the Cₘ → c⁺ direction and readout / projection in the c⁺ → Cₘ direction is a **formal candidate** consistent with the ontology; where the meaning of direction is not separately locked as direct canon, it is not written as a theorem |

```text
phase relation ≠ direction
direction ≠ stamp class
stamp class ≠ stamp emission
stamp emission ≠ sealing
sealing ≠ archive change
archive change ≠ coordination / readout
```

---

## 14.8 Path relations and the cycle character

Under the forgetful map $q$ and the axiom $q(T_{ji})=q(T_{ij})^{-1}$, the following relation for reverse paths may be **a derived result**; it should not be multiplied as an independent physical axiom:

$$q(\gamma_-)=q(\gamma_+)^{-1}$$

```latex
q(\gamma_-)=q(\gamma_+)^{-1}
```

The same inverse cannot be written for the stamp map; $\Sigma(\gamma_-)=\Sigma(\gamma_+)^{-1}$ is in general meaningless, because $M$ is not a group.

On a cycle, order-insensitive additive information can be summarized by the character $\Omega$. Order-sensitive content, by contrast, requires a representation:

$$\rho:M\to\operatorname{End}(V)$$

```latex
\rho:M\to\operatorname{End}(V)
```

If a Cₘ projection model defines a Schnakenberg-like cycle affinity:

$$\mathcal A_{\rm cyc}\neq0$$

```latex
\mathcal A_{\rm cyc}\neq0
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\gamma_+$, $\gamma_-$ | A path and its reverse |
| $\rho$ | A representation of the stamp monoid on a vector space |
| $\operatorname{End}(V)$ | The linear transformations of the vector space $V$ |
| $\mathcal A_{\rm cyc}$ | Cycle affinity (in the Cₘ projection model) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates which information on closed phase cycles is order-insensitive and which is order-sensitive |
| **Why does it do it?** | To show that the trace left in the archive by a cyclic phase path may be too rich to be summarized by a single net number |
| **How does it do it?** | It carries the order-insensitive part with a character and the order-sensitive part with a linear representation |
| **What result is expected?** | A measurable candidate reading of the asymmetry or strength of sealing |
| **What result is obtained?** | If the relevant subsector of $\rho$ is invertible, the language of "holonomy" can be used; for the general monoid, **ordered transport** is safer. $\mathcal A_{\rm cyc}\neq0$ is **a candidate reading** for the asymmetry of sealing; it is not a rate law in the timeless fundamental layer |

## 14.9 The distinction between recording and sealing

Two concepts are disentangled in the archive:

| Concept | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Cₘ trace (recording)** | A process within Cₘ producing a history-carrying, persistent trace that can be read back from Cₘ — a matter of preservability |
| **Sealing** | The irreversible writing of a record into the c⁺ archive — a matter of irreversibility |

$$\boxed{\;\text{A "recoverable trace of the past" can be defined only at the level of the Cₘ trace; sealing alone does not bring back the past.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{A "recoverable trace of the past" can be defined only at the level of the Cₘ trace; sealing alone does not bring back the past.}\;}
```

Critical ruling: sealing is the writing of a record into the c⁺ archive (14.1), and in this sense it requires a record; but it **does not guarantee** leaving a trace readable back within Cₘ, a complete record of the physical state, or the recoverability of the record from Cₘ. Cₘ traces are in most cases partial and selective; trace density ≠ rate of time production. (The phrase "sealing does not require a record" in the previous version was a contradiction arising from the word "record" being used both for the c⁺ archive record and for the Cₘ trace; it has been corrected.)

## 14.10 The stateless universe — recoverable and encoded history

The "stateless universe" record in the archive says this: the condition of the universe is not a state that stores the past; history is preserved in multiple records (traces).

$$\boxed{\;\mathrm{durum}(t) \neq \text{stored history}\quad \text{and}\quad R_\varphi(t+\Delta t)\in[R_\varphi(t),\; R_\varphi(t)+\Delta R_{\max}]\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\mathrm{durum}(t) \neq \text{stored history}\quad \text{and}\quad R_\varphi(t+\Delta t)\in[R_\varphi(t),\; R_\varphi(t)+\Delta R_{\max}]\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\mathrm{durum}(t)$ | The state at time $t$ in Cₘ |
| $R_\varphi$ | Phase memory (14.4) |
| $\Delta R_{\max}$ | The maximum increase of phase memory in one step |
| $t$, $\Delta t$ | Cₘ readout time and step |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates the instantaneous state of the universe from stored history, and writes the bounded monotone increase of phase memory |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make explicit that the assumption "observation automatically preserves the record" is wrong, and that history lives in traces |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes in range form that phase memory does not decrease at any step but does not increase by more than a bounded amount |
| **What result is expected?** | A testable distinction between **encoded history** (the record is only stored) and **recoverable history** (the structural residue can be actively read) |
| **What result is obtained?** | The "recording hypothesis" (that observation automatically preserves the record) **has been rejected**. Evertophy's further claim is recoverable history, and it **has not been proven** |

---

### Equation set — the Ω phase-transition character

The composition law of transitions between phases: the transitions A → B and B → C must give A → C.

$$\omega_{AB} + \omega_{BC} = \omega_{AC}\ \text{(exact)} \qquad \Omega_{AB} \boxplus_B \Omega_{BC} = \Omega_{AC}\ \text{(dependent)} \qquad \Omega_{AC} \subseteq \Omega_{AB} \oplus \Omega_{BC}\ \text{(independent coverage)}$$

```latex
\omega_{AB} + \omega_{BC} = \omega_{AC}\ \text{(exact)} \qquad \Omega_{AB} \boxplus_B \Omega_{BC} = \Omega_{AC}\ \text{(dependent)} \qquad \Omega_{AC} \subseteq \Omega_{AB} \oplus \Omega_{BC}\ \text{(independent coverage)}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\omega_{AB}(s)$ | The exact scalar character at the common state $s$: $\ln W_A(s) - \ln W_B(s)$ |
| $\Omega_{AB}$ | Interval-valued character |
| $\boxplus_B$ | Composition that preserves dependence through the intermediate phase B |
| $\oplus$ | Independent (Minkowski) sum |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes at three different levels how the characters of two consecutive transitions combine |
| **Why does it do it?** | The naive range equality $\Omega_{AB}+\Omega_{BC}=\Omega_{AC}$ is wrong when dependency information is lost |
| **How does it do it?** | Exact additivity at the exact level; equality when dependence is preserved; only inclusion for independent endpoints |
| **What result is expected?** | A consistent composition rule in the Range Formalism |
| **What result is obtained?** | The naive range equality **is wrong**; the three-layer structure (exact / dependent / coverage) **is correct** |



# SECTION 15 — MEASUREMENT AND READOUT

## 15.1 Measurement belongs to Cₘ

$$\boxed{\;\text{DIRECT PHYSICAL MEASUREMENT IS POSSIBLE ONLY IN Cₘ.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{DIRECT PHYSICAL MEASUREMENT IS POSSIBLE ONLY IN Cₘ.}\;}
```

Measurement means the formation of a record, the running of a clock, the click of a detector. All of these require differentiated matter, established time and a metric. In c₀ these are not yet established; in c⁺ they are dissolved. For this reason, everything we know about the outer phases comes from their **readings** in Cₘ.

## 15.1a Phase roles: native, source and observation phase

When speaking of the relation of a quantity to the phases, three different roles must not be confused with one another:

| Role | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Native phase** | The phase in which the quantity is defined in its own language |
| **Source phase** | The phase from which an effect comes |
| **Observation phase** | The phase in which the effect is measured — measurement is always in Cₘ |

This distinction removes a confusion: when a c⁺-sourced effect is observed in Cₘ, the observed value is a value in Cₘ; what is dissolved in c⁺ still remains dissolved. This distinction has been formally encoded with a typed readout structure. The **type** of the readout is established; the **law** of the readout — which c⁺ source gives which value in Cₘ — is, as stated below, an open debt.

## 15.2 The readout map — open debt

The map from the Cₘ state to the measured quantities **is undefined**.

$$\boxed{\;\text{The question is NOT "what is the readout map" but "what is the additional principle that makes it UNIQUE".}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The question is NOT "what is the readout map" but "what is the additional principle that makes it UNIQUE".}\;}
```

This is because such a map cannot be extracted uniquely from observation: different structures can give the same observational result. This is also consistent with the framework's third principle: a representation is valid within the scope in which it reproduces the measurement, and there is no ontological priority among representations.

### Tools of open quantum systems

Tools such as Kraus, Lindblad and Stinespring can be used for this map.

$$\boxed{\;\text{These are POSSIBLE Cₘ MODELS / SCAFFOLDS — they are NOT FUNDAMENTAL ELEMENTS OF THE CORE ONTOLOGY.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{These are POSSIBLE Cₘ MODELS / SCAFFOLDS — they are NOT FUNDAMENTAL ELEMENTS OF THE CORE ONTOLOGY.}\;}
```

The definition of sealing **cannot be reduced** to these tools; sealing is ontological, these tools are a computational language within Cₘ. The completely positive and trace-preserving (CPTP) Kraus model in the archive is a bridge candidate within this limit:

$$\mathcal E(\rho)=\sum_i K_i\rho K_i^\dagger,\qquad \sum_iK_i^\dagger K_i=I$$

```latex
\mathcal E(\rho)=\sum_i K_i\rho K_i^\dagger,\qquad \sum_iK_i^\dagger K_i=I
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\rho$ | The density matrix of the system (a Cₘ quantum state) |
| $\mathcal E$ | Quantum channel (state transformation) |
| $K_i$ | Kraus operators |
| $K_i^\dagger$ | The conjugate transpose of $K_i$ |
| $I$ | The identity operator |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes how a quantum state transforms after interaction with the environment |
| **Why does it do it?** | To provide the computable language in Cₘ of measurement and recording processes |
| **How does it do it?** | It transforms the state by a sum of Kraus operators; the completeness condition preserves total probability |
| **What result is expected?** | A computable model of the Cₘ projection of the sealing event |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a bridge candidate.** "Sealing = transfer of entanglement", and the interpretation of partial loss of trace in the Cₘ projection as a unidirectional history, are **computable model hypotheses**; they are not the definition of sealing or a fundamental element of c⁺. The Hamiltonian / Lindblad language is likewise a candidate only for effective dynamics within Cₘ; it does not import a three-plus-one-dimensional Lagrangian / Hamiltonian structure into c₀ |

## 15.3 Measurement and collapse — the limit of the model

Evertophy may explore reinterpreting measurement in the language of phase, readout and sealing; but it does not claim that the quantum measurement problem has been solved. The chain "liminal regime → Cₘ projection → sealing" is a **model / interpretation candidate**.

Equating quantum uncertainty directly with "the finite resolution of the Cₘ screen" is not the current core; such an interpretation could acquire the status of a physical bridge only if it derived the standard quantum probabilities and the behaviour of the Born rule.

Experiments such as the delayed-choice quantum eraser are treated within this framework in the **compatible reading** mode: the measured results of the experiment are accepted unconditionally; the Evertophy reading offers an interpretation that does not contradict these results, but it does not claim to certify them or to be the only one to explain them.

## 15.4 Sealing cost

The sealing cost, the refresh time and the resolution noise are **conceptual model variables** used in the archive; they are not fundamental elements of the current ontology, and their quantitative definitions have not yet been closed. The sealing cost $C_s$ is, as in 14.3, in the **candidate-reading** mode.

The engineering protocol matured in the archive for experimental bridges is:

|---|---|---|
| 1 | Input phase | $(\Phi,\Xi,X)$ → impedance / phase parameters; ports and normalization are made explicit |
| 2 | Coupling | Input–output transition matrix (CMT) |
| 3 | Asymmetry | $S_{21}/S_{12}\to\chi_S$; a reciprocity check is mandatory (17.1) |
| 4 | Safety | Achromaticity $\partial X/\partial E_{\rm probe} = 0$ (consistency with LHAASO constraints); in discrete modelling, verification with S-parameters |
| 5 | Output phase | Acceleration mapping (draft bridge), the equation below |

$$\mathbf g_{\rm eff} = +c^2\nabla\ln Z_g \quad\text{or}\quad \mathbf g_{\rm eff}=-\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S - (\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda$$

```latex
\mathbf g_{\rm eff} = +c^2\nabla\ln Z_g \quad\text{or}\quad \mathbf g_{\rm eff}=-\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S - (\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda
```

Candidate mechanism for active threshold modulation:

$$\Delta\Phi(t) = \Phi_0\sin(\omega_m t)$$

```latex
\Delta\Phi(t) = \Phi_0\sin(\omega_m t)
```

|---|---|

| $\Delta\Phi(t)$ | Threshold modulation applied from a single port |

| **What does it do?** | It defines a five-step laboratory protocol, from input to output, for testing the channel bridge |
| **Why does it do it?** | So that bridge claims are tied to a test arrangement that is measurable, repeatable and preregistrable |
| **How does it do it?** | It fixes the port normalization, measures scattering in both directions, checks reciprocity, applies the achromaticity constraint and ties the result to an acceleration mapping; it uses time modulation as a candidate reciprocity breaker (band-stop gaps are preferred because they reduce leaky modes) |
| **What result is expected?** | That a measurable, preregistered band is produced when reciprocity is broken |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a test design**, not a prediction |

## 15.6 A physical redefinition of the observer principle

The locked sentence in the archive is:

$$\boxed{\;\text{The observer is not consciousness; it is the matter phase (} C_m \text{).}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The observer is not consciousness; it is the matter phase (} C_m \text{).}\;}
```

This is the archive extension of the core ruling of 1.4: all Cₘ processes create an observer effect; observation is the role of systems that produce physical records and readouts in Cₘ. The concept of consciousness is in no way necessary for this ruling (24.8). Together with the liminal-regime reading: measurement is the mandatory projection of liminal transport onto the Cₘ phase — this is a model candidate, not core.

# SECTION 16 — INFORMATION, ENTROPY, RECORD

## 16.1 The entropy property family and its meaning by phase

Entropy $S(x)$ can be kept as a property family in the Phase-Parameter Matrix; but the language of standard thermodynamic entropy is not carried unchanged into the three phases.

| Phase | Safe interpretation |
|---|---|
| **c₀** | The standard $dS/dt$ language is not admissible; a pre-temporal structural order / potential must be defined separately |
| **Cₘ** | Thermodynamic / statistical entropy and entropy production carry physical meaning |
| **c⁺** | The timeless archive / provenance structure is not identical with the evolution of thermodynamic entropy |

The old "potential / flow / coherent-locked" table may be used only as a pedagogical / model intuition.

## 16.2 Information and phase form

Information is not "lost" — its phase form changes. In c₀, information is potentially preserved in the integrated single structure without being destroyed; in c⁺, it stands as a sealed record.

$$\boxed{\;\text{This is NOT A THEOREM; it is a REINTERPRETATION.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{This is NOT A THEOREM; it is a REINTERPRETATION.}\;}
```

No counting of microstates has been done, and no quantitative relation with the standard black-hole entropy has been established.

## 16.3 The kinds of entropy are separate

| Kind | What |
|---|---|
| Registry entropy | A candidate counting measure defined in the provenance / registry model; it has not been proven to be an ontological root |
| Thermodynamic entropy | A projection within Cₘ |
| Information-theoretic entropy | A projection within Cₘ |

$$\boxed{\;\text{NO RAW EQUALITY HAS BEEN ESTABLISHED among these three; none is the DEFINITION of another.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{NO RAW EQUALITY HAS BEEN ESTABLISHED among these three; none is the DEFINITION of another.}\;}
```

### The directional entropy chain — candidate bridge

The following directional reading is found in the archive:

$$S_{C_m}\uparrow\;\Rightarrow\;\text{clustering / attraction reading},\qquad S_{c^+}\uparrow\;\Rightarrow\;\text{expansion / dark-energy reading}$$

```latex
S_{C_m}\uparrow\;\Rightarrow\;\text{clustering / attraction reading},\qquad S_{c^+}\uparrow\;\Rightarrow\;\text{expansion / dark-energy reading}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $S_{C_m}$ | The entropy measure in Cₘ |
| $S_{c^+}$ | The entropy / dissolution measure in the c⁺ direction (model label) |
| $\uparrow$ | Tendency to increase — it does not mean a time derivative within c⁺ |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It proposes that two entropy tendencies correspond to two different cosmological readings in Cₘ |
| **Why does it do it?** | To test whether clustering and expansion can be read as two directions of the same recording process |
| **How does it do it?** | It maps the increase of entropy in Cₘ to the clustering / attraction reading, and the increase in the c⁺ direction to the expansion reading |
| **What result is expected?** | That the two tendencies are separated quantitatively |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a candidate bridge**; it does not break the definitional distinction of 16.3. $S_{c^+}$ has not been locked as a standard thermodynamic entropy-production variable, and $\uparrow$ does not mean a derivative with respect to a physical time flowing within c⁺. It is not the definition of the Force of Attraction (Postulate 0) |

### Equation set — the K6 entropy model and the distinction of N

This is a **model** that derives an entropy from a record count $N$ and an occupancy ratio $F$:

$$S = k_B\,N\,\frac{F}{F_{\max} - F}, \qquad \frac{dS}{d\tau} = k_B\left[\frac{N' F}{F_{\max}-F} + \frac{N F_{\max} F'}{(F_{\max}-F)^2} - \frac{N F F_{\max}'}{(F_{\max}-F)^2}\right]$$

```latex
S = k_B\,N\,\frac{F}{F_{\max} - F}, \qquad \frac{dS}{d\tau} = k_B\left[\frac{N' F}{F_{\max}-F} + \frac{N F_{\max} F'}{(F_{\max}-F)^2} - \frac{N F F_{\max}'}{(F_{\max}-F)^2}\right]
```

$$N_{\rm arşiv}(n+1) \geq N_{\rm arşiv}(n) \quad \text{(in provenance order — NOT a derivative with respect to physical time)}$$

```latex
N_{\rm arşiv}(n+1) \geq N_{\rm arşiv}(n) \quad \text{(in provenance order — NOT a derivative with respect to physical time)}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $S$ | Model entropy |
| $k_B$ | Boltzmann constant |
| $N$ ($N_{\rm entropi}$) | The record-count quantity of the K6 model |
| $F$, $F_{\max}$ | Occupancy and its upper bound |
| $N'$, $F'$, $F_{\max}'$ | Derivatives with respect to $\tau$ |
| $\tau$ | Model parameter (the scaffold variable of 7.2) |
| $N_{\rm arşiv}(n)$ | The length of the $n$-th prefix of the append-only raw archive |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It computes an entropy and its rate of change from the record count and the occupancy |
| **Why does it do it?** | To look for a testable model bridge between the accumulation of the archive and thermodynamic entropy |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the entropy with a ratio that diverges as the occupancy approaches its upper bound; it expands the derivative by the chain rule |
| **What result is expected?** | An entropy curve that rises rapidly as the occupancy approaches its upper bound |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is the K6 model.** The discrete monotonicity of the archive count **is proven**; but $N_{\rm arşiv}$ and $N_{\rm entropi}$ are not automatically identical. The inference "$F'<0 \Rightarrow$ erasure" **is wrong**: a decrease of occupancy in the model does not mean that the archive has been erased |

## 16.4 Existence ≠ record

$$\boxed{\;\text{The ONTOLOGY "only what is sealed exists" DOES NOT ENTER THE CORE.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The ONTOLOGY "only what is sealed exists" DOES NOT ENTER THE CORE.}\;}
```

Record and sealing are **a provenance / formal layer**. They cannot be identified with existence itself. In particular, since c₀ is pre-temporal **potential**, the statement "unsealed potential is not real" contradicts the framework: in c₀ information is not yet sealed, but it exists.

## 16.5 The threshold of objective record

The occurrence of an event and the formation of its physical record are not the same thing. For an objective record, the information must acquire a sufficient number of independent points of accessibility.

---

## 16.6 Registry entropy — the two-layer structure

A two-layer structure has been proposed in the archive for the registry and information:

| Layer | Structure | Conservation |
|---|---|---|
| **Global** | Unitary evolution + monotonicity of the registry | Information is conserved |
| **Local** | Kraus / CPTP: $\rho' = \sum_i K_i\rho K_i^\dagger$, $\sum K_i^\dagger K_i = I$ | Readout model within Cₘ |

The GKSL / Lindblad template (general form under the Markov approximation):

$$\frac{d\rho}{d\tau} = -\frac{i}{\hbar}[H(\Phi,\Xi),\rho] + \sum_k \gamma_k(\Phi)\left(L_k\rho L_k^\dagger - \tfrac12\{L_k^\dagger L_k,\rho\}\right)$$

```latex
\frac{d\rho}{d\tau} = -\frac{i}{\hbar}[H(\Phi,\Xi),\rho] + \sum_k \gamma_k(\Phi)\left(L_k\rho L_k^\dagger - \tfrac12\{L_k^\dagger L_k,\rho\}\right)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\rho$ | Density matrix |
| $H(\Phi,\Xi)$ | Effective Hamiltonian (a candidate depending on threshold and asymmetry parameters) |
| $[\cdot,\cdot]$ | Commutator |
| $\{\cdot,\cdot\}$ | Anticommutator |
| $L_k$ | Lindblad (jump) operators |
| $\gamma_k(\Phi)$ | Damping rates |
| $\hbar$ | Reduced Planck constant |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes the time evolution of an open quantum system under interaction with the environment |
| **Why does it do it?** | To build the computational language in Cₘ of the duality "globally information is conserved, locally it appears lost" |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the unitary part with a commutator and the effect of the environment with Lindblad terms |
| **What result is expected?** | A measurable model of decoherence once $\{H, L_k, \gamma_k\}$ are chosen |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a computational template**; the choice of $\{H, L_k, \gamma_k\}$ is an open debt and is not a fundamental element of the ontology. The pair "global unitarity + local Kraus" says that the loss of information is observational; it opens a path towards the black-hole information paradox but is not a solution (0.3) |

## 16.7 Derivation of the arrow of entropy — a three-layer summary

For the relation between the arrow of time and entropy, a three-layer working model is preserved in the archive:

1. **Core layer:** the time scaffold — physical time is produced in Cₘ:

$$dt = \Gamma\cdot\mathcal G_{C_m}\cdot d\tau$$

```latex
dt = \Gamma\cdot\mathcal G_{C_m}\cdot d\tau
```

2. **Record layer:** monotonicity of the archive, in provenance order (the same ruling as in 14.4):

$$R_\varphi(r_{n+1}) \ge R_\varphi(r_n)$$

```latex
R_\varphi(r_{n+1}) \ge R_\varphi(r_n)
```

3. **Entropy layer:** only as a thermodynamic projection within Cₘ; the second law is written with the system boundary and the exchange of entropy specified:

$$\frac{dS_{\rm sis}}{dt}=\Phi_S+\sigma,\qquad \sigma\ge0;\qquad \text{isolated system }(\Phi_S=0):\ \frac{dS}{dt}\ge0;\qquad \frac{dS_{\rm sis}}{dt}+\frac{dS_{\rm çevre}}{dt}\ge0$$

```latex
\frac{dS_{\rm sis}}{dt}=\Phi_S+\sigma,\qquad \sigma\ge0;\qquad \text{isolated system }(\Phi_S=0):\ \frac{dS}{dt}\ge0;\qquad \frac{dS_{\rm sis}}{dt}+\frac{dS_{\rm çevre}}{dt}\ge0
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $dt$ | The interval of physical time produced in Cₘ |
| $\Gamma$ | The coefficient of the Cₘ readout / reporting-regulation family (7.2) |
| $\mathcal G_{C_m}$ | The structure of measurable change and differentiation within Cₘ (7.2; an object not yet closed) |
| $d\tau$ | The interval of the scaffold parameter |
| $R_\varphi(r_n)$ | Phase memory after the $n$-th record |
| $S$, $S_{\rm sis}$, $S_{\rm çevre}$ | Thermodynamic entropy; the entropy of the system and of the environment |
| $\Phi_S$ | Net entropy flux into the system (may be negative for an open system) |
| $\sigma$ | Internal entropy production (cannot be negative) |

$$\boxed{\;\text{The core arrow, the accumulation of records and thermodynamic entropy production are NOT the SAME law.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The core arrow, the accumulation of records and thermodynamic entropy production are NOT the SAME law.}\;}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates the direction of time into three separate layers — production, record, entropy |
| **Why does it do it?** | The identity "arrow of time = increase of entropy" would contradict Evertophy's distinction time ≠ entropy ≠ archive order (7.8) |
| **How does it do it?** | It gives each layer its own law and its own domain of validity |
| **What result is expected?** | That in the candidate model it is shown that the arrow of time arises from the recording protocol, while the thermodynamic arrow appears in the statistical Cₘ projection of this record |
| **What result is obtained?** | The three layers are related to one another, but this relation **has not yet been shown**; the layers are not definitions of one another |

## 16.7a Entropy and energy: conservation at the level of Uₓ

**Author's ruling:** Entropy is the state of Cₘ structure dissolved into c⁺. Energy is not annihilated; both the record and the energy share of the dissolving structure are sealed in c⁺.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Total energy is conserved at the level of } U_x \text{; part of the energy cannot be measured in Cₘ — this share is sealed in c⁺.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Total energy is conserved at the level of } U_x \text{; part of the energy cannot be measured in Cₘ — this share is sealed in c⁺.}\;}
```

This ruling distinguishes three separate kinds of energy:

$$E_{U_x}^{\rm muhasebe}\;\supseteq\;\big\{E_{C_m},\;E_+^{\rm mühürlü}\big\}\qquad(\text{distinction of kinds; the quantitative law of summation is open})$$

```latex
E_{U_x}^{\rm muhasebe}\;\supseteq\;\big\{E_{C_m},\;E_+^{\rm mühürlü}\big\}\qquad(\text{distinction of kinds; the quantitative law of summation is open})
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $E_{C_m}$ | The energy measured in Cₘ |
| $E_{U_x}^{\rm muhasebe}$ | The total energy accounting at the level of $U_x$ |
| $E_+^{\rm mühürlü}$ | The share of energy sealed in c⁺ (not a Cₘ-type energy, but a sealed form specific to c⁺) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It states that energy is not lost, but that part of it is sealed in c⁺ and passes outside Cₘ measurement |
| **Why does it do it?** | To unite the increase of entropy and the conservation of energy in a single picture with the archive role of c⁺ |
| **How does it do it?** | It ties conservation not to the time symmetry of Cₘ but to a global accounting principle at the level of $U_x$ |
| **What result is expected?** | That the unmeasurable share becomes evident at cosmological scale; a quantitative map among the three kinds |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is an author's ruling. Measured energy conservation in closed Cₘ systems is **a mandatory external condition** and is satisfied. The quantitative map among the kinds, the common value space and the transfer law **have not yet been established** |

This ruling has four subtleties:

- **It is not a Noether conservation but a global accounting principle.** Energy conservation in Cₘ comes from a symmetry in Cₘ time; there is no global time at the level of $U_x$. For this reason, conservation at the level of $U_x$ is not derived from Cₘ physics but is an ontological postulate, and it does not contradict the ruling "time is valid only in Cₘ".
- **Three separate kinds.** The three energies above belong to different layers of definition and use; this does not mean that their values differ in every case.
- **Agreement with measurements.** Measured energy conservation in closed systems is, within measurement precision, a mandatory external condition. That the unmeasurable share becomes evident at cosmological scale is, by contrast, an expectation not yet calculated; in general relativity, too, energy conservation is not globally defined at cosmological scale. This ruling claims neither that energy is conserved in every Cₘ measurement nor that conservation is violated in any closed system; local conservation, open-system exchange and cosmological global energy are separate scopes.
- **Gate structure.** That a quantity is not on the list of Cₘ-specific quantities does not determine its native definition in other phases, or its transport permission for a particular pair of phases (13.1a). The native status of energy in c⁺ and its transport permission are open.

The formal distinction is also preserved: the discrete monotonicity of the archive count, the monotonicity of phase memory and the increase of thermodynamic entropy do not follow from the same theorem (14, 7.8). An increase in the raw record count is not physical entropy production. The author's ruling establishes an ontological link among these; the quantitative map showing which entropy measure corresponds to which sealing process is open.

# SECTION 17 — CHANNEL ASYMMETRY (CANDIDATE BRIDGE)

## 17.1 The basic idea

The asymmetric behaviour of a medium between two directions can be measured through scattering parameters:

$$\chi_S \;\equiv\; \frac{|S_{21}|}{|S_{12}|}$$

```latex
\chi_S \;\equiv\; \frac{|S_{21}|}{|S_{12}|}
```

The logarithmic measure and the derived field are:

$$\Xi = \ln\chi_S, \qquad \Phi_\chi = \Lambda\ln\chi_S, \qquad \mathbf{g}_\chi = -\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S-(\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda \quad (\text{short form only if } \nabla\Lambda=0)$$

```latex
\Xi = \ln\chi_S, \qquad \Phi_\chi = \Lambda\ln\chi_S, \qquad \mathbf{g}_\chi = -\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S-(\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda \quad (\text{short form only if } \nabla\Lambda=0)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $S_{21}$, $S_{12}$ | The forward (1 → 2) and backward (2 → 1) transmission amplitudes of the scattering matrix |
| $\chi_S$ | Ratio of forward / backward transmission amplitudes |
| $\Xi$ | Logarithmic asymmetry measure |
| $\Lambda$ | Channel scale coefficient (not the cosmological constant) |
| $\Phi_\chi$ | Channel potential (not identified with the historical $\Phi(x)$ of 4.2) |
| $\mathbf g_\chi$ | Channel-sourced candidate acceleration field |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It measures the asymmetry in the two-way transmission of a medium and derives from it a potential and an acceleration field |
| **Why does it do it?** | To look for whether a directional structure between phases has a trace measurable in the laboratory |
| **How does it do it?** | It takes the ratio of the forward and backward amplitudes; it builds a potential by multiplying its logarithm by a scale coefficient; it extracts the acceleration from its gradient. The symmetry condition is automatic: $\chi_S = 1 \Rightarrow \Phi_\chi = 0$ |
| **What result is expected?** | A signal in a reciprocity-breaking system that is measurable and can be compared independently with a gravitational reading |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a candidate bridge.** In every reciprocal system the criterion is identically 1 (see the note below); the fate of the bridge is left to the author's decision. The retired criterion "$\nabla\ln\chi_S\neq0$ for dark matter" is not used |

### Note on reciprocity

This criterion has an important limit. In every **reciprocal** system under the same port normalization ($S_{21} = S_{12} \neq 0$), the ratio is identically 1; losslessness is not required for this — Onsager reciprocity is sufficient. The phase difference $\arg S_{21} - \arg S_{12}$ is also identically zero under the same condition; that is, it is not an automatic backup criterion.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Reciprocal system:}\quad \chi_S \equiv 1,\qquad \Delta\varphi \equiv 0\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Reciprocal system:}\quad \chi_S \equiv 1,\qquad \Delta\varphi \equiv 0\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Delta\varphi$ | $\arg S_{21} - \arg S_{12}$, the phase difference of two-way transmission |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It shows why the channel-asymmetry criterion is empty in reciprocal systems |
| **Why does it do it?** | The earlier canon used $\chi_S$ as a general criterion; Onsager reciprocity invalidates this |
| **How does it do it?** | Since $S_{21}=S_{12}$ under reciprocity, it concludes that both the amplitude ratio and the phase difference are identically constant |
| **What result is expected?** | That the criterion is used only when a **reciprocity-breaking** physical class, port configuration and reference plane are explicitly defined |
| **What result is obtained?** | $\chi_S$ as a general criterion **is wrong**; $\Delta\varphi$ as an automatic backup **is wrong**; the choice of the reciprocity-breaking class is **To Be Addressed Later** (author's decision) |

### 17.1a The family of impedance and potential bridges

The channel-asymmetry line does not consist only of the definition of $\chi_S$. At the model level, the following chain has been investigated:

$$\boxed{\;\chi_S\to Z_{\rm eff}\to\Phi_\chi\to \mathbf g_\chi\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\chi_S\to Z_{\rm eff}\to\Phi_\chi\to \mathbf g_\chi\;}
```

In some CMT / impedance realizations, a link can be established between $Z_{\rm eff}$, or a similar effective channel magnitude, and $\chi_S$. But this chain is **a candidate bridge**, not an ontological law of gravitation. If $\Lambda$ is defined as a product of several factors, measuring only the product does not predict the factors separately; this situation is priced under the parameter and bridge budget (ParamBudget / BridgeBudget).

### Equation set — Z_eff, the canonical directional effective impedance

KAP-03, K5v2 and K12v2 all use $Z_{\rm eff}$; the **canonical definition** of this quantity is given here:

$$Z_{{\rm eff},i}(\omega) \equiv Z_{0,i}\,\frac{1 + S_{ii}(\omega)}{1 - S_{ii}(\omega)}, \qquad \Phi_{\rm ch}^{(\pm)}(\mathbf{x},\omega) \equiv \lambda\ln\frac{Z_{\rm eff}^{(\pm)}(\mathbf{x},\omega)}{Z_{\rm ref}}$$

```latex
Z_{{\rm eff},i}(\omega) \equiv Z_{0,i}\,\frac{1 + S_{ii}(\omega)}{1 - S_{ii}(\omega)}, \qquad \Phi_{\rm ch}^{(\pm)}(\mathbf{x},\omega) \equiv \lambda\ln\frac{Z_{\rm eff}^{(\pm)}(\mathbf{x},\omega)}{Z_{\rm ref}}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $S_{ii}(\omega)$ | The reflection coefficient of port $i$ |
| $Z_{0,i}$ | Port reference impedance |
| $Z_{{\rm eff},i}$ | The effective impedance seen from port $i$ |
| $\Phi_{\rm ch}^{(\pm)}$ | The channel potential for the two directions |
| $\lambda$, $Z_{\rm ref}$ | Scale and reference impedance |
| $\omega$ | Angular frequency |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines the effective impedance from the reflection coefficient, and from it the directional channel potential |
| **Why does it do it?** | A single canonical definition is needed to prevent different models from using the same quantity in different forms |
| **How does it do it?** | It uses the standard reflection–impedance relation of transmission-line theory; it writes the potential as a logarithmic ratio with respect to a reference |
| **What result is expected?** | That the correspondence $\Phi_g\leftrightarrow\Phi_{\rm ch}$ of 8.7 is calibrated through this definition |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a recorded reference definition** (definedness conditions below); the physical bridge is **To Be Addressed Later** |

**Note on complex values and the domain of definition.** The reflection coefficient is in general complex; therefore $Z_{\rm eff}$ and its logarithm may also be complex, and the logarithm is multivalued. The quantity to be compared with a real gravitational potential is **the amplitude potential**; the phase is carried as a separate observable:

$$\Phi_{\rm ch}^{\rm gen}=\lambda\ln\left|\frac{Z_{\rm eff}}{Z_{\rm ref}}\right|,\qquad \phi_{\rm ch}=\arg\frac{Z_{\rm eff}}{Z_{\rm ref}}\ (\text{with the branch choice stated}),\qquad S_{ii}\neq1,\ \ Z_{\rm eff}\neq0$$

```latex
\Phi_{\rm ch}^{\rm gen}=\lambda\ln\left|\frac{Z_{\rm eff}}{Z_{\rm ref}}\right|,\qquad \phi_{\rm ch}=\arg\frac{Z_{\rm eff}}{Z_{\rm ref}}\ (\text{with the branch choice stated}),\qquad S_{ii}\neq1,\ \ Z_{\rm eff}\neq0
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Phi_{\rm ch}^{\rm gen}$ | The real amplitude potential |
| $\phi_{\rm ch}$ | The phase of the impedance ratio |
| $\arg$ | The argument (angle) of a complex number |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It gives the well-defined way of extracting a real potential from a complex impedance |
| **Why does it do it?** | For $S_{ii}=0.2+0.3i$ one gets $\ln Z_{\rm eff}\approx0.370+0.604i$; for $S_{ii}=i/2$, $Z_{\rm eff}=0.6+0.8i$ and $\ln Z_{\rm eff}\approx0.927i$ ($Z_0=Z_{\rm ref}=1$): the direct logarithm does not give a real potential |
| **How does it do it?** | It separates amplitude and phase; it removes the points $S_{ii}=1$ (infinite impedance) and $Z_{\rm eff}=0$ from the domain of definition |
| **What result is expected?** | That the amplitude potential is used in comparison with a gravitational reading; that the phase effect is tested as a separate observable |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a repair of mathematical well-definedness**, not an ontological change. If it is to be read as a gravitational potential, one must have $[\lambda]=\mathrm{m^2\,s^{-2}}$. Which form (amplitude, phase or both) is physical requires separate justification; the $\Phi_{\rm ch}$ of 8.7 is read in this amplitude form |

### 17.1b Operational measurement protocol — model test

If the channel bridge is to be tested, an example vector network analyser (VNA) / CMT protocol is:

1. $S_{21}(\omega)$ and $S_{12}(\omega)$ are recorded with two-way scattering measurements.
2. $\chi_S(\omega)$ is produced under the same instrument and calibration conditions.
3. $\chi_S\simeq1$ is verified in a reciprocal control regime.
4. If there is an active or non-reciprocal component, its source is measured separately; the inference "we saw asymmetry = gravitation" is not made.
5. If the $\Phi_\chi$ or $\mathbf g_\chi$ bridge produces a preregistered band, it is compared with independent data.

Soft kill: if channel asymmetry cannot be tied independently to a physical gravitational reading, **the bridge line closes**, not the core ontology.

### 17.1c The CMT model scaffold

One of the standard coupled-mode forms can be used as a computable model language for channel asymmetry. To avoid confusion with the $\Gamma$ of 7.2, the damping / loss matrix is denoted here by $\Gamma_{\rm CMT}$:

$$S(\omega)=C+D\,[\,i(\omega I-\Omega)+\Gamma_{\rm CMT}\,]^{-1}K^{\mathsf T}$$

```latex
S(\omega)=C+D\,[\,i(\omega I-\Omega)+\Gamma_{\rm CMT}\,]^{-1}K^{\mathsf T}
```

Near a resonance, an approximate reading of asymmetry depending on the chosen port / mode normalization is:

$$\chi\approx\frac{|d_2k_1|}{|d_1k_2|}$$

```latex
\chi\approx\frac{|d_2k_1|}{|d_1k_2|}
```

Here, for $S=C+DRK^{\mathsf T}$, $D=(d_1,d_2)^{\mathsf T}$, $K=(k_1,k_2)^{\mathsf T}$, one has $S_{21}=C_{21}+d_2Rk_1$ and $S_{12}=C_{12}+d_1Rk_2$; the ratio holds when the direct-path components are negligible. If $\Lambda$ in the channel potential depends on position, then $-\nabla\Phi_\chi=-\Lambda\nabla\ln\chi_S-(\ln\chi_S)\nabla\Lambda$; the last term drops only if $\Lambda$ is constant in space. The operational signature of a breaking of reciprocity, and the single-port effective-impedance reading:

$$\boxed{\;S_{21}\neq S_{12}\;}\qquad\qquad Z_{{\rm eff},i}=Z_{0,i}\frac{1+S_{ii}}{1-S_{ii}}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;S_{21}\neq S_{12}\;}\qquad\qquad Z_{{\rm eff},i}=Z_{0,i}\frac{1+S_{ii}}{1-S_{ii}}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $C$ | The direct (non-resonant) path scattering matrix |
| $D$, $K$ | Coupling matrices from modes to ports and from ports to modes |
| $d_1,d_2$, $k_1,k_2$ | Coupling components for a single mode |
| $\Omega$ | The matrix of mode frequencies |
| $\Gamma_{\rm CMT}$ | Damping / loss matrix |
| $R$ | Resonant response $[\,i(\omega-\Omega)+\Gamma_{\rm CMT}]^{-1}$ |
| $I$ | Identity matrix |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It computes the scattering matrix from the couplings of resonant modes and gives the approximate form of the asymmetry ratio near resonance |
| **Why does it do it?** | A computable model is needed to tie channel asymmetry to measurable hardware parameters |
| **How does it do it?** | It uses the standard coupled-mode-theory form; if the direct path is small, it reduces the ratio to a ratio of coupling coefficients |
| **What result is expected?** | That in a reciprocity-breaking structure $S_{21}\neq S_{12}$ is designable and measurable |
| **What result is obtained?** | CMT is the computable **model language** of channel asymmetry; it is not the ontological bridge candidate itself. Matrix dimensions, port normalizations and the notations $C,D,K,\Omega,\Gamma_{\rm CMT}$ depend on the chosen realization. $S_{21}\neq S_{12}$ alone does not count as a verification of gravitation or of Evertophy |
