## 17.4 The χ_S definition set (model candidate)

*The single authority for definitions is 17.1c; for the reciprocity limit see 17.1.* The completed archive form of the definition in 17.1:

$$\chi_S \equiv \frac{|S_{21}|}{|S_{12}|}, \qquad \Xi = \ln\chi_S, \qquad Z_{{\rm eff},i} = Z_{0,i}\frac{1+S_{ii}}{1-S_{ii}}$$

```latex
\chi_S \equiv \frac{|S_{21}|}{|S_{12}|}, \qquad \Xi = \ln\chi_S, \qquad Z_{{\rm eff},i} = Z_{0,i}\frac{1+S_{ii}}{1-S_{ii}}
```

Near resonance, the approximate single-mode reading is $\chi \approx |d_2k_1|/|d_1k_2|$ (when the direct-path components are negligible; dependent on port / mode normalization).

## 17.5 The three-factor decomposition and range-value form

*Status:* The mechanical × coupling × resistance decomposition is a model assignment; without independent measurement and definition of the factors, it is not a derived law.

$$\chi_S = \chi_{\rm mek}\cdot\chi_{\rm bağ}\cdot\chi_{\rm dir},\qquad \chi_S \in [\chi_S^-, \chi_S^+]$$

```latex
\chi_S = \chi_{\rm mek}\cdot\chi_{\rm bağ}\cdot\chi_{\rm dir},\qquad \chi_S \in [\chi_S^-, \chi_S^+]
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\chi_{\rm mek}$ | Mechanical asymmetry contribution |
| $\chi_{\rm bağ}$ | Coupling asymmetry contribution |
| $\chi_{\rm dir}$ | Resistance / loading effect |
| $[\chi_S^-,\chi_S^+]$ | The range value of the asymmetry ratio |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It decomposes the asymmetry ratio into the product of three physical sources and writes it as a range value |
| **Why does it do it?** | To be able to tell from which source a measured asymmetry comes |
| **How does it do it?** | It defines each source as a separate factor; it carries the uncertainty with the Range Formalism |
| **What result is expected?** | That the factors are fixed separately by independent measurements |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a model assignment.** Fixing the product $\Lambda$ while leaving the factors free is forbidden (17.2). The corrected ruling of 21.6 also applies here: a claim of derivation through a range is meaningful only if the bridge has been frozen |

## 17.6 The channel potential

This is the reading, in the language of potentials, of the definition $\Phi_\chi = \Lambda\ln\chi_S$. When reciprocity is not broken, $\Phi_\chi \equiv 0$; the potential is meaningful only when the source of the asymmetry is physically defined (the safeguard of 17.1). $\Phi_\chi$ is not identified with the historical symbol $\Phi(x)$ of 4.2.

## 17.7 Necessary conditions for breaking reciprocity

The local statement, on this line, of the classes of mechanism in 8.8 is: in a linear medium satisfying the reciprocity conditions (containing no magnetic bias or time modulation), $S_{21} = S_{12}$; passivity alone does not guarantee this. $\chi_S \neq 1$ does not require an active element; a concrete reciprocity-breaking physical class (for example, static magnetic bias) is sufficient. The inference "we saw asymmetry = gravitation" is forbidden (17.1b).

## 17.8 The principle of the inversion of causality

The candidate principle in the archive is: the source of a directional asymmetry observed in a channel is not "a weakening of causality" but the projection of the structural priorities of the channel. The directional reading refers not to the source but to the carrier. This principle is at the model level; it adds no ontological interpretation of causality.

## 17.9 The χ_S ≈ 137 hypothesis — status and red-flag record

In the archive, an attempt was made to relate $\chi_S$ to the fine-structure constant. The recorded status:

$$\boxed{\;\text{The } \chi_S \approx 137 \text{ hypothesis is not taken into the canon until the fitting–derivation distinction is resolved.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The } \chi_S \approx 137 \text{ hypothesis is not taken into the canon until the fitting–derivation distinction is resolved.}\;}
```

| Red flag | Why |
|---|---|
| Exogeneity of source | The scale coefficient $\Lambda$ is taken from outside |
| Factor indeterminacy | Product fixed, factors free |
| Absence of asymmetry | In a reciprocal system $\chi_S \equiv 1$ — a testable instance of the hypothesis is undefined |

This hypothesis produces no counter increment; unless it is turned into a preregistered bridge test, it remains **an open debt**.

# SECTION 18 — QUANTUM PHENOMENA

## 18.0 Quantum behaviour and the phase regime–threshold boundary

The subatomic level lies on **the phase regime–threshold boundary** between c⁺ and Cₘ — just like light on the $v=c$ boundary. By the author's ruling: a structure on the boundary of the c threshold regime is at the same time on the boundary of the c⁺ regime. This is not a contradiction; lying on **the shared boundary** of two regimes does not mean being a member of both (boundary ≠ membership).

$$\boxed{\;v=c\;\text{: the liminal shared boundary of Cₘ and } c^+\qquad \text{lying on the boundary}\neq\text{being a member of two phases}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;v=c\;\text{: the liminal shared boundary of Cₘ and } c^+\qquad \text{lying on the boundary}\neq\text{being a member of two phases}\;}
```

There is no time, and no time-dependent constraint, in c⁺. For this reason the concept of time becomes indeterminate for a structure on the boundary: seen from the Cₘ side, time runs; on the c⁺ side, it is not established. Wave–particle duality, the measurement problem and the apparent inconsistency of quantum behaviour with time are read as behaviours of this boundary; measurement, by contrast, always takes place in Cₘ.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It reads the oddities of quantum behaviour as consequences of lying on the shared boundary of c⁺ and Cₘ |
| **Why does it do it?** | To connect quantum behaviours that appear inconsistent with time (delayed choice, entanglement) with the ontology in which time is established only in Cₘ |
| **How does it do it?** | It places the subatomic level on the phase regime–threshold boundary (25.1); the similarity with light is that both are boundary phenomena — not the same boundary parameter (the two axes below); it keeps separate the state of time on the two sides of the boundary (established in Cₘ, absent in c⁺) |
| **What result is expected?** | That Born probabilities, interference, Bell correlations, contextuality and no-signalling are recovered in the same model |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is **a reading**; it does not touch the measured predictions of quantum mechanics. For it to become a physical explanation, the recoveries above are required, and they **are open** |

**Two axes: kinematic membership and the phase boundary.** Massive subatomic particles such as the electron, the proton and the neutrino are kinematically in the band $0<v<c$ and **are members of Cₘ**. That they lie "on the phase regime–threshold boundary" does not mean that they lie on the kinematic boundary $v=c$; it is a boundary defined on a separate axis — the axis of phase coherence / quantum readout. The kinematic boundary $v=c$ is reserved for light (massless particles) only:

$$\beta=\frac{v_{\rm üyelik}}{c}\in(0,1)\ \ (\text{kinematic membership axis}),\qquad q_\partial\ \ (\text{phase boundary / quantum readout axis}),\qquad \text{light: }\beta=1$$

```latex
\beta=\frac{v_{\rm üyelik}}{c}\in(0,1)\ \ (\text{kinematic membership axis}),\qquad q_\partial\ \ (\text{phase boundary / quantum readout axis}),\qquad \text{light: }\beta=1
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\beta$ | Dimensionless kinematic membership reading |
| $v_{\rm üyelik}$ | Membership reading (not the laboratory coordinate speed; 3.3) |
| $q_\partial$ | Reading of proximity to the phase regime–threshold boundary (its type and measure are open) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes the Cₘ membership of massive subatomic particles and their quantum boundary behaviour on two separate axes |
| **Why does it do it?** | If the expression "the subatomic level lies on the same boundary as light" were read literally, it would contradict the $0<v<c$ membership of massive particles |
| **How does it do it?** | It defines the kinematic boundary on the $\beta$ axis and the phase regime–threshold boundary on the $q_\partial$ axis; a particle can be a member of Cₘ with $\beta<1$ while showing boundary behaviour with respect to $q_\partial$ |
| **What result is expected?** | That the type and measure of $q_\partial$ are defined |
| **What result is obtained?** | The author's ruling (lying on the boundary, boundary ≠ membership) **is preserved unchanged**; only which boundary is meant is made explicit. $q_\partial$ is open (APPENDIX K.1) |

As an example, **neutrino oscillation** — the change of flavour of neutrinos during travel — is a robustly measured phenomenon and a candidate for the reading of lying on the phase-threshold boundary. The 2011 measurement claiming that neutrinos travel faster than light was withdrawn as a measurement error; Evertophy does not rely on that result.

## 18.1 Entanglement

$$\boxed{\;c^+ \text{ IS NOT A COMMUNICATION CHANNEL.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;c^+ \text{ IS NOT A COMMUNICATION CHANNEL.}\;}
```

This is the critical constraint that preserves the prohibition of signalling. The correlation of two entangled particles can be interpreted as a reading of c⁺ in Cₘ; but no controllable information can be sent through this correlation.

**The reading of quantum teleportation.** In quantum teleportation experiments, the transfer of a state necessarily requires that measurement results be transmitted through **a classical channel** (without exceeding the speed of light). In the Evertophy reading, c⁺ is **the source of the correlation** of the transfer; it is not the channel that carries the information. Information travels through the classical channel of Cₘ, at most at the speed of light. This reading is **a compatible reading** of the measured results of the experiments and does not overstep the prohibition of signalling.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Teleportation} = \underbrace{c^+\text{ source of correlation}}_{\text{reading}} + \underbrace{\text{classical channel}}_{C_m,\ \le c}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Teleportation} = \underbrace{c^+\text{ source of correlation}}_{\text{reading}} + \underbrace{\text{classical channel}}_{C_m,\ \le c}\;}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It limits the role of c⁺ in entanglement and teleportation to that of a source of correlation |
| **Why does it do it?** | Giving c⁺ the role of a signal channel would contradict the measured prohibition of signalling of relativity and quantum mechanics |
| **How does it do it?** | It separates the transfer into two components: the correlation read from c⁺, and the classical information channel in Cₘ |
| **What result is expected?** | That Bell correlations and no-signalling are recovered quantitatively in the same model |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a compatible reading.** Honest record: Evertophy **does not explain** entanglement; it proposes a reading framework |

## 18.2 The phase dictionary

The following correspondences are **historical / conceptual analogies or model candidates** — they are not core ontology and not derivations:

| Phenomenon | Phase reading |
|---|---|
| Spin | The state label of a structural node on Cₘ |
| Pauli exclusion | An address-collision barrier in the recording protocol |
| Quantum Zeno | Continuous measurement freezes the rhythm of sealing |
| Tunnelling | Loosening of the record, crossing of the barrier, resealing |
| Delayed choice | Consistency of the sealing decision independent of the time index |
| Uncertainty | The finite resolution of the Cₘ reading |

$$\boxed{\;\text{None of them produces a PREDICTION DIFFERENT from standard quantum mechanics.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{None of them produces a PREDICTION DIFFERENT from standard quantum mechanics.}\;}
```

## 18.3 The status of quantum field theory

$$\boxed{\;\text{Quantum field theory is NOT AN ALTERNATIVE to Evertophy; it is the EFFECTIVE COMPUTATIONAL LIMIT in the Cₘ phase.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Quantum field theory is NOT AN ALTERNATIVE to Evertophy; it is the EFFECTIVE COMPUTATIONAL LIMIT in the Cₘ phase.}\;}
```

This is the direct application of the framework's third principle: a mathematical representation that correctly reproduces a measurement is accepted unconditionally within the scope of that measurement; there is no ontological priority among representations. The vacuum state of quantum field theory is likewise the computational language within Cₘ of the identity c⁺ ≡ vacuum (Section 3).

### The rule of structural separation

$$\boxed{\;\text{The main text is PURE PHASE ONTOLOGY. The tools of quantum field theory are an ADDITIONAL LAYER that TRANSLATES Cₘ calculations.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The main text is PURE PHASE ONTOLOGY. The tools of quantum field theory are an ADDITIONAL LAYER that TRANSLATES Cₘ calculations.}\;}
```

It is proposed that the parameters of quantum field theory may in principle depend on phase; the form of these dependences is not known.

## 18.4 The production of matter from the vacuum

Calculations of particle production on a changing background assume **a given metric**; in Evertophy geometry is an output of Cₘ.

$$\boxed{\;\text{These tools may be used WITHIN Cₘ; they CANNOT BE APPLIED to the } c_0 \to C_m \text{ transition.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{These tools may be used WITHIN Cₘ; they CANNOT BE APPLIED to the } c_0 \to C_m \text{ transition.}\;}
```

Under the identity c⁺ ≡ vacuum, the production of particles from the vacuum by a strong field (the Schwinger mechanism), or pair production from high-energy photons, is read as **a local phase transition triggered by external energy on the c⁺ → Cₘ edge** (candidate reading). All measured and calculated results remain fully valid as the Cₘ computational language of quantum field theory.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It reads the production of matter from the vacuum as a phase transition under the identity c⁺ ≡ vacuum |
| **Why does it do it?** | If the vacuum is the laboratory reading of c⁺, the birth of matter from the vacuum is the appearance in Cₘ of a transition from c⁺ to Cₘ |
| **How does it do it?** | It leaves the calculation to quantum field theory; it adds only the ontological label (the Δ₊ₘ edge, triggering by external energy) |
| **What result is expected?** | One-to-one agreement with the standard results for threshold fields and production rates |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a candidate reading**; it produces no new numerical prediction. The same tools are not applied to the c₀ → Cₘ transition, because in c₀ the metric is not yet established |

## 18.5 Laboratory analogues

Bose–Einstein condensation (BEC) and superfluidity are laboratory examples of **collective and coherent behaviour**.

**Author's ruling:** The BEC is not c₀; **it is the environment in the matter phase that is closest to the c₀ environment.** The gathering of atoms into a single quantum state is the best way of looking from within Cₘ at the c₀ intuition of an integrated single structure; but the BEC is still in Cₘ: it has time, a metric and a measurable particle structure.

$$\boxed{\;\text{BEC}\neq c_0;\qquad \text{BEC = the environment in the matter phase closest to the } c_0 \text{ environment}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{BEC}\neq c_0;\qquad \text{BEC = the environment in the matter phase closest to the } c_0 \text{ environment}\;}
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{The correspondence superfluid} \to c^+ \text{ is NOT USED.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The correspondence superfluid} \to c^+ \text{ is NOT USED.}\;}
```

The reason is this: the distinctive property of c⁺ is not coherence but **the dissolution of Cₘ matter support**.

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It puts laboratory condensates in the right place in the phase architecture |
| **Why does it do it?** | Identifying the BEC with c₀ would be a category error; but cutting it off completely from the c₀ intuition would lose an instructive bridge |
| **How does it do it?** | It keeps the BEC as a member of Cₘ; it positions it as "the Cₘ environment closest" to c₀; it forbids mapping the superfluid onto c⁺ |
| **What result is expected?** | That BEC experiments provide a qualitative testing ground for the c₀ single-structure intuition |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is an author's ruling; the "closeness" of the BEC to c₀ has not been defined quantitatively |

## 18.6 Write protection in entanglement

The candidate reading in the archive: entanglement is modelled as a "write-protected correlation store" that is effective in c⁺.

| Concept | Evertophy reading |
|---|---|
| Sealing | Writing |
| Recall | Reading |
| Entanglement | A write-protected correlation store effective in c⁺ |
| Signalling | Blocked because of the families of recall |

The sentence "distant correlations can be recalled but not changed" is a model candidate; it is consistent with the prohibition of signalling, but not derived from it.

## 18.7 Jitter = a candidate information carrier

The proposal that time jitter at the Planck scale may be an information carrier is preserved in the archive:

$$\Delta t \sim \sqrt{\frac{G\hbar}{c^5}} \approx 5.39\times10^{-44}\ \mathrm{s}$$

```latex
\Delta t \sim \sqrt{\frac{G\hbar}{c^5}} \approx 5.39\times10^{-44}\ \mathrm{s}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Delta t$ | The jitter scale of the order of the Planck time |
| $G$, $\hbar$, $c$ | Gravitational constant, reduced Planck constant, speed of light |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It gives the smallest meaningful scale of Cₘ time as the Planck time and counts it as a candidate information carrier |
| **Why does it do it?** | To ask, on the view that time is a reading established in Cₘ, whether this reading has a resolution limit |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds the time scale from the fundamental constants by dimensional analysis |
| **What result is expected?** | That the jitter budget is related to information capacity |
| **What result is obtained?** | The archive notes (an upper bound on the jitter budget of ~$10^{-44}$ s; corresponding frequency ~$10^{44}\ \mathrm{s^{-1}}$) are kept as **a record of information capacity**. It has not been shown that the jitter really has this character, and it vanishes by itself under CMT linearization |

## 18.8 The commit / rollback ontology

A commit / rollback language for quantum superposition: superposition is "an uncommitted draft area", measurement is the "commit" operation.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Superposition = uncommitted draft area; measurement = commit. Rollback does not take place in Cₘ after the commit.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Superposition = uncommitted draft area; measurement = commit. Rollback does not take place in Cₘ after the commit.}\;}
```

This is a model candidate — an import from the language of computing, not an ontological declaration.

## 18.9 Entanglement swapping — chained entanglement

The archive reading of entanglement swapping is "a chain of commits". Correlation cannot be transferred without the mediation of a Bell measurement; this reading is consistent with the prohibition of signalling and does not overstep it. The whole family of analogies treated as "c⁺ = network, EPR = short circuit, entanglement = write-protected correlation" is at the model level.

## 18.10 The three-stage nested double slit — a physical model

The physical model in the archive for a double-slit arrangement with an intermediate layer: the level $\varphi_b$ is tied to c⁺; the interference pattern arises from the correlation $d\Phi(x)$. The candidate statement of the resolution of timelessness is: "c⁺ is persistent; Cₘ time is derived; superposition is intra-phase persistence." This is **a compatible reading**, within the framework, of delayed-choice experiments — not a derivation but a candidate interpretation, and it does not derive the Born rule.

## 18.11 The reading of quantum uncertainty as imaging noise

The candidate reading of the uncertainty principle as "the finite-resolution noise of the Cₘ reading" has the same status as the constraint of 15.3: unless it derives the standard probabilities and the Born rule, it does not count as a physical bridge. The uncertainty relations are preserved in their stated forms:

$$\Delta x\,\Delta p \;\ge\; \frac{\hbar}{2},\qquad \sigma_x\,\sigma_p \;\ge\; \frac{\hbar}{2}$$

```latex
\Delta x\,\Delta p \;\ge\; \frac{\hbar}{2},\qquad \sigma_x\,\sigma_p \;\ge\; \frac{\hbar}{2}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Delta x$, $\Delta p$ | Uncertainties of position and momentum |
| $\sigma_x$, $\sigma_p$ | Standard deviations of position and momentum |
| $\hbar$ | Reduced Planck constant |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It proposes reading quantum uncertainty as the finite resolution of the Cₘ screen |
| **Why does it do it?** | To relate uncertainty naturally to the fact that measurement belongs to Cₘ |
| **How does it do it?** | It preserves the standard relations as they are; it adds a reading only in the interpretive layer |
| **What result is expected?** | That the reading derives the Born rule and the standard probabilities |
| **What result is obtained?** | The relations are measured physics and are accepted unconditionally; the reading is **a model candidate**, not the current core |

## 18.12 Note of comparison with interpretations of quantum mechanics

| Interpretation | Relation to Evertophy |
|---|---|
| **Copenhagen** | Parallel emphasis on measurement; the "observer" is not an ontological class (15.6) |
| **Many worlds** | The archive / branch language is not confused with c⁺; c⁺ is not a many-worlds structure |
| **Pilot wave (Bohm)** | The hidden-variable programme is not imported into the core |
| **QBism** | No separate heading has been opened owing to insufficient source support; preserved at the level of a note |
| **Relational quantum mechanics (RQM)** | Parallel interaction-centred language of measurement (7.11d) |

$$\boxed{\;\text{Evertophy chooses no interpretation; it repositions measurement in the language of phase / readout / sealing (15.3).}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Evertophy chooses no interpretation; it repositions measurement in the language of phase / readout / sealing (15.3).}\;}
```

# SECTION 19 — TOPOLOGY

## 19.1 The current topology

$$\boxed{\;\text{Complete bidirectional triangle: } c_0 \leftrightarrow C_m,\; C_m \leftrightarrow c^+,\; c_0 \leftrightarrow c^+\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Complete bidirectional triangle: } c_0 \leftrightarrow C_m,\; C_m \leftrightarrow c^+,\; c_0 \leftrightarrow c^+\;}
```

Each of the three phases is directly connected to the other two (K3). Two of the edges touch Cₘ and carry stamps (Δ₀ₘ, Δ₊ₘ); the third (Δ₀₊) does not touch Cₘ and is stampless. Cₘ is **the epistemic centre** — we measure everything from there —, but it is not a topologically mandatory intermediary: there is a direct edge between c₀ and c⁺ that does not pass through Cₘ.

## 19.1a The complete triangle is independent of dimension

That the phase gates form a complete triangle is a **type** property; it does not depend on a particular geometry. A counterexample makes this clear: wells placed only on a line form a chain and cannot give a complete triangle; but on a circle, a potential such as $V(\theta) = -A\cos 3\theta$ gives a complete triangle in one dimension. Consequently, the generalization "at least two dimensions are required for a complete triangle" is wrong and has been withdrawn.

Two-dimensional realizations are at the **model** level. In the ideal equilateral Gaussian landscape, as the well width $\sigma$ increases, the structure passes through three regimes: complete triangle, central star and single node. The Euclidean structure of the readout space is a Cₘ representation; it attributes no Euclidean structure to c₀ or c⁺. The claim that the disk realization is "canonical" or "unique" has also been withdrawn.

### Equation set — phase topology: Morse landscape and the complete triangle

It is canon that the transitions among the three phases form **a complete triangle** (K3). The question is: how is this structure realized in a potential landscape, and how many dimensions are needed?

$$\chi = m - s + M \quad (\text{disk: } 3 - 3 + 1 = 1;\ \ S^1: 3 - 3 = 0)$$

```latex
\chi = m - s + M \quad (\text{disk: } 3 - 3 + 1 = 1;\ \ S^1: 3 - 3 = 0)
```

$$V(\theta) = -A\cos 3\theta,\quad \theta \in S^1 \quad \text{(counterexample)}$$

```latex
V(\theta) = -A\cos 3\theta,\quad \theta \in S^1 \quad \text{(counterexample)}
```

$$V(\mathbf{x}) = -A\sum_{i=1}^{3}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{v}_i|^2}{2\sigma^2}\Big),\qquad \mathbf{v}_1 = (0,1),\ \ \mathbf{v}_{2,3} = \big(\mp\tfrac{\sqrt3}{2}, -\tfrac12\big)$$

```latex
V(\mathbf{x}) = -A\sum_{i=1}^{3}\exp\!\Big(-\frac{|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{v}_i|^2}{2\sigma^2}\Big),\qquad \mathbf{v}_1 = (0,1),\ \ \mathbf{v}_{2,3} = \big(\mp\tfrac{\sqrt3}{2}, -\tfrac12\big)
```

$$H(0) = \frac{3A(2\sigma^2 - 1)}{2\sigma^4}\,e^{-1/2\sigma^2}\,I \;\Rightarrow\; \sigma^* = \tfrac{1}{\sqrt2},\qquad \sigma_{\rm fold} \approx 0.7391346294$$

```latex
H(0) = \frac{3A(2\sigma^2 - 1)}{2\sigma^4}\,e^{-1/2\sigma^2}\,I \;\Rightarrow\; \sigma^* = \tfrac{1}{\sqrt2},\qquad \sigma_{\rm fold} \approx 0.7391346294
```

| $\sigma$ regime | $(m,s,M)$ | Adjacency graph |
|---|---|---|
| $0 < \sigma < 1/\sqrt2$ | (3, 3, 1) | $K_3$ |
| $1/\sqrt2 < \sigma < \sigma_{\rm fold}$ | (4, 3, 0) | $K_{1,3}$ |
| $\sigma > \sigma_{\rm fold}$ | (1, 0, 0) | Single node |

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $V$ | The potential in readout space |
| $\theta$ | The angular coordinate on the circle |
| $(m, s, M)$ | Numbers of minima, saddles and maxima |
| $\chi$ | Euler characteristic |
| $K_3$, $K_{1,3}$ | Complete triangle; one centre + three arms (star) |
| $\mathbf v_i$ | The vertices of an equilateral triangle (on the unit circle) |
| $\sigma$ | Width of the Gaussian wells |
| $A$ | Well depth ($A > 0$) |
| $H(0)$ | The Hessian matrix of the potential at the centre |
| $\sigma^*$ | The threshold at which the centre turns from a maximum into a minimum |
| $\sigma_{\rm fold}$ | The fold threshold at which the saddles disappear |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It computes how the complete-triangle phase topology can be realized in a potential landscape, and in which parameter range it breaks down |
| **Why does it do it?** | To test generalizations such as "at least two dimensions are needed for a complete triangle" and "the disk is the only canonical realization" |
| **How does it do it?** | With Morse theory it ties the numbers of critical points to the Euler characteristic; it builds a one-dimensional counterexample on the circle; in two dimensions it computes analytically the Hessian of the Gaussian wells at the centre |
| **What result is expected?** | Exact values of the regime thresholds and the independence of the complete triangle from geometry |
| **What result is obtained?** | The typed gate K3 **is proven** (★). The circle, disk and Gaussian realizations are **models**; the ideal Gaussian regimes **are correct** (model). The full range certificate **is partial**. The claims "$d_{\min}=2$", the canonicity of the disk and "Morse-7" **are wrong** and have been withdrawn |

## 19.2 Additional topological structures are not imported

$$\boxed{\;\text{Extra dimensions, torsion structures, Chern–Simons terms, supersymmetry, a multiverse — NONE of them is imported into the core.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Extra dimensions, torsion structures, Chern–Simons terms, supersymmetry, a multiverse — NONE of them is imported into the core.}\;}
```

If these are needed, they **must be justified separately**, and their parameter cost must be counted explicitly. This is also consistent with the ruling of three-dimensional flat Euclidean space in 9.2a: there is no extra dimension in the base space of Cₘ.

## 19.3 A known structural constraint

In a structure with a single independent cycle, the image of representations is cyclic — and hence commutative. This means that the whole of a non-commutative gauge group cannot be generated from the bare cycle. On the other hand, a unitary holonomy **can be assigned** to the single cycle: for every unitary $U$, $\rho(n)=U^n$ is a representation. For example:

$$U_{jk}=\frac{\omega^{jk}}{\sqrt3},\quad \omega=e^{2\pi i/3},\ \ j,k=0,1,2;\qquad J=\operatorname{Im}\!\big(U_{00}U_{11}U_{01}^*U_{10}^*\big)=\frac{\sqrt3}{18}\neq0$$

```latex
U_{jk}=\frac{\omega^{jk}}{\sqrt3},\quad \omega=e^{2\pi i/3},\ \ j,k=0,1,2;\qquad J=\operatorname{Im}\!\big(U_{00}U_{11}U_{01}^*U_{10}^*\big)=\frac{\sqrt3}{18}\neq0
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\rho(n)=U^n$ | The representation corresponding to going around the single cycle $n$ times |
| $U$ | Unitary matrix (example of the 3×3 discrete Fourier matrix) |
| $\omega$ | The third root of unity |
| $J$ | A Jarlskog-like phase invariant (measure of CP violation) |
| $^*$ | Complex conjugate |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It shows the limit that single-cycle phase topology places on gauge and mixing structures, and the possibility it leaves open |
| **Why does it do it?** | To test the claim that the CKM / PMNS mixing matrices would come "by themselves" out of the phase triangle |
| **How does it do it?** | It shows that the representations of the singly generated fundamental group are cyclic; it nevertheless computes, with a concrete example, that a unitary holonomy can be assigned and that this can give a non-zero CP phase |
| **What result is expected?** | That the additional physical structure that selects particular mixing values is found |
| **What result is obtained?** | The correct ruling is: **the bare phase topology does not by itself select particular CKM / PMNS mixing values; additional physical structure is required for this selection.** The single-cycle holonomy approach is open as an alternative and awaits physical derivation |

$$\boxed{\;\text{This is a STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINT — if additional structure is imported, a PARAMETER COST arises.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{This is a STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINT — if additional structure is imported, a PARAMETER COST arises.}\;}
```

## 19.4 Order sensitivity

The effect accumulated along a cycle can be measured with two different objects: an order-insensitive scalar character and an order-sensitive ordered transport. Abelianization **erases** order information. Since stamps are not invertible, the name "holonomy" is reserved for the invertible sector only; the general case is **ordered transport** (14.8).

## 19.5 The homology of the bare triangle

If the three-edge structure is taken only as a boundary graph:

$$V=3,\qquad E=3,\qquad \beta_1=E-V+1=1,\qquad \boxed{\;H_1\cong\mathbb Z,\qquad \pi_1\simeq\mathbb Z\;}$$

```latex
V=3,\qquad E=3,\qquad \beta_1=E-V+1=1,\qquad \boxed{\;H_1\cong\mathbb Z,\qquad \pi_1\simeq\mathbb Z\;}
```

If the triangle is **filled** as a 2-simplex:

$$\boxed{\;H_1=0\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;H_1=0\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $V$, $E$ | Number of vertices (phases) and edges (not a potential in this context) |
| $\beta_1$ | The first Betti number (number of independent cycles) |
| $H_1$ | The first homology group |
| $\pi_1$ | The fundamental group |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It computes the topological invariants of the phase triangle for two different formalizations |
| **Why does it do it?** | To show that the cycle programme (holonomy, mixing) depends on whether the interior of the triangle is regarded as empty or filled |
| **How does it do it?** | In the boundary graph it finds a single independent cycle; in the filled simplex it shows that the cycle is contractible and the homology vanishes |
| **What result is expected?** | That the question "boundary or filled simplex?" is closed by a separate formal decision |
| **What result is obtained?** | The image of every representation of the singly generated fundamental group is cyclic; the bare single cycle does not by itself generate a non-commutative gauge structure and does not select particular mixing values. Assigning a unitary holonomy to the single cycle is possible (19.3). The choice does not change the ontological existence of the three edges |

## 19.6 Torsion and the cost of additional structure

In the bare boundary triangle, $H_1\cong\mathbb Z$ is torsion-free. Consequently, a $\mathbb Z_k$ condition such as

$$W^k=I$$

```latex
W^k=I
```

does not come free from the topology; it imports additional cells, relations or representation structure and incurs a parameter / bridge budget cost. Here $W$ is the cycle holonomy, $k$ an integer and $I$ the identity matrix.

## 19.7 Character and order-sensitive representation

Order-insensitive additive information can be kept with the abelianized character. For order-sensitive content, a representation such as

$$\rho:M\to \operatorname{End}(V)$$

```latex
\rho:M\to \operatorname{End}(V)
```

is needed. If $\rho(M)$ lies in an invertible subsector, the language of holonomy can be used; for the general monoid effect, "ordered transport" is the more accurate term.

# SECTION 20 — THE STANDARD MODEL AND GENERAL RELATIVITY

## 20.1 Aim and present status

The aim is to turn the constants of the Standard Model from arbitrary inputs into solutions of the phase structure.

$$\boxed{\;\text{NO DERIVED number or narrow range HAS BEEN PRODUCED for any Standard Model constant.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{NO DERIVED number or narrow range HAS BEEN PRODUCED for any Standard Model constant.}\;}
```

## 20.2 Assignment of mechanism ≠ derivation

$$\boxed{\;\text{Saying "this constant corresponds to such-and-such a phase event" does not show that the event PRODUCES the number.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Saying "this constant corresponds to such-and-such a phase event" does not show that the event PRODUCES the number.}\;}
```

If this distinction is not preserved, the framework appears to have "explained" everything while predicting nothing.

## 20.3 Two different problems

| Problem | Question |
|---|---|
| **A — Absolute value** | Why exactly this number? |
| **B — Dynamics** | How does this quantity change with conditions? |

$$\boxed{\;\text{At present Evertophy has made progress ONLY on B.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{At present Evertophy has made progress ONLY on B.}\;}
```

## 20.4 Three machine programmes

Aim: **few inputs → many observables**.

| Machine | Chain | Target |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — Kinetic** | $X$, edges → gauge kinetic coefficients → couplings | Gauge sector |
| **2 — Potential** | $X$, edges → effective potential → scalar sector | Higgs-like quantities |
| **3 — Yukawa** | $X$, edges → Yukawa structures → masses and mixings | Fermion sector |

### Target value ≠ prediction

$$\boxed{\;\text{WRITING a TARGET VALUE is NOT PREDICTING it.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{WRITING a TARGET VALUE is NOT PREDICTING it.}\;}
```

Listing an observational value as "a target to be reached" is legitimate; presenting it as "a prediction" is **not**. The real question is always: *why this value?*

### Kill tests

| Machine | Kill condition |
|---|---|
| 1 | If the electroweak consistency relations cannot be reproduced at tree level |
| 1 | If the known coefficients of the running of the couplings cannot be reproduced |
| 3 | If the mixing matrix is freely fitted — it re-enters covertly as a parameter |

### CKM and single-cycle holonomy

$$\boxed{\;\text{The bare phase topology does not select particular CKM / PMNS values; single-cycle holonomy is an alternative and awaits physical derivation (19.3).}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The bare phase topology does not select particular CKM / PMNS values; single-cycle holonomy is an alternative and awaits physical derivation (19.3).}\;}
```

The living hypothesis is a relative sector structure; but if it is freely fitted, the parameter budget is violated.

### Details of the machines — programme targets only

The research chain for the kinetic machine, the scalar machine and the Yukawa machine can be written as follows:

$$X,\Delta \longrightarrow \{Z_B,Z_W,Z_G\}\longrightarrow\{\alpha_{\rm EM},\sin^2\theta_W,\alpha_s\}$$

```latex
X,\Delta \longrightarrow \{Z_B,Z_W,Z_G\}\longrightarrow\{\alpha_{\rm EM},\sin^2\theta_W,\alpha_s\}
```

$$X,\Delta \longrightarrow V_{\rm eff}\longrightarrow\{v,\ m_H/v,\ \Gamma_{\rm vac}\}$$

```latex
X,\Delta \longrightarrow V_{\rm eff}\longrightarrow\{v,\ m_H/v,\ \Gamma_{\rm vac}\}
```

$$X,\Delta\longrightarrow\{Y_u,Y_d,Y_e,Y_\nu\}\longrightarrow\{\text{masses, CKM, PMNS}\}$$

```latex
X,\Delta\longrightarrow\{Y_u,Y_d,Y_e,Y_\nu\}\longrightarrow\{\text{masses, CKM, PMNS}\}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $X$, $\Delta$ | The dynamic meta-resolver and the phase edges |
| $Z_B$, $Z_W$, $Z_G$ | Kinetic coefficients of the hypercharge, weak-isospin and colour gauge fields |
| $\alpha_{\rm EM}$ | Fine-structure constant |
| $\theta_W$ | Weak mixing (Weinberg) angle |
| $\alpha_s$ | Strong coupling constant |
| $V_{\rm eff}$ | Effective scalar potential |
| $v$ | Higgs vacuum expectation value |
| $m_H$ | Higgs mass |
| $\Gamma_{\rm vac}$ | Vacuum decay rate |
| $Y_u, Y_d, Y_e, Y_\nu$ | Yukawa matrices of up quarks, down quarks, charged leptons and neutrinos |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines three research chains from the phase structure to observables for the three sectors of the Standard Model |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make concrete and testable the aim of producing many measured values from few inputs |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes each chain through intermediate quantities and defines kill tests for each |
| **What result is expected?** | That the measured couplings, masses and mixings come out without free fitting |
| **What result is obtained?** | **They are programme targets**; they do not enter the current list of numerical predictions. No chain has yet been closed |

### Record of an effective potential with three minima — candidate

The historical development records contain a model candidate that represents the three phase values as effective minima:

$$V(C)=\beta(C-C_0)^2(C-C_m)^2(C-C_+)^2,\qquad \Box C+V'(C)=J_{\rm int}$$

```latex
V(C)=\beta(C-C_0)^2(C-C_m)^2(C-C_+)^2,\qquad \Box C+V'(C)=J_{\rm int}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $C$ | Effective order-parameter field |
| $C_0$, $C_m$, $C_+$ | The effective minimum values of the three phases |
| $\beta$ | Potential depth coefficient |
| $\Box$ | The d'Alembert operator (only in the Cₘ effective-field realization) |
| $V'(C)$ | The derivative of the potential with respect to $C$ |
| $J_{\rm int}$ | Internal source term |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It represents the three phases as three minima of a single effective field |
| **Why does it do it?** | To try modelling phase transitions in the familiar language of field theory (transitions between minima) |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds a sixth-order potential with three minima and writes the field equation with a source term |
| **What result is expected?** | Testable transition dynamics, if $\beta$, $C_i$ and $J_{\rm int}$ are derived |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is not fundamental phase ontology.** The use of $\Box$ belongs to the Cₘ effective-field realization and does not insert a metric or tetrad into c₀ / c⁺. Without derived correspondences, this form produces no prediction |

## 20.4a Sharper kill tests for the three machines

### Machine 1 — kinetic / gauge sector

An example candidate parametrization is:

$$\sin^2\theta_W\stackrel{\rm aday}{=}\frac{Z_W}{Z_W+Z_B}$$

```latex
\sin^2\theta_W\stackrel{\rm aday}{=}\frac{Z_W}{Z_W+Z_B}
```

The value of this form lies not in substituting the measured $\sin^2\theta_W$, but in being able to show **why a particular ratio $Z_W/Z_B$ necessarily comes out**. One of the comparison targets used for Machine 1 in the archive is:

$$\sin^2\theta_W^{\rm eff}(M_Z)\approx0.23149(13)$$

```latex
\sin^2\theta_W^{\rm eff}(M_Z)\approx0.23149(13)
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{measured comparison value}\neq\text{Evertophy prediction}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{measured comparison value}\neq\text{Evertophy prediction}\;}
```

Before publication or numerical comparison, the **scheme, notation and external source** used must be verified separately. One of the smallest electroweak consistency targets is the recovery of the tree-level limit:

$$\rho_{\rm EW}=\frac{m_W^2}{m_Z^2\cos^2\theta_W}\simeq1$$

```latex
\rho_{\rm EW}=\frac{m_W^2}{m_Z^2\cos^2\theta_W}\simeq1
```

If the structure of the neutral mass matrix and the consistency of custodial symmetry cannot be derived, one does not say "the electroweak derivation is complete". In the strong sector, merely saying "$\alpha_s$ decreases with energy" is insufficient; in the perturbative limit, the known one-loop structure coefficient must be recovered from the correct effective limit:

$$b_0=11-\frac{2}{3}n_f$$

```latex
b_0=11-\frac{2}{3}n_f
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\sin^2\theta_W^{\rm eff}(M_Z)$ | The effective weak mixing angle at the Z-mass scale (measured) |
| $M_Z$, $m_Z$ | Z-boson mass |
| $m_W$ | W-boson mass |
| $\rho_{\rm EW}$ | The electroweak $\rho$ parameter |
| $b_0$ | The one-loop beta-function coefficient of the strong coupling |
| $n_f$ | The number of effective quark flavours |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It makes numerically precise the testable targets and kill criteria for the gauge sector |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make it a rule that the claim "the electroweak structure has been explained" can be made only if the known consistency relations are recovered |
| **How does it do it?** | It puts side by side the candidate parametrization, the measured value, the tree-level $\rho$ relation and the one-loop running coefficient |
| **What result is expected?** | That the ratio $Z_W/Z_B$ comes out necessarily from the phase structure; that $\rho_{\rm EW}\simeq1$ and $b_0$ are recovered |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a programme target.** The numbers are measured comparison values, not Evertophy predictions |

### Machine 2 — effective potential / scalar sector

Listing a measured ratio or coupling as a target is legitimate; **a target value is not a prediction**. Adding a free potential function, moreover, can mean infinite functional freedom and is therefore written into the bridge budget.

### Machine 3 — Yukawa / flavour sector

The automatic production of the CKM matrix as a non-commutative holonomy by the single cycle of the phase triangle is excluded. The living formal candidate is the relative transport of two sectors:

$$V_{\rm mix}\stackrel{\rm aday}{=}H_u^\dagger H_d$$

```latex
V_{\rm mix}\stackrel{\rm aday}{=}H_u^\dagger H_d
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $V_{\rm mix}$ | Mixing matrix (CKM or PMNS) |
| $H_u$, $H_d$ | The unitary transformations that diagonalize the up and down sectors |
| $^\dagger$ | Conjugate transpose |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes the mixing matrix as the relative position of the diagonalizing transformations of the two sectors |
| **Why does it do it?** | In the Standard Model mixing arises exactly in this form; Evertophy would need to produce it from the phase structure |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the transport of the two sectors separately and defines the relative transformation as the mixing |
| **What result is expected?** | That $H_u$ and $H_d$ come out of the phase structure tied to each other; that a Jarlskog-type invariant is recovered |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a candidate form.** If $H_u$ and $H_d$ are fitted freely and independently, the mixing matrix covertly re-enters as an input. For CP violation, the phase of the determinant alone is not sufficient |

### Note on strong CP

Making $\theta_{\rm QCD}$ small by adding an exponential suppression parameter moves the fine tuning to another symbol if the suppression scale is free. A strong solution must produce a structural / topological necessity instead of adding new fine tuning. This is **a programme target**, not an existing solution.

## 20.5 Relation to general relativity

General relativity **is not wrong** — it is the effective language of Cₘ. But:

$$\boxed{\;\text{The action used by Evertophy is NOT A FUNDAMENTAL ACTION — it is the EFFECTIVE action of Cₘ.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The action used by Evertophy is NOT A FUNDAMENTAL ACTION — it is the EFFECTIVE action of Cₘ.}\;}
```

## 20.6 What must be recovered

| Target | Status |
|---|---|
| Newtonian limit, weak field | Accessible through the scalar–tensor language |
| FRW cosmology | Accessible |
| Quantum formalism | No bridge |
| Gauge structure | No bridge; there is a structural constraint |
| Second law | Partly established |

## 20.7 Inactive lines of derivation

For neutrino masses, the baryon asymmetry, the full Yukawa matrices and the absolute fermion masses, **no effective and natural derivation bridge has been established** today. For this reason they are not at the centre of the current programme of numerical derivation. This statement is not a permanent ontological prohibition; a new bridge can be opened only with a parameter / bridge budget cost and independent kill tests.

---

## 20.8 The electron = plasma cloud model (historical)

In an early layer, treating the electron as a "plasma cloud" was tried. In the current core, particles are Cₘ readout projections (10.3); this model is not brought back. The record is preserved to show the variety of models in the archive.

## 20.9 The proton mass (QCD) — a literature classification record

Most of the proton mass comes from quantum chromodynamics (QCD); the direct contribution of the Higgs mechanism through the quark masses is small. Exact percentages depend on the decomposition method and are not used without a source. This is a literature record external to the framework:

$$\boxed{\;\text{Mass is mostly dynamical; this fact does not mean that Evertophy has carried out a derivation of mass.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Mass is mostly dynamical; this fact does not mean that Evertophy has carried out a derivation of mass.}\;}
```

The proton mass has been predicted from lattice QCD calculations; this is the yardstick of work the framework cannot yet do — not of work it has done.

## 20.10 The "texture knot" and the question of deriving SU(3)

Reading colour confinement in the language of phase constraints (25.8) is a strong candidate; but whether the SU(3) gauge structure itself can be derived from the phase topology is open. The term "texture knot" was used in the archive to name this question; it is not an ontological term.

## 20.11 The question of three generations and the mass hierarchy

The question whether the three-generation structure and the fermion mass hierarchy can be derived from the phase structure is open. The measured hierarchy is very wide: the mass ratio of the top quark to the electron is about $3.4\times10^{5}$, and its ratio to the heaviest neutrino is of order $10^{12}$; since oscillation data place a **lower bound** on the mass of the heaviest neutrino, they bound this ratio **from above**.

$$\frac{m_t}{m_e}\approx\frac{172.7\ \mathrm{GeV}}{0.511\ \mathrm{MeV}}\approx3.4\times10^{5},\qquad m_{\nu,\max}\gtrsim0.05\ \mathrm{eV}\ \Rightarrow\ \frac{m_t}{m_{\nu,\max}}\lesssim\frac{172.7\ \mathrm{GeV}}{0.05\ \mathrm{eV}}\approx3.5\times10^{12}$$

```latex
\frac{m_t}{m_e}\approx\frac{172.7\ \mathrm{GeV}}{0.511\ \mathrm{MeV}}\approx3.4\times10^{5},\qquad m_{\nu,\max}\gtrsim0.05\ \mathrm{eV}\ \Rightarrow\ \frac{m_t}{m_{\nu,\max}}\lesssim\frac{172.7\ \mathrm{GeV}}{0.05\ \mathrm{eV}}\approx3.5\times10^{12}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $m_t$ | Top-quark mass |
| $m_e$ | Electron mass |
| $m_{\nu,\max}$ | The mass of the heaviest neutrino, $\max(m_{\nu_1},m_{\nu_2},m_{\nu_3})$ (unknown). In the normal ordering it is $\nu_3$, in the inverted ordering $\nu_2$; the bound holds in both orderings |
| $0.05\ \mathrm{eV}$ | The lower bound from oscillation data (atmospheric mass splitting): $m_{\nu,\max}\gtrsim\sqrt{\vert\Delta m^2_{\rm atm}\vert}\approx0.05$ eV |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It sets out numerically the size of the mass hierarchy that must be explained |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make explicit which range a claim of derivation must cover |
| **How does it do it?** | It takes ratios of measured masses |
| **What result is expected?** | That three generations and these ratios come out of the phase structure |
| **What result is obtained?** | The numbers are measured context, not predictions. The archive proposal of factors varying for different density levels (1, 20, 1836; 1, ~200, 3500) is preserved as a table record — a numerical match is not a derivation. The order-of-magnitude notation "~10⁵⁵–10¹⁷" in minor12 has been editorially corrected because it was inconsistent with the measured mass ratios |

## 20.12 The question of agreement with QED process amplitudes

Whether Feynman diagrams can be reread within the framework under the condition of agreement with measured process amplitudes is an open question. This question is one of the unclosed ends of the machine programme (20.4).

# SECTION 21 — EMPIRICAL STATUS AND AUDIT

## 21.1 The closed line

For a time, the framework's strongest empirical claim was the comparison of data from different physical domains through percentile alignment. Very high correlation values had been obtained.

**This line has been permanently closed by internal audit.**

### Three root errors

| # | Error | Repairable? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The data were sorted by value, not by time | Yes |
| 2 | **There is no physical common key** | **No — structural** |
| 3 | The permutation test tested the wrong question | Yes |

### Structural consequence

The operations of sorting and pseudo-pairing can produce a strong visual and correlation structure even when there is no physical common key. For this reason a high correlation **by itself** is not evidence of a common, hidden physical coordinate.

In the audit, the corrected null / permutation run did not support the claim in question. In the archive transfer record, $p\approx0.530$ (FAILED) is reported for the corrected run; this number has the status **reported in the archive** and awaits independent final confirmation from the raw-run source. The structural consequence does not change: the multi-domain Xa / Xz / Xt line **has been permanently removed from the status of empirical evidence**.

### Prohibition of language

$$\boxed{\;\text{For this line, the expressions "proven", "locked" and high-sigma CANNOT BE USED. Retired constants CANNOT BE BROUGHT BACK.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{For this line, the expressions "proven", "locked" and high-sigma CANNOT BE USED. Retired constants CANNOT BE BROUGHT BACK.}\;}
```

## 21.2 Change of direction

$$\boxed{\;\text{NOT "LOOK FOR correlation in the data" — but "DERIVE A RANGE, then COMPARE".}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{NOT "LOOK FOR correlation in the data" — but "DERIVE A RANGE, then COMPARE".}\;}
```

The five-step protocol is:

| # | Step |
|---|---|
| 1 | Choose a single target quantity that has data and units |
| 2 | Write the chain and **count the free parameters** |
| 3 | Derive the range — narrower than the prior |
| 4 | **PREREGISTER — without looking at the data** |
| 5 | Compare |

The closed line lost precisely at the fourth step.

### The present primary domain of verification

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The primary active domain is NOT multi-domain correlation; it is the "derive a range, then compare" protocols.}\;}
```

The envelope modulation of nuclear binding energy is the first concrete candidate application of this protocol (Section 22).

## 21.3 The provenance of data must be sealed

$$\boxed{\;\text{"We sealed it in advance" IS INSUFFICIENT BY ITSELF. The DATA LINEAGE must also be sealed.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{"We sealed it in advance" IS INSUFFICIENT BY ITSELF. The DATA LINEAGE must also be sealed.}\;}
```

What must be sealed: the records that determine the parameters producing the prediction · the target selection · the data version · the held-out test set, if any. Otherwise one obtains a test that "looks blind" but is in fact not blind.

## 21.5 Tools of falsification

### Quick-rejection thresholds

A **claim of support or prediction** is **rejected immediately** in the following cases (the refutation of the model or of the model family is a separate ruling, 21.7):

- If the derived range does not intersect the observation range
- If the statistical significance of the claimed effect cannot be established in a blind test (the claim is rejected; this does not mean the model is refuted)
- If it gives a result that erases or shifts known structure (such as measured shell positions)
- If a coefficient was added after the data were seen
- If an active constraint is violated

### Early failure S1 — a candidate protocol extracted from the archive

The following thresholds are neither ontological postulates nor Evertophy predictions; they are preserved as **methodological candidates** for model runs to be preregistered in the future:

| Check | Candidate decision rule |
|---|---|
| Background fit | $\chi^2_{\rm red}\le1.2$ acceptance region; $1.2$–$1.5$ warning; $>1.5$ hard rejection |
| Growth of structure | $\Delta\chi^2_{\rm growth}\le0$ expected |
| Stability | Ghost / gradient / tachyon pathology → immediate rejection |
| Effective Planck mass | $M_*^2\le0$ → rejection |
| Clock / constant cross-check | Inconsistency with $\Delta\alpha$ → rejection |
| Window stability | $\Delta\chi^2\le3$ between windows; $>5$ hard brake |
| Cost of freedom | If the improvement per new parameter is $<2$, inflation warning |
| Effective-field-theory interpretation | If $\alpha_M$ is left free rather than derived, it counts as a post-general-relativity threshold signal |
| Strong field | EHT / gravitational-wave results are reported not in the language of "horizon" but, within the scope in which they are verified, in the language of "near-horizon" |

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\chi^2_{\rm red}$ | Chi-square divided by the degrees of freedom (reduced goodness-of-fit measure) |
| $\Delta\chi^2_{\rm growth}$ | The chi-square difference relative to the reference model in growth-of-structure data |
| $M_*^2$ | The square of the effective Planck mass |
| $\Delta\alpha$ | The relative variation of the fine-structure constant |
| $\alpha_M$ | The running rate of the effective Planck mass (an effective-field-theory parameter) |

These numbers are not canonical thresholds unless preregistered. For the same reason, the threshold $r_{\rm global}<0.5$ in the archive is also **a methodological candidate**; it cannot be used as a rescue limit after the data have been seen.

Three observational protocols are kept in the research programme: multi-probe simultaneity (clustering of BAO / $f\sigma_8$ / lensing residual breaks around the same $z$), the departure of a phase-wall sigmoid form from smooth CPL, and masked locality. These are **not predictions but designs of falsification bridges**; unless the six conditions of 0.4 are satisfied, they do not change the 0/10 counter.

### Warning on broad canonical improvement — model note

A multimodal posterior distribution is by itself neither a pathology nor a sign that the model is wrong. A verdict of pathology requires additional criteria such as non-identifiability, branch selection, post-data improvement, instability or physically inadmissible modes. This note gives no licence to "select and report the best mode"; the prohibition of branch-shopping and worst-case reporting apply unchanged.

### The intersection measure

The overlap ratio of the prediction and observation ranges can be used as a criterion.

$$\boxed{\;\text{This criterion is NOT a MODEL-RESCUE tool; it is part of falsification.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{This criterion is NOT a MODEL-RESCUE tool; it is part of falsification.}\;}
```

### Worst-case reporting

$$\boxed{\;\text{Reporting the fit ONLY WITH THE BEST CASE IS FORBIDDEN. The fit is reported together with the WORST admissible case; for family rejection the best member (inf) is used.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Reporting the fit ONLY WITH THE BEST CASE IS FORBIDDEN. The fit is reported together with the WORST admissible case; for family rejection the best member (inf) is used.}\;}
```

If the improvement of fit per new parameter is below a certain threshold, the model **is buying freedom**.

### Families of kill switches

| Family | Example |
|---|---|
| **Ontology** | Using $U_0$ as a fourth phase → rejection |
| **Ontology** | Attributing a metric directly to c⁺ → hard contradiction |
| **Notation** | Using the historical kernel in place of the current $X$ → rejection |
| **Data** | Data leakage → publication kill |
| **Prediction** | Intersection measure persistently low → rejection of the prediction |
| **Overfitting** | Range inflation → kill |
| **Bridge** | If the bridge line is not supported → soft kill |
| **Tool** | A tool declaring its own output canon → rejection of the output |

## 21.6 The range intersection measure and the discipline of calibration

To quantify the overlap of two ranges, an IOM-like measure can be used:

$$\mathrm{IOM}(B_{\rm pred},B_{\rm obs})=\frac{\mu(B_{\rm pred}\cap B_{\rm obs})}{\mu(B_{\rm pred}\cup B_{\rm obs})},\qquad \mu(B_{\rm pred}\cup B_{\rm obs})>0$$

```latex
\mathrm{IOM}(B_{\rm pred},B_{\rm obs})=\frac{\mu(B_{\rm pred}\cap B_{\rm obs})}{\mu(B_{\rm pred}\cup B_{\rm obs})},\qquad \mu(B_{\rm pred}\cup B_{\rm obs})>0
```

$$\mathrm{Compat}(B_{\rm pred},B_{\rm obs})=\mathbf 1\big[B_{\rm pred}\cap B_{\rm obs}\neq\varnothing\big]\qquad(\text{for every band; point ranges included})$$

```latex
\mathrm{Compat}(B_{\rm pred},B_{\rm obs})=\mathbf 1\big[B_{\rm pred}\cap B_{\rm obs}\neq\varnothing\big]\qquad(\text{for every band; point ranges included})
```

The criterion of hard rejection is:

$$\text{Hard rejection} \iff B_{\rm pred} \cap B_{\rm obs} = \varnothing$$

```latex
\text{Hard rejection} \iff B_{\rm pred} \cap B_{\rm obs} = \varnothing
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| IOM | Range-overlap measure (width-based Jaccard ratio); defined only when the width of the union is positive |
| $\mu$ | The chosen width measure (length of a range) |
| $\mathrm{Compat}$ | Compatibility indicator: 1 if the intersection is non-empty, 0 if it is empty |
| $\mathbf 1[\cdot]$ | Indicator function |
| $B_{\rm pred}$, $B_{\rm obs}$ | Prediction and observation bands |
| $\vert\cdot\vert$ | Band width according to the chosen measure |
| $\cap$, $\cup$ | Intersection and union |
| $[P,P]$ | Zero-width (point) range |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It measures how much the prediction and observation bands overlap, and defines the condition of hard rejection |
| **Why does it do it?** | To decide by a numerical and predetermined rule whether a model is to be rejected |
| **How does it do it?** | It divides the width of the intersection by the width of the union; it ties rejection only to the intersection being empty |
| **What result is expected?** | A diagnostic score of agreement for bands of positive width; definite rejection for disjoint bands |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Hard rejection holds only for an empty intersection** (★ proven). Hard rejection by IOM **is wrong**: if a zero-width prediction $[P,P]$ lies inside the observation band, IOM still comes out 0, but the prediction **is not excluded**. The statement "rejected if IOM = 0" of the earlier version has therefore been corrected |

**Point ranges.** When both bands are of the form $[P,P]$, numerator and denominator are zero and IOM is undefined as $0/0$. In this case only the compatibility indicator $\mathrm{Compat}$ is used; $[P,P]$ remains a fully valid Range value. The criterion of hard rejection ($\mathrm{Compat}=0$, that is, an empty intersection) is written in the same way for all band types.

**Band types.** Whether an empty intersection means definite rejection depends on the type of the bands. For guaranteed-coverage bands ($B_{\rm kesin}$), an empty intersection is a logical rejection; for confidence regions ($B_{1-\alpha}$) and Bayesian credible regions ($B_{\rm inanç}$), an empty intersection is reported as **rejection under the stated uncertainty model and test level**. For example, an empty intersection with an observation band built from the 16–84% quantiles is not automatically a definite falsification. The band type is specified in the preregistration.

This number **is not a universal law of physics**. In the archive, practical or draft thresholds such as 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.8 are seen in different runs; in addition, in the historical cosmological standard of agreement, $\mathrm{IOM}\ge0.7$ and $\chi^2_{\rm range}\le1.2$ were recorded as **locked run thresholds**. The value 0.7 is not a universal ontological constant; the final threshold must be calibrated according to the data type, the error model and the preregistered test protocol. The basic meaning does not change:

- A high IOM shows only agreement; it is not verification.
- Increasing the IOM by widening the prediction band afterwards is **overfitting**.

## 21.7 Worst-case agreement and the cost of freedom

A interval-valued model must not be reported only through its "best point". Agreement is also evaluated with the **worst admissible** behaviour of the preregistered band. If adding a new parameter gives a small improvement in a $\chi^2$ or likelihood criterion, the improvement must exceed the cost of model freedom. The rules of "$\Delta\chi^2$ per new parameter" used in the archive are **methodological candidates**, not universal thresholds.

### Equation set — KAP-09: family rejection and range χ²

$$\chi^2(\theta) = \sum_i\left[\frac{y_i - f_i(\theta)}{\sigma_i}\right]^2, \qquad \text{family rejection} \iff \inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\chi^2(\theta) > T, \qquad d\big(y, [l,h]\big) = \max(l - y,\ 0,\ y - h)$$

```latex
\chi^2(\theta) = \sum_i\left[\frac{y_i - f_i(\theta)}{\sigma_i}\right]^2, \qquad \text{family rejection} \iff \inf_{\theta\in\Theta}\chi^2(\theta) > T, \qquad d\big(y, [l,h]\big) = \max(l - y,\ 0,\ y - h)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $y_i$ | The $i$-th observed value |
| $f_i(\theta)$ | The model's prediction for the $i$-th observation with parameters $\theta$ |
| $\sigma_i$ | The uncertainty of the $i$-th observation |
| $\theta$, $\Theta$ | The common parameter vector and its permitted set |
| $\inf$ | Infimum (the best fit of the family) |
| $T$ | Rejection threshold |
| $d(y,[l,h])$ | The distance of a point to the band $[l,h]$ (zero if inside the band) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It defines when a model family as a whole is to be rejected, and gives the distance of a point to a band |
| **Why does it do it?** | To avoid the wrong rule by which the failure of a single member rejects the whole family |
| **How does it do it?** | It rejects the family only if even its best member exceeds the threshold; it measures band distance as the amount lying outside the range |
| **What result is expected?** | That model families are evaluated with a fair and robust rule |
| **What result is obtained?** | For family rejection, **the inf rule is correct**; **the sup rule is wrong** |

### Family rejection and worst-case agreement

The failure of a single parameter choice in a model family is not the failure of the whole family. **Best-case agreement** matters for whether the family can be rejected as a whole; **worst-case agreement** matters for robustness against parameter uncertainty. The two can be reported together, but they do not replace each other; the worst-case discipline of 21.5 does not break the criterion of family rejection. The absence of a statistically significant result is not automatic refutation; and agreement with data is written not as "the prediction is correct" but as **"not excluded in the stated test"**.

## 21.8 Classes of kill switches

| Class | What it kills | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Ontological hard kill | Violation of core type | Making $U_0$ a fourth phase; assigning a metric to c⁺ |
| Bridge soft kill | The bridge line | If $\chi_S$ is not tied to gravitation, the channel bridge closes |
| Data kill | The validity of the test | Data leakage / wrong lineage |
| Method kill | The statistical protocol | Wrong null model, pseudo-pairing |
| Overfitting kill | The claim of prediction | Post-data band inflation / adding coefficients |
| Publication kill | The status of the claim | Presenting a non-blind result as a blind prediction |
| Tool / AI kill | The source | A tool declaring its own output "evidence" |

The kill switch does not destroy the core ontology with every failed bridge; it states explicitly **which layer has been rejected**.

## 21.9 Classes of error — F / C / R

Kill results do not carry the same epistemic weight:

| Class | Meaning | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **F — Fatal** | A structural error that stops the core programme | The programme does not advance until the relevant fundamental structure is closed |
| **C — Correctable** | A repairable error in a coefficient, limit, scale or similar | A revision is made; the programme continues |
| **R — Retractable** | A particular realization / window / route is withdrawn | The theory does not automatically die; route information is produced |

The historical F class is recorded with four core examples: direct contradiction from the same assumption; unjustified violation of causality; a fundamental concept remaining mathematically undefined; the retraction / early-failure mechanism itself not working. This list does not mean that **every failed experiment is F**.

$$\boxed{\;\text{EARLY FAILURE}\neq\text{WHOLESALE FAILURE}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{EARLY FAILURE}\neq\text{WHOLESALE FAILURE}\;}
```

The withdrawal of the old Xa / Xz / Xt empirical line, and the failure of particular bridge tests, are read at the level of **class R** unless shown otherwise. Nor does an R failure mean that the core ontology has been experimentally verified.

## 21.10 Time-series evidence protocol v0.5

The recorded protocol for time-series analysis has four stages: normalization → residual extraction → cross-correlation → white-noise test. How a record-sealing signature would be tested with this protocol is an open research question; the numerical thresholds of the protocol have not yet been defined in the source.

## 21.11 Real Pantheon+ H(z) analysis — Evertophy and ΛCDM

A historical run recorded in the source compared the Evertophy reading with ΛCDM on $H(z)$ data:

$$\chi^2_{\min}=204.7\quad(\gamma=+0.328,\ n=300),\qquad \chi^2/\text{dof}=0.685,\qquad \Delta\chi^2=+2.7$$

```latex
\chi^2_{\min}=204.7\quad(\gamma=+0.328,\ n=300),\qquad \chi^2/\text{dof}=0.685,\qquad \Delta\chi^2=+2.7
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\chi^2_{\min}$ | Chi-square at the best fit |
| $\gamma$ | The Evertophy readout parameter in the run |
| $n$ | Number of data points |
| dof | Degrees of freedom |
| $\Delta\chi^2$ | Chi-square difference between Evertophy and ΛCDM (positive: not in favour of Evertophy) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It tests whether the Evertophy reading fits expansion-history data better than ΛCDM |
| **Why does it do it?** | To compare a cosmological reading claim with the standard model on the same data |
| **How does it do it?** | It computes the chi-square fit for both models and takes the difference |
| **What result is expected?** | A distinctive difference in a preregistered run |
| **What result is obtained?** | In this run **there is no advantage in favour of Evertophy.** The record carries its own proxy warnings: Pantheon+ is not a direct $H(z)$ table but Type Ia supernova distance data; a direct comparison requires a reconstruction step and the full covariance. A preregistered rerun is an open task. Counter 0/10 |

## 21.12 The triple-CDF Q-Align and φ-manifold PCA test

Runs of the triple cumulative-distribution (X, z, t) Q-Align correlation and of the Φ-manifold principal-component-analysis test are preserved in the archive. It is reported that significant deviations were observed even in fully anonymized upper runs; but the structural error of 21.1 (the absence of a physical common key) also limits these lines. ## 21.13 Null-model tests — C1–C5 and extensions

| Test | Content |
|---|---|
| C1 | Time-dependent weight |
| C2 | Three-component random texture |
| C3 | Constant phase difference |
| C4 | Flat universe ($a(t) = H_0t$) |
| C5 | Constant speed of sound |

No null model rejects Evertophy; but none of them supports it either. The task of the null set is to define "expected failures" in advance. The extension null models (shuffled frequency, deterministic phase, noise only, fixed seed) are listed in full in the archive.

## 21.14 The destruction-test set D1–D5

| Test | Fragility |
|---|---|
| D1 | Parameter removal |
| D2 | Changing constants |
| D3 | Breaking the physics |
| D4 | Adding noise |
| D5 | Change of scheme |

The purpose of the D set is to distinguish whether success comes from physics or from numerical tuning. A finding that does not pass the D tests shows that it is a fit, not a prediction.

## 21.15 Falsification protocol for hysteresis and refresh rate

Tests designed for the hysteresis / refresh-rate signatures expected in a phase transition are preserved in the archive: forward / backward ramps, dwell-time trials, frequency-sweep hysteresis. These tests are not predictions; they are protocols to be applied if the bridge line is supported.

## 21.16 The non-metricity hysteresis test

This is the design of a search for traces of hysteresis due to Weibel / connection anisotropy ($k_\parallel$ and $k_\perp$) and non-metricity. Findings of research on cosmic magnetism (a smallest scale of ~100 kpc for universal magnetic fields) have been incorporated as context.

## 21.17 Catalogue of observational anomalies and the five-anomaly stress test

The five stress-test domains recorded in the source are: early galaxies at $z>10$, the Hubble tension, the "Axis of Evil", the dark matter – MOND debate and the "Little Red Dots". These are the domains in which Evertophy wishes to be tested; for none of them has an Evertophy explanation or prediction been established. The framework's earlier claim about the Hubble tension has been withdrawn three times.

## 21.18 Cosmic birefringence — note on method

The recorded note on cosmic birefringence is methodological only: phase ambiguity and morphology. Observed angle values and significance levels are not used in this document until supported by direct citation of papers. The relation of the signal to the Evertophy reading is an open research question.

## 21.19 The in-phase-wave signature test card (preregistered)

The preregistered test card of the in-phase-wave (IPW) line: the parameter space $(A, \omega, \varphi, \mathrm{FWHM}, \rho)$ for the $X_T$ spectral-decomposition signature is defined; if the condition fails, the line closes. Preregistration is the only way for this line to acquire evidential status.
## 21.20 The BEC / super-atom laboratory package — three falsifiable predictions

| Prediction | Observation | Time window | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Non-Gaussian pre-freezing signature | In a two-dimensional gas | 1–5 years | Open |
| Separation of a phase-wall sigmoid from CPL | Cosmic | 5–10 years | Open |
| Multi-probe simultaneity (BAO / $f\sigma_8$ / lensing) | Cosmic | 10–15 years | Open |

None of them has yet satisfied the six conditions of the 0/10 → 1/10 gate (0.4). The BEC in the name of the package is not c₀ itself but **the environment in the matter phase closest to the c₀ environment** (18.5); laboratory results are read not directly about c₀, but through this closeness.

**Additional preregistration — the Page corner (thought experiment).** In a thought experiment dated 2026-10-13, from the author's definitions according to which c₀ capacity scales with mass ($N=M/m_P$, Section 3), and with the additional definitions in V24 §736 (Y1–Y3) — $N$ alone does not determine the Hilbert-space dimension, the entropy or the correlation curve —, a class of curves departing from standard physics was derived for the measurable correlation between the outside and the core during the outflow of mass from a black hole, and frozen before the data (V24 §736). Today there is no test platform; the preregistration does not satisfy the six conditions of the 0/10 → 1/10 gate and does not change the counter.
