# SECTION 22 — THE NUCLEAR BRIDGE PROTOCOL

This section describes the first concrete design for applying the "derive a range, then compare" protocol.

## 22.1 Target

The target is **the modulation of shell strength** in the nuclear binding structure. That is, not *where* the shells are, but *how strong* they are and how this strength changes with mass.

## 22.1a The nuclear bridge chain — a conditional structural candidate

The target chain of the nuclear programme can be written with a domain reading, without requiring a separate fundamental entity $X_A$:

$$[X_A]\equiv[\Pi_A(X)],\qquad [X_A],[\Delta_{ij}]\longrightarrow[Z_G]\longrightarrow[\alpha_s(Q)]\longrightarrow\mathcal F_{\rm nuc}\longrightarrow[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm pred}$$

```latex
[X_A]\equiv[\Pi_A(X)],\qquad [X_A],[\Delta_{ij}]\longrightarrow[Z_G]\longrightarrow[\alpha_s(Q)]\longrightarrow\mathcal F_{\rm nuc}\longrightarrow[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm pred}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $[\cdot]$ | A range value |
| $\Pi_A$ | The projection of $X$ in the atomic / nuclear context |
| $X_A$ | The nuclear-context reading (not a separate fundamental $X$) |
| $\Delta_{ij}$ | The phase edges |
| $Z_G$ | The kinetic coefficient of the colour gauge field |
| $\alpha_s(Q)$ | The strong coupling at energy scale $Q$ |
| $\mathcal F_{\rm nuc}$ | The nuclear bridge function |
| $[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm pred}$ | The prediction band for the shell gap |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes the chain from the phase structure to the prediction band of the nuclear shell gap |
| **Why does it do it?** | To apply the "derive a range, then compare" protocol to a measured quantity with units and abundant data |
| **How does it do it?** | It goes from the nuclear reading of $X$ to the kinetic coefficient of the colour sector, from there to the strong coupling, the bridge function and the shell gap |
| **What result is expected?** | A preregistered narrow band for the shell gap |
| **What result is obtained?** | Every arrow is **a bridge that must be derived**; having written the chain is not a prediction. $X_A$ is not a re-canonization of the old correlation variable $X_a$ that failed audit; it is only the notation of a context projection within the current single-$X$ ontology. While the protocol architecture can be conditionally established, the derivation from fundamentals to the nuclear level **is open** |

## 22.2 The two-layer ansatz

$$[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm ET} \;=\; [\mu_A] \;\odot\; [\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm yapı}$$

```latex
[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm ET} \;=\; [\mu_A] \;\odot\; [\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm yapı}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm ET}$ | Evertophy's shell-gap band |
| $[\mu_A]$ | The envelope modulation band depending on the mass number ($A$) (Evertophy) |
| $[\Delta_{\rm kabuk}]_{\rm yapı}$ | The structural gap given by the shell model (positions, magic numbers) |
| $\odot$ | Range multiplication |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It writes the shell gap as the range product of a structural part and a modulation factor |
| **Why does it do it?** | To separate formally the fact that Evertophy only modulates the strengths of shells without inventing their positions |
| **How does it do it?** | It leaves the structure to the shell model; it gives Evertophy only the $A$-dependent envelope factor |
| **What result is expected?** | That $[\mu_A]$ is derived from the phase structure and compared with measured shell strengths |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is an ansatz**; $[\mu_A]$ has not yet been derived |

$$\boxed{\;\text{Evertophy DOES NOT INVENT shell POSITIONS — it MODULATES THE ENVELOPE.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Evertophy DOES NOT INVENT shell POSITIONS — it MODULATES THE ENVELOPE.}\;}
```

## 22.3 Four routes

| Route | Method | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| **α** | **Ratios** — cancellation of normalization + ordering map + zero calibration + sealed preregistration | **Primary** |
| β | Single anchor | Secondary |
| γ | Absolute normalization + direct bridge function | Tertiary |
| δ | Residual analysis | Auxiliary |

**Reason for choosing α:** the ratio test is free of normalization; it gives the cleanest intersection test and depends least on the bridge function.

## 22.4 Kill conditions

| Condition | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Prediction–observation intersection empty | Falsified |
| It erases or shifts known shells | **Structure-preservation kill** |
| A mass-dependent coefficient added after the data | **Parameter-budget kill** |
| An active constraint is violated | Kill |
| The gauge range refitted to nuclear data | **Blind-projection kill** |

## 22.5 The data-lineage lock — Strict Blind Lineage

What must be sealed: bridge inputs · parameter-production records · target selection · data version · the subset used for training / calibration, if any · the blind held-out set · the freezing time.

$$\boxed{\;\text{"We sealed it in advance" IS NOT SUFFICIENT BY ITSELF.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{"We sealed it in advance" IS NOT SUFFICIENT BY ITSELF.}\;}
```

The basic independence constraint and the freezing rule are:

$$\boxed{\;D_{\rm türetim}\cap D_{\rm kör}=\varnothing\;}\qquad\qquad \boxed{\;\theta_{\rm köprü}\ \text{is frozen BEFORE the blind data are opened}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;D_{\rm türetim}\cap D_{\rm kör}=\varnothing\;}\qquad\qquad \boxed{\;\theta_{\rm köprü}\ \text{is frozen BEFORE the blind data are opened}\;}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $D_{\rm türetim}$ | The data used in deriving the prediction or in calibration |
| $D_{\rm kör}$ | The data held out for blind verification |
| $\theta_{\rm köprü}$ | Bridge calibration parameters |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It requires that derivation data and verification data do not intersect, and that the bridge parameters are frozen in advance |
| **Why does it do it?** | Both tuning and reporting success with the same data turns a prediction into a fit |
| **How does it do it?** | It keeps the data sets disjoint; it locks the parameters before the blind data are opened; it seals the lineage |
| **What result is expected?** | A genuinely blind test |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a rule of method**; it prevents AME2020 or any other data set from being at the same time a source of calibration and of blind verification |

Three methodological levels are distinguished:

1. **Strongest:** the prediction band is derived from non-target / fundamental inputs; the nuclear target data do not enter the calibration.
2. **Acceptable:** only a preregistered training subset is used; the bridge is frozen; it is tested on a strictly held-out set.
3. **Not a prediction:** the whole target data set is fitted and success is reported on the same set.

## 22.6 Status

This line is followed in the nuclear bridge programme as **a conditional structural candidate**.

$$\boxed{\;\text{PROTOCOL ARCHITECTURE CLOSED} \;/\; \text{DERIVATION FROM FUNDAMENTALS TO THE NUCLEAR LEVEL OPEN}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{PROTOCOL ARCHITECTURE CLOSED} \;/\; \text{DERIVATION FROM FUNDAMENTALS TO THE NUCLEAR LEVEL OPEN}\;}
```

This nuclear bridge line **is not a canonical numerical prediction**; the counter remains at 0/10. That is, *how it is to be tested* is determined; *what is to be predicted* has not yet been derived.

## 22.7 The use of distant targets

Regions of heavy nuclei not yet synthesized **are not made the main target in the first blind run**. Testing is first done in the measured closure regions.

---

## 22.8 The extended semi-empirical mass formula and shell-term corrections

The semi-empirical form in the archive ($E_{B0}$ base + FM two-layer shell correction + $\beta X$ / $\rho\Omega$ relations) is the structural-candidate layer of Section 22. The critical safeguards of 12.4 remain unchanged:

$$\boxed{\;\text{Evertophy modulates the envelope; it does not invent shell positions; the FM correction is an external physical calibration.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Evertophy modulates the envelope; it does not invent shell positions; the FM correction is an external physical calibration.}\;}
```

In this block, the [mod 33] occupancy-element contribution, the [mod 5] correction, the related term and the $\delta_{\rm yüzey}$ layers are preserved as archive records; none of them counts as a prediction.

## 22.9 The extended form of κ_i(Φ, s) (including ∇²Φ)

The archive form of the shell-modulation parametrization is:

$$\kappa_i(\Phi, s) = \kappa_i^{(0)} + \kappa_i^{(1)}\,\frac{\Phi}{\Phi_{\rm ref}} + \kappa_i^{(2)}\,\frac{\nabla^2\Phi}{\Phi_{\rm ref}/L_{\rm ref}^2} + \kappa_i^{(3)}\,s$$

```latex
\kappa_i(\Phi, s) = \kappa_i^{(0)} + \kappa_i^{(1)}\,\frac{\Phi}{\Phi_{\rm ref}} + \kappa_i^{(2)}\,\frac{\nabla^2\Phi}{\Phi_{\rm ref}/L_{\rm ref}^2} + \kappa_i^{(3)}\,s
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\kappa_i$ | The $i$-th shell-modulation coefficient |
| $\kappa_i^{(0)}\ldots\kappa_i^{(3)}$ | Constant coefficients of the expansion (free parameters) |
| $\Phi$ | Phase scalar (historical notation; a reading of $X$) |
| $\Phi_{\rm ref}$ | Reference value |
| $\nabla^2\Phi$ | The Laplacian of $\Phi$ (the curvature effect of the local gravitational environment) |
| $L_{\rm ref}$ | Reference length |
| $s$ | Structure parameter |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It ties the shell-modulation coefficients to the local phase reading, to its second derivative and to a structure parameter |
| **Why does it do it?** | To parametrize the possible effect of the local gravitational environment on shell strengths |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes a first-order expansion; it makes each term dimensionless with reference scales |
| **What result is expected?** | That the coefficients are derived from the phase structure and frozen |
| **What result is obtained?** | All the coefficients $\kappa_i$ **are free parameters**; unless they are frozen, there is no claim of derivation |

## 22.10 The full form of Υ(Z, N; Φ) and the E_B record

The archive form of the modulated binding energy is:

$$E_B(Z, N; \Phi) = E_B^{(0)}(Z, N)\cdot\left[1 + \Upsilon(Z, N; \Phi)\right], \qquad \Upsilon = \sum_i \kappa_i(\Phi, s)\,f_i(Z, N)$$

```latex
E_B(Z, N; \Phi) = E_B^{(0)}(Z, N)\cdot\left[1 + \Upsilon(Z, N; \Phi)\right], \qquad \Upsilon = \sum_i \kappa_i(\Phi, s)\,f_i(Z, N)
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $E_B$ | Binding energy |
| $Z$, $N$ | Proton and neutron numbers |
| $E_B^{(0)}$ | Base (standard semi-empirical) binding energy |
| $\Upsilon$ | Evertophy modulation term |
| $f_i(Z,N)$ | Basis functions depending on shell structure |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It corrects the standard binding energy by a small modulation factor depending on the phase reading |
| **Why does it do it?** | To bring the two-layer ansatz of 22.2 into a concrete and computable form |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes the modulation as a weighted sum of shell basis functions |
| **What result is expected?** | A blind comparison with measured binding energies once the coefficients are frozen |
| **What result is obtained?** | Without frozen coefficients **it carries no predictive status**; it is the concrete archive parametrization of the ruling of 22.6, "protocol architecture closed / derivation from fundamentals to the nuclear level open" |

$$\boxed{\;\text{The } E_B \text{ record carries no predictive status unless the coefficients are frozen.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The } E_B \text{ record carries no predictive status unless the coefficients are frozen.}\;}
```

# SECTION 23 — THE ONTOLOGY OF METHOD

## 23.1 Context and risk

The framework has been developed by a single researcher, using many AI systems as tools. The greatest risk of this environment is to enter **a self-confirming loop**: the tools learn the researcher's language, the researcher adopts the sentences the tools produce, and in the end no one can distinguish what has genuinely been derived from what has merely been repeated. This section gathers the rules set up to break this loop.

## 23.2 The architecture of conflicting multiple systems

Several systems are run simultaneously, and information is **deliberately compartmentalized**. No system sees the whole picture. *(This is the pre-marathon Değnek Ambarı ("baton depot") method. In the 23–26 September marathon, by contrast, all the outputs of each round were given to all participants in a single text; the consensus of systems that saw the shared outputs does not count as fully independent verification.)* The researcher remains the sole integrator; only the researcher decides whether an output enters the canon.

### The rule of source separation

$$\boxed{\;\text{All production is SEPARATED into blocks of ARCHITECT SOURCE and TOOL PRODUCTION.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{All production is SEPARATED into blocks of ARCHITECT SOURCE and TOOL PRODUCTION.}\;}
```

This is not a preference of format but **an epistemic / provenance firewall**. A tool cannot present its own production as evidence.

### A known limit

$$\boxed{\;\text{The CONSENSUS of multiple systems IS NOT INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The CONSENSUS of multiple systems IS NOT INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE.}\;}
```

Since the systems come from similar training distributions, they may drift into the same error at the same time. The closed empirical line (21.1) is a concrete example of this. The general principle of the framework also holds here: **consensus ⇏ canon**.

## 23.3 The standard of verification

A valid verification includes: explicit steps of derivation · symbolic / computational output · reproducible calculation · an audited trail of equations. Two physical checks are also mandatory:

**Scale check.** Does the resulting number lie in a physically accessible range?

**Notation check.** Is the result a function of an arbitrary choice of definition?

$$\boxed{\;\text{ARITHMETIC VERIFICATION IS NOT PHYSICAL VERIFICATION.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{ARITHMETIC VERIFICATION IS NOT PHYSICAL VERIFICATION.}\;}
```

**Author's ruling — binding models.** It is not mandatory to remain bound to theoretical mathematical models that are not derived from observation or experiment; models derived from observation and experiment are binding. For example, the area theorem, which says that the area of a black-hole horizon does not decrease, is an observationally tested constraint; the Bekenstein–Hawking area law, which says that entropy is proportional to area, is by contrast a theoretical result not yet tested by observation. This ruling does not forbid the use of theoretical models.

$$\text{area theorem: }\ \frac{dA}{dt}\ge0\qquad\qquad \text{area law: }\ S_{BH}=\frac{A}{4\ell_P^2}$$

```latex
\text{area theorem: }\ \frac{dA}{dt}\ge0\qquad\qquad \text{area law: }\ S_{BH}=\frac{A}{4\ell_P^2}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $A$ | Horizon surface area |
| $dA/dt$ | The change of area; the theorem says it does not decrease (observationally tested) |
| $S_{BH}$ | Bekenstein–Hawking entropy (theoretical; not directly measured), in units of $k_B$ |
| $\ell_P$ | Planck length |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It separates which external models the framework is bound to and which it is not |
| **Why does it do it?** | To make explicit what is binding in case author's rulings such as the capacity scaling of c₀ (Section 3) conflict with untested theoretical results |
| **How does it do it?** | It classifies the observationally tested area theorem as a binding constraint, and the not-yet-tested area law as a usable but non-binding theoretical result |
| **What result is expected?** | That the framework remains tightly bound to measured physics while staying free with respect to theoretical results |
| **What result is obtained?** | It is an author's ruling. The use of theoretical models is not forbidden; their bindingness is tied to observation |

## 23.4 Classification of numbers

| Class | What |
|---|---|
| **Postulated** | Has neither an external source nor a derivation |
| **External measurement** | Taken from data |
| **Fit** | Fitted to data |
| **In-branch derivation** | Obtained within a model branch |
| **Framework output** | Derived from the ontology |

A number derived from a postulate is **epistemically weaker** than one derived from an external measurement.

## 23.5 The parameter budget

$$\boxed{\;\text{NUMBER OF FREE INPUTS} \;<\; \text{NUMBER OF PREDICTED OBSERVABLES}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{NUMBER OF FREE INPUTS} \;<\; \text{NUMBER OF PREDICTED OBSERVABLES}\;}
```

This rule counters a recurring error pattern: fixing a **product** while leaving the **factors** free.

### Functional freedom

$$D_{\rm model} = N_{\rm skaler} + N_{\rm fonksiyonel} + N_{\rm hiper}$$

```latex
D_{\rm model} = N_{\rm skaler} + N_{\rm fonksiyonel} + N_{\rm hiper}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $D_{\rm model}$ | The total number of degrees of freedom of the model |
| $N_{\rm skaler}$ | Number of free scalar parameters |
| $N_{\rm fonksiyonel}$ | Number of parameters of free functions in a finite basis |
| $N_{\rm hiper}$ | Number of hyperparameters |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It counts the real freedom of a model as the sum of three sources |
| **Why does it do it?** | Free functions carry infinite freedom; counting only scalar parameters hides how much the model "buys" |
| **How does it do it?** | It makes functions countable by parametrizing them in a finite basis, and adds them to the scalar and hyperparameters |
| **What result is expected?** | That $D_{\rm model}$ stays smaller than the number of predicted observables |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a necessary filter**; identifiability and degeneracy must be addressed separately |

### Necessary but not sufficient

$$\boxed{\;\text{Knowing } ab = 6 \text{ is NOT PREDICTING } a \text{ and } b\text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Knowing } ab = 6 \text{ is NOT PREDICTING } a \text{ and } b\text{.}\;}
```

### The bridge budget

The bridges from the core ontology to observable quantities are also priced separately:

| Kind of freedom | Example |
|---|---|
| **Continuous** | A new adjustable coefficient |
| **Functional** | A free map / functional form |
| **Structural** | A new assumption of correspondence, order, selection or topology |

Even if $N_{\rm serbest}<N_{\rm gözlenen}$ holds in a model, a free bridge function or structural choice can in effect take back the prediction.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Passing the parameter budget} \;\nRightarrow\; \text{predictive closure}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Passing the parameter budget} \;\nRightarrow\; \text{predictive closure}\;}
```

## 23.6 Numerology and the error of direction

| Risk | What |
|---|---|
| **Numerology** | The source of the number is unclear |
| **Error of direction** | Its source is known, but it enters as an **input** and is presented as an **output** |

The second is more insidious. A warning sign is also this: the prior probability of encountering a number that is very common in physics is high; the information content of a match is low.

## 23.7 The culture of kill tests

$$\boxed{\;\text{FALSE ENTITY} \;\neq\; \text{OPEN DEBT}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{FALSE ENTITY} \;\neq\; \text{OPEN DEBT}\;}
```

The most dangerous state of a theory is not its open hole but **the false ruling it believes to be closed**. An open debt is an honestly written deficiency; a false entity is an error that appears to have closed the deficiency.

## 23.8 Prohibition of branch-shopping

Alternative models are listed and sealed **in advance**. Looking at the data and choosing the best-fitting branch is forbidden.

## 23.9 Discipline of language

Forbidden: *"proven"* (outside formal proof), *"physical law"*, *"completed theory"*, *"solved"*. Instead: *"is established"*, *"is a candidate"*, *"is open"*, *"is structurally excluded"*, and for experiments *"is a compatible reading"*.

$$\boxed{\;\text{If language loosens, accounting loosens.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{If language loosens, accounting loosens.}\;}
```

## 23.10 The layer of formal verification

The ontological constraints of the framework have been encoded at the type level in Lean-based formal packages. The pre-marathon project inventory contains the following count:

$$\boxed{\;66\ \text{old} + 53\ \text{V03} = 119\ \text{theorem declarations}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;66\ \text{old} + 53\ \text{V03} = 119\ \text{theorem declarations}\;}
```

In the source archive, a clean `lake build` is reported in one run, and in another, independent tool run, **119 theorem declarations and no unfinished proofs**. The same discipline of attribution applies to the `stamp.py` counts and to the tool confirmations of $G_{\rm univ}(M)\simeq F_2$ and $K(M)\simeq\mathbb Z^2$: these were reported in source runs; they do not count as having been executed a second time within this work. Since the relevant ZIP / Lean / Python sources have not been rerun here, the report is not upgraded to "local independent re-verification". This number **is not a count of physical verifications**.

The inventory at the end of the 23–26 September 2026 marathon is:

$$\boxed{\;46\ \text{production} + 223\ \text{archive} = 269\ \text{theorem / proposition declarations}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;46\ \text{production} + 223\ \text{archive} = 269\ \text{theorem / proposition declarations}\;}
```

The archive part contains 66 old, 53 V03 and 104 V24 declarations ($66+53+104=223$); the production part is the single canonical core, isolated from the old interfaces. No declaration contains an unfinished proof ("0 axioms" is a count for the project's own declarations; it does not mean that there are no assumptions at all in the logical foundation of Lean), and the isolation of the production core from the old interfaces has additionally been tested with adversarial test cases. This number is **a formal inventory**, not a count of physical verifications. Moreover, two tools or two AI systems reaching the same result is, without a record of independent execution, not a reproduction but only an agreement of opinion.

Formal records must be separated into five classes:

| Class | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **SPEC** | A specification result coming directly from a definition / axiom |
| **DERIVED** | A theorem derived from general definitions |
| **FINITE-CHECK** | A finite, exhaustive scan / sanity check |
| **PHYS-BRIDGE** | A result requiring an additional bridge between formal structure and physics |
| **POLICY** | An accounting / method invariant |

$$\boxed{\;\text{A FORMAL THEOREM IS NOT A THEOREM OF PHYSICS.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{A FORMAL THEOREM IS NOT A THEOREM OF PHYSICS.}\;}
```

### Open formal debts

- The general associativity and global confluence chain for the rewriting / normal-form reducer — **closed** (formally proven; 14.2).
- Archive–Σ integration, and the entry of the meaning of direction into this integration.
- Uniqueness of `rid` and `WellFormedArchive` (well-formed archive).
- Transition from the integer proxy to an $I(\mathbb Q)$ / real-range structure.
- Separation of the exact character $\omega$ and the coverage range $\Omega_{\rm enc}$ as formal types.
- Parameter / bridge budget accounting of the c₀ ↔ c⁺ $X$-fibre structure.
- The interface for metric reconstruction.
- Preservation of the reserve `U0_bidirectional := false` in a form consistent with the ontological upper layer.
- Independent external compilation / reproduction.

The formal package tests the type consistency of the current ontology; it does not prove the recovery of general relativity / quantum field theory, field equations, or the claim of being the correct theory of nature.

### 23.10a Double sealing — the protocol of archive integrity

In transitions between AI systems and formats, it is not enough to keep only the rendered form of equations. In the archive method, an equation record is preserved, where possible, with two representations:

$$S(E)=\{\mathrm{Skeleton}(E)\in R_{\rm ham},\;\mathrm{Rendered}(E)\in R_{\rm görünür}\}$$

```latex
S(E)=\{\mathrm{Skeleton}(E)\in R_{\rm ham},\;\mathrm{Rendered}(E)\in R_{\rm görünür}\}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $E$ | An equation |
| $S(E)$ | The stored double record of the equation |
| $\mathrm{Skeleton}(E)$ | The raw (LaTeX) skeleton |
| $\mathrm{Rendered}(E)$ | The rendered representation |
| $R_{\rm ham}$, $R_{\rm görünür}$ | Raw and visible record stores |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It stores each equation both as a raw skeleton and in rendered form |
| **Why does it do it?** | To prevent equations from being silently corrupted in encoding repairs and tool transitions |
| **How does it do it?** | It keeps the two representations in separate stores and cross-checks them against each other before any bulk character change |
| **What result is expected?** | That the source of equations is preserved without damage through format transitions |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is not a physical postulate but an archive protocol.** The LaTeX code block following every equation in this document is a direct application of this protocol |

### 23.10b The ontological status of V24 technical results

- **The Cₘ zero-vacuum local-mass proposition (§509.4):** a conditional technical result of scalar–tensor / effective field theory; it is kept in the model layer and is not automatically elevated into the core ontology. The name "zero-vacuum" is a technical model name; it does not contradict or limit the identity c⁺ ≡ vacuum.
- **The Z operator $Z:\Phi\to T$:** defined in the historical family of kinematic dualities; since its correspondence with the old notation $d(x)/Z(x)$ is open, it is not added to the current definition of $X$ as a fundamental element.
- **The layer scheme $X_{\rm durum}\to X_{\rm alan}\to X_{\rm okuma}$:** it is not directly identified with the current "readout faces" of 4.5; the correspondence between them is an open editorial debt.
- **$\Pi_{+\to m}$ and the historical ruling "necessarily through c₀":** it does not count as a current transition law until it is harmonized with the canon of the complete bidirectional triangle; it awaits the architect's decision.

## 23.11 Historical lines and why they were abandoned

| Line | Why |
|---|---|
| The logistic scalar kernel | It was a model; it was not the ontological $X$ |
| Numerical readings of $U_0$ | They reduced $U_0$ to a Cₘ quantity |
| The multi-domain correlation line | A structural artefact |
| Selection of particular CKM / PMNS values from the bare phase topology | Without additional physical selection structure the values are not determined; single-cycle holonomy is an open alternative (19.3) |
| The chain topology | Replaced by the complete triangle |
| The language prohibiting point physics | $[P,P]$ is legitimate |
| The Zero Energy / Flat Universe line | A shelving decision; not a resolved result of the current core |

| The constraint "c⁺ ≠ vacuum" | Removed by author's ruling; the current canon is c⁺ ≡ vacuum |

These records are not deleted; they are kept **together with the reasons why they were abandoned**.

## 23.12 Document boundary for homonymous concepts

Terms such as "bare skeleton" occurring in other works are not the same concept as the c₀ "skeleton" analogy here. A shared word gives no licence to merge, in meaning, concepts belonging to different families of documents.

---

## 23.18 The discard group — discipline of record and justification

Excluded proposals are not deleted; they are preserved together with the reasons for their rejection ("the discipline of negative records"). Kill justifications are kept in a classification: out of scope, insufficient source, deduplication, contradiction.

## 23.19 Tool-sourced epistemic risks (including AI fallacies)

| Risk | Safeguard |
|---|---|
| Content arising from fabrication (hallucination) | Source label mandatory; unverifiable content does not enter the document |
| Common drift into the same error | Visibility ledger; cross-audit |
| Declaring tool output as evidence | The rule of source separation (23.2) |
| Numerical fitting | APPENDIX H source record; an external comparison is not used unless re-verified |

## 23.20 The minimal safe claim / final-ruling output protocol

The minimal safe form of claim for the output of every working run: non-canonical content is carried with the triple "STATUS + SOURCE + SAFEGUARD"; content without a protective sentence does not enter the document. This protocol was applied to the non-canonical sections in the preparatory versions of the monograph; in the publication text, the level of confidence is stated directly in the sentence. The rows "What result is obtained?" in this version are the reader-facing form of the same protocol.

## 23.21 The double-rendering and LODO verification protocol

It is the working-layer extension of double sealing (23.10a): each equation is stored as raw skeleton + visible rendering; LODO (leave one data set out) verification is defined for checking leakage between data sets.
# SECTION 24 — LIMITS AND OPEN QUESTIONS

## 24.1 Six critical questions

| # | Question |
|---|---|
| **1** | What is the state-space decomposition of the phase sectors? |
| **2** | Where are the positions of the phases in field space? |
| **3** | What is the characteristic scale of the gravitational coupling? |
| **4** | What is **the additional principle that makes** the readout map **unique**? |
| **5** | What is the justification for the number of phases and their distribution? |
| **6** | Why is Cₘ a weakly coupled fixed point? |

**And the central gap:**

$$\boxed{\;\text{The CHAIN } X \;\longrightarrow\; \{\text{physical functions}\} \quad \text{HAS NOT BEEN WRITTEN.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The CHAIN } X \;\longrightarrow\; \{\text{physical functions}\} \quad \text{HAS NOT BEEN WRITTEN.}\;}
```

### The formal place of the fourth question

Why the fourth question is hard has been shown definitively. Type rules, range safety, fixed endpoints, monotonicity and well-definedness — even all of these together **do not uniquely select** the bridge from $X$ to a physical quantity; two different bridges can satisfy all of these conditions. This has been shown in Lean for a finite toy model, and by analytic counterexamples in the continuous case.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Type} + \text{range} + \text{well-definedness} \;\nRightarrow\; \text{a unique bridge}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Type} + \text{range} + \text{well-definedness} \;\nRightarrow\; \text{a unique bridge}\;}
```

The conclusion is clear: what will make the readout map unique is **a physical selection principle** and observation. In the weak field, for a response operator with constant coefficients, the linear response can be derived conditionally; but the constitutive map from $X$ to the source, coefficient and boundary data is open. The central gap is no longer only "unwritten"; it is a gap **whose location has been determined**. The general physical selector is a frozen open debt (APPENDIX K.1).

### Equation set — the non-uniqueness of the bridge and distinguishing principles

The **bridge** ($J_X$ / $\mathcal{E}_\phi$) from $X$ to physical quantities in Cₘ is Evertophy's point of contact with observation. The question is: can the rules of type and well-definedness **by themselves select** this bridge?

$$B_1(x) = x, \qquad B_2(x) = x + \tfrac{x(1-x)}{2} \quad (B_2' = \tfrac32 - x > 0) \qquad B_1(\tfrac12) = \tfrac12 \neq \tfrac58 = B_2(\tfrac12)$$

```latex
B_1(x) = x, \qquad B_2(x) = x + \tfrac{x(1-x)}{2} \quad (B_2' = \tfrac32 - x > 0) \qquad B_1(\tfrac12) = \tfrac12 \neq \tfrac58 = B_2(\tfrac12)
```

$$\operatorname{diam} B(I_x) \leq \frac{L_x}{1-q}\operatorname{diam} I_x \ \text{(contraction)}, \qquad \operatorname{diam} B(I_x) \leq \frac{L_x}{\mu}\operatorname{diam} I_x \ \text{(variational)}$$

```latex
\operatorname{diam} B(I_x) \leq \frac{L_x}{1-q}\operatorname{diam} I_x \ \text{(contraction)}, \qquad \operatorname{diam} B(I_x) \leq \frac{L_x}{\mu}\operatorname{diam} I_x \ \text{(variational)}
```

| Distinguishing principle | $B_1$ | $B_2$ | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| Additive superposition $B(x{+}y) = B(x) + B(y)$ | ✓ | ✗ | **Selects uniquely** (Cauchy + monotonicity) — correct |
| Multiplicative composition | ✓ | ✗ | Reduces to the family $x^p$ — partial |
| Reflection symmetry | ✓ | ✗ | Not unique — partial |
| The inference "a variational principle selects by itself" | — | — | **Fails:** $J_1=\int_0^1 \tfrac12 B'^2\,dx \to B_1=x$ and $J_2=\int_0^1 (\tfrac12 B'^2-B)\,dx \to B_2=\tfrac32x-\tfrac12x^2$; both are strictly convex and have a unique solution. The variational method is a mathematically successful alternative; which action is physical is open |
| Finite calibration | — | — | **Fails:** the counterexample $B_\varepsilon$ |
| Conservation alone | — | — | **Fails:** dust and radiation satisfy the same continuity equation |

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $A_{\min}$ | The frozen minimal axioms: A1 type · A2 Range safety · A3 fixed endpoints · A4 monotonicity · A5 single output |
| $B_1, B_2, B_\varepsilon$ | Candidate bridges (on $[0,1]$, with fixed endpoints) |
| $I_x$ | Input range |
| $\operatorname{diam}$ | The width of a range |
| $q$ | Contraction constant ($0\le q<1$) |
| $L_x$ | Lipschitz constant |
| $\mu$ | Strong-convexity constant |
| $J_1$, $J_2$ | Two different action functionals |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It shows two different bridges satisfying all the formal conditions, and tabulates which additional principles narrow the choice |
| **Why does it do it?** | To test the expectation that the bridge could be derived from the rules of type and well-definedness alone |
| **How does it do it?** | It builds two functions that preserve the endpoints and monotonicity; it applies the distinguishing principles one by one to these two functions; it gives contraction and variational bounds for the range width |
| **What result is expected?** | The identification of the physical principle that selects the bridge uniquely |
| **What result is obtained?** | **A no-go result is proven:** the formal rules alone do not select the bridge. Additive superposition selects uniquely; a variational principle alone does not. The finite selector **is a toy model**; the general physical selector is **To Be Addressed Later** |

### Equation set — physical bridge candidates A / B / C and the selection chain

Since it has been shown that the bridge cannot be selected by type rules, this set establishes **which physical principles** narrow the freedom, by how much, and where empirical calibration is unavoidable.

$$\textbf{A:}\ G_{\mu\nu} + \Lambda g_{\mu\nu} = \frac{8\pi G}{c^4}\,T_{\mu\nu}[J_X] \qquad \textbf{B:}\ S = \int\!\sqrt{-g}\Big[\tfrac{M^2}{2}F(\chi)R - \tfrac12 K(\chi)(\nabla\chi)^2 - V(\chi)\Big] + S_m[A^2 g]$$

```latex
\textbf{A:}\ G_{\mu\nu} + \Lambda g_{\mu\nu} = \frac{8\pi G}{c^4}\,T_{\mu\nu}[J_X] \qquad \textbf{B:}\ S = \int\!\sqrt{-g}\Big[\tfrac{M^2}{2}F(\chi)R - \tfrac12 K(\chi)(\nabla\chi)^2 - V(\chi)\Big] + S_m[A^2 g]
```

$$\textbf{C (DD CANDIDATE-01):}\ -\nabla\!\cdot\!(Z\nabla\phi) + \mu^2\phi + 4\pi G\rho = 0,\qquad \phi_{\rm dış} = -C\,\frac{e^{-m r}}{r},\ \ m = \frac{\mu}{\sqrt{Z}}\ \ (\text{constant } Z > 0)$$

```latex
\textbf{C (DD CANDIDATE-01):}\ -\nabla\!\cdot\!(Z\nabla\phi) + \mu^2\phi + 4\pi G\rho = 0,\qquad \phi_{\rm dış} = -C\,\frac{e^{-m r}}{r},\ \ m = \frac{\mu}{\sqrt{Z}}\ \ (\text{constant } Z > 0)
```

In a source-free region with constant $Z$, the equation becomes $\nabla^2\phi = (\mu^2/Z)\phi$; the decay is $m = \mu/\sqrt{Z}$. The earlier notation $e^{-\mu r}$ is correct only for $Z = 1$. In the Einstein equation with physical units, the coefficient is $8\pi G/c^4$ ($8\pi G$ in units with $c = 1$). The field ratio and its inversion:

$$Q(m) = e^{-m(r_2 - r_1)}\,\frac{1 + m r_2}{1 + m r_1},\quad 0 < r_1 < r_2,\ m > 0 \;\Rightarrow\; m \in \big[Q^{-1}(Q^+),\ Q^{-1}(Q^-)\big],\quad 0 < Q^- \leq Q^+ < 1$$

```latex
Q(m) = e^{-m(r_2 - r_1)}\,\frac{1 + m r_2}{1 + m r_1},\quad 0 < r_1 < r_2,\ m > 0 \;\Rightarrow\; m \in \big[Q^{-1}(Q^+),\ Q^{-1}(Q^-)\big],\quad 0 < Q^- \leq Q^+ < 1
```

$Q$ is strictly decreasing, $Q(0) = 1$, $Q(\infty) = 0$; therefore a measured range of $Q$ inverts to a single range of $m$. Inverting $Q$ gives **$m$**; if $Z$ is unknown, the ratio does not determine $\mu$ and $Z$ separately.

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $G_{\mu\nu}$, $\Lambda$ | Einstein tensor, cosmological constant |
| $T_{\mu\nu}[J_X]$ | The energy–momentum tensor of the source coming from $X$ |
| $\chi = \pi_\chi(X)$ | Scalar projection (not to be confused with $\chi_S$ of 17) |
| $M$ | Mass scale (of the order of the Planck mass) |
| $R$ | Ricci scalar |
| $F, K, V, A$ | Free functions of the scalar–tensor theory |
| $S_m[A^2g]$ | The matter action coupling to the metric $A^2g$ |
| $\phi$ | Screened Poisson potential |
| $Z$, $\mu$ | The coefficient field of screened Poisson and the screening mass |
| $\rho$ | Matter density |
| $C$ | Amplitude constant of the exterior solution |
| $m$ | Decay parameter |
| $r_1$, $r_2$ | Two measurement radii |
| $Q(m)$ | The ratio of field strengths (scaled by $r^2$) at the two radii |
| $Q^\pm$ | The endpoints of the measured ratio range |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It builds three concrete candidates for the bridge — the standard Einstein base, a scalar–tensor action and a screened-Poisson test bed — and shows how a measurable ratio inverts to the decay parameter |
| **Why does it do it?** | To see step by step how much each physical principle narrows the freedom of the bridge |
| **How does it do it?** | Starting from Lovelock's theorem, it tabulates what each additional principle fixes and what it leaves (below); in the test bed it writes the exterior solution in Yukawa form and makes the ratio at two radii invertible because it is strictly decreasing |
| **What result is expected?** | A conditional derivation in the weak field; explicit marking of the open ends in the strong field and in the constitutive map |
| **What result is obtained?** | A is **a bridge** (the standard base); B is **a model** (the global P3 subclass); C is **a model** (test bed). The weak-field response is **conditionally derived**; the general $J_X$ is **To Be Addressed Later** |

**The selection chain:**

| Principle | What it fixes | Remaining freedom |
|---|---|---|
| Lovelock's theorem | The geometric side — **under the assumptions**: four dimensions, from the metric only, local / natural, symmetric, divergence-free and of second order in derivatives of the metric ⇒ $aG_{\mu\nu} + bg_{\mu\nu}$ | All in $J_X$ |
| P1 — covariance + conservation | That $T$ is not arbitrary | Every conserved tensor |
| P2 — variational origin (Hilbert tensor) | Makes conservation automatic on shell | $F, K, V, A$ |
| P3 — **globally constrained subclass**: $F \equiv 1$, $K \equiv 1$, $A \equiv 1$ | Constancy of $G_{\rm eff}$; matter coupling | **Only $V(\chi)$** — only under this global assumption |
| P3′ — **local frozen general-relativity matching**: $F(\chi_0) = 1$, $F'(\chi_0) = 0$, $A(\chi_0) = 1$, $A'(\chi_0) = 0$, $V'(\chi_0) = 0$, $V(\chi_0) \propto \Lambda M_{\rm Pl}^2$ (normalization-dependent) | Only the matching around $\chi_0$ | **The global functional freedom of $F, K, A$ does not close**; $A'(\chi_0) = 0$ alone does not give the result "only $V$" |

| Component of $J_X$ | Status |
|---|---|
| Weak-field **response operator** with constant coefficients | Conditionally derived (DERIVED-CONDITIONAL) |
| The constitutive map $X \to (\rho, Z, \mu,$ boundary data$)$ | Open |
| Strong field | Open |
| $V(\chi)$ (in the constrained P3 model) | Empirical calibration ($\dot G/G$, PPN $\gamma$, $w(z)$) |

## 24.2 Other open questions

The frozen list of open debts is in APPENDIX K.1; the following questions are read together with it.

Metric-birth maps · consistency of the potential structure with the number of phases · the risk of a privileged frame · the two-clock problem · global conservation laws · perturbation and stability criteria · the argument family of $X$ · the origin of gauge groups · the internal profile of the c₀ single structure and its quantitative relation to the event horizon · **the $\Gamma/\mathcal G_{C_m}$ double-counting debt:**

$$\Gamma\sim\frac{1}{C_s}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathcal G_{C_m}\sim\frac{1}{C_s}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \frac{dt}{d\lambda}\sim\frac{1}{C_s^2}$$

```latex
\Gamma\sim\frac{1}{C_s}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathcal G_{C_m}\sim\frac{1}{C_s}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \frac{dt}{d\lambda}\sim\frac{1}{C_s^2}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It shows that in the old $C_s$ bridges the sealing cost could enter the time scaffold twice |
| **Why does it do it?** | Counting the same physical effect in two different factors would artificially suppress the time reading quadratically |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes that when both factors scale as $1/C_s$, their product gives $1/C_s^2$ |
| **What result is expected?** | The determination of whether the two factors are independent physical roles or two representations of the same role |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is an open debt**; $C_s$ is in the candidate-reading mode (14.3) |

**The meaning of the reserved headings:** the type of the relation SYNTAX ↔ $U_x$ ↔ $X$ · the width of the phase regime–threshold boundary and its link with $X$ · the full definitions of Phase Collapse and Phase Rupture (Section 25).

**Open questions at the global level:** the mathematical type of the global arrows (it is known that they are not phase gates) · the ontological type of $U_x$ (inclusion or membership) · the function sentence for $U_+$ and the generalized rule of birth · the link $\mathrm{CycleSeed} \dashrightarrow \mathrm{Genesis}$ · an atemporal definition of the cycle index.

## 24.3 What is deliberately left open

$$\boxed{\;\text{The full mathematical realization of } X \text{ is DELIBERATELY OPEN.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The full mathematical realization of } X \text{ is DELIBERATELY OPEN.}\;}
```

The full characterization of the internal structure of the pre-layer of $U_0$, and the **final mathematical type / closure law / joint law of variation of the property families** of $X$, belong to the same category. By contrast, the conceptual role of $X$ is clear in the current core: it is the single, dynamic meta-resolver, sensitive to phase / range / context. What is left open is not its conceptual meaning but its mathematical realization.

## 24.4 Forbidden interpretations — consolidated list

### The global level
$U_0$, $U_x$, $U_+$ **are not** phases and are not placed in the phase triangle · $U_+$ **is not** a fifth node, $c^+$, $X$ or the future · $U_+$ **is not** "the last stop / death" · the global poles **are not thresholds** · the global arrows **are not phase gates**; they connect to **the whole**, not to a phase · global bidirectionality **is not** mathematical invertibility · the global cycle **is not a flow of time or a mandatory periodic circuit** · the correspondence $U_0 : c_0 :: U_+ : c^+$ **is not set up** · the Big Rupture (Absolute Rupture ≡ Absolute Dissolution) **is not identified** with the terminal Big Rip scenario of standard cosmology · **no judgement is passed** on the direct relation $U_0 \leftrightarrow U_+$ · no metric, tetrad, speed or duration **is written** at the global level.

### U₀
It is not a phase · it is not a gate endpoint · it is not a subtype of c₀ · it is not a topological hole · it is not a fibre · it is not a metaphysical entity · it cannot be identified with a numerical mass value · it cannot be confused with $u(0)$.

### c₀
It is not a temporal moment of beginning · time has not "stopped"; it **is not yet established** · the c₀ single structure is not a Planck-sized point; it is a metric-gapless, contiguous, finite single form, and it grows with mass · the BEC is not c₀ (it is the environment in the matter phase closest to the c₀ environment) · the historical over-concrete definitions (full Planck saturation, stasis lock, zero-differential engine) are not the current core.

### c⁺
It **is not** the future · it **is not** a communication channel · Cₘ matter support dissolves · Cₘ metric length / duration / speed are not carried over unchanged · no matter carrying Cₘ-type material / rest mass is assumed · the historical K2 `Dim.mass` record does not count as a current c⁺-local mass observable · it **is not** superluminal matter · a strict identity with the event horizon **has not been established**. *(The item "c⁺ is not the vacuum" of earlier versions has been removed by author's ruling: the current canon is **c⁺ ≡ vacuum**; the vacuum state of quantum field theory is the Cₘ computational language of this identity.)*

### X
It is not the Force of Attraction · it is not gravitation · it is not the metric · it is not a single scalar · it is not a logistic function · it is not a row of the matrix · it is not the average of properties · it is not confined to $[0,1]$ · it is not divided · it is not merely a filter of permission · it is not merely a post-solution readout layer · its supra-phase status is not derived solely from dimensionlessness.

### The Range Formalism
The language "point-valued physics is forbidden" **is not used** · $[P,P]$ is legitimate and requires no special mechanism · being interval-valued is not being time-variable.

### Topology and algebra
The chain topology is not current · the mixture-simplex model is not mandatory · $U_0$ is not made a topological centre · additional topological structures are not imported · the unit and "no stamp" are not identified at the type level · group completion does not mean physical invertibility.

### General
**Map position ≠ phase membership** · **boundary ≠ membership** · $U_x$ is neither a fourth phase nor another name for $X$ · dimensionlessness is not sufficient for transition · structural similarity is not verification · assignment of mechanism is not derivation · arithmetic verification is not physical verification · a formal theorem is not a theorem of physics · existence is not reduced to record · consensus is not canon.

## 24.6 The question of the continuity of X(x) across regimes

Whether $X$ behaves continuously in transitions between different phases / regimes is an open question. The archive record of the risk of a discontinuous limit is preserved: when a candidate type of $X$ is chosen, it must be tested separately whether it produces discontinuities at transitions. This is one of the concrete ends of the decision "the full mathematical realization of $X$ is open" (24.3).

## 24.7 The question of the numerical value of the effective mass m_Ξ

A proposal of a numerical value for the phase-dependent propagator mass $m_\Xi$ exists in the archive; but the value has not been frozen. By the discipline of APPENDIX H, an unfrozen number does not enter this document.

## 24.8 Consciousness — a cautious marker (reserved framework record)

The archive record is preserved in a single sentence: the concept of consciousness has no place in the framework; it has been marked as a "distant target".

$$\boxed{\;\text{Consciousness is not a subject of Evertophy; the observer is a physical system within Cₘ (15.6).}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Consciousness is not a subject of Evertophy; the observer is a physical system within Cₘ (15.6).}\;}
```

## 24.9 The reason for rejecting the Hamiltonian formalism in c₀

The Hamiltonian formalism is rejected in c₀; the reason is the prohibition of importing the structure of Cₘ into c₀ (15.2). Time in c₀ has not stopped; it **has not yet been established**; the language "$dt = 0$" remains only a pedagogical shorthand (3.2). The Hamiltonian itself is not the definition of the state in c₀.

# SECTION 25 — RESERVED ONTOLOGICAL HEADINGS AND MECHANISM CANDIDATES

This section carries the headings that have been **opened but not closed** in the framework. All of them are on record; for some, **the name, the rule of distinction or the decision to include them in the ontology** may have been fixed at the architect level. By contrast, for the headings marked here as open, **the full ontological meaning and / or the mechanism has not yet been closed**. A reserved ontological heading, a mechanism candidate and completed canon do not have the same status:

$$\boxed{\;\text{RESERVED ONTOLOGICAL HEADING} \;\neq\; \text{MECHANISM / THOUGHT-EXPERIMENT CANDIDATE}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{RESERVED ONTOLOGICAL HEADING} \;\neq\; \text{MECHANISM / THOUGHT-EXPERIMENT CANDIDATE}\;}
```

| Class | What | Who opens it |
|---|---|---|
| **Reserved ontological heading** | A structure that has been decided to be added to the framework, but whose full meaning has not been written | The architect only |
| **Mechanism candidate** | An attempt to read a phenomenon in the language of the framework | The working layer; requires separate derivation and preregistration |

The two do not have the same status and must not be confused.

---

## 25.1 The phase regime–threshold boundary — a reserved ontological heading

$$\boxed{\;\textbf{CANONICAL NAME: PHASE REGIME–THRESHOLD BOUNDARY}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\textbf{CANONICAL NAME: PHASE REGIME–THRESHOLD BOUNDARY}\;}
```

For each edge of the triangle, an ontological heading **phase regime–threshold boundary** has been reserved. The name has two components, because the reserved structure was set up to express together the relation between the boundary of a regime and the threshold of a transition. This record does not determine in advance the final mathematical type or the physical thickness of the boundary.

This heading is directly related to the author's ruling of "lying on the boundary" (18.0): a structure on the boundary of the c threshold regime is at the same time on the boundary of the c⁺ regime. The phase regime–threshold boundary is the place of such structures in the ontology; lying on the boundary does not mean being a member of two phases.

### Distinguishing representation from physical width

$$\boxed{\;\text{In the notation of the ontology, the phase regime–threshold boundary is REPRESENTED as A BOUNDARY BAND rather than as a sharp line.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{In the notation of the ontology, the phase regime–threshold boundary is REPRESENTED as A BOUNDARY BAND rather than as a sharp line.}\;}
```

This is **a decision of notation**, not a physical ruling:

$$\text{"a band notation is used"} \;\neq\; \text{"the physical transition is a band of finite width"}$$

```latex
\text{"a band notation is used"} \;\neq\; \text{"the physical transition is a band of finite width"}
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{Whether the physical width of the band is non-zero, and its mathematical profile, HAVE NOT YET BEEN DERIVED.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Whether the physical width of the band is non-zero, and its mathematical profile, HAVE NOT YET BEEN DERIVED.}\;}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It reserves a place in the ontology for the transition region on the phase edges and represents it as a band |
| **Why does it do it?** | Without an explicit heading in which to place boundary phenomena such as light, subatomic structures and the liminal regime, they would be read either as a new phase or as a wrong membership |
| **How does it do it?** | It represents the boundary as a band rather than a sharp line; but it separates explicitly that this is not a claim of physical width |
| **What result is expected?** | The derivation of the mathematical type, physical width and microphysical profile of the boundary |
| **What result is obtained?** | **Canonical:** the reservation of the heading in the ontology and the use of the boundary / band notation. **Open:** the final mathematical type, physical width and microphysical profile of the boundary |

**Not to be confused:** the transitional character of the threshold channels in 12.1 is **a separate model statement** written for those channels; it does not count as physical evidence for the notational decision here.

**What it is not — four safeguards:**

| It is not | Why |
|---|---|
| **A new phase** | The number of phases is three |
| **A new gate** | The three edges are already defined |
| **A change of the triangle topology** | The complete bidirectional triangle is preserved |
| **A new node** | It adds no point to the graph |

**Its purpose:** readability for humans, ontological consolidation and **the placement of boundary phenomena**. The sigmoid character of threshold transitions (12.1) and the liminal regime (15.3) are positioned inside this band.

### Rule of terminology — locked

During the work, an everyday keyword was used for this structure to make it easy to remember. That word is **only a reminder**; it is not an ontological term, and its connotations do not belong to the framework.

$$\boxed{\;\text{The everyday keyword DOES NOT ENTER the ACADEMIC TEXT or THIS DOCUMENT. The only valid name: PHASE REGIME–THRESHOLD BOUNDARY.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The everyday keyword DOES NOT ENTER the ACADEMIC TEXT or THIS DOCUMENT. The only valid name: PHASE REGIME–THRESHOLD BOUNDARY.}\;}
```

This is not a stylistic preference: the connotations of an intermediate state / waiting carried by the everyday word do not coincide with the ontological content of the band (regime boundary + transition threshold) and are open to misreading.

**Combined record of open debts:** the final mathematical type of the boundary; whether its physical width is non-zero and on what it depends; its microphysical profile; its relation to $X$; whether it behaves the same or differently on the three edges.

---

## 25.2 SYNTAX — a reserved ontological heading

SYNTAX has been conceived as the system of rules that determines which configurations are **physically valid**.

$$\text{VALID / INVALID state} \qquad \text{permitted / non-permitted configuration of phase transition}$$

```latex
\text{VALID / INVALID state} \qquad \text{permitted / non-permitted configuration of phase transition}
```

**What it is not:** it is not a new phase · it is not a new physical object · it is not a force or a field.

### The most critical open point

$$\boxed{\;\text{The FULL TYPE of the relation }\text{SYNTAX} \;\leftrightarrow\; U_x \;\leftrightarrow\; X(x) \quad \text{IS OPEN.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The FULL TYPE of the relation }\text{SYNTAX} \;\leftrightarrow\; U_x \;\leftrightarrow\; X(x) \quad \text{IS OPEN.}\;}
```

Three possibilities are on record, and none has been chosen:

| Possibility | Content |
|---|---|
| (a) | SYNTAX **replaces** $U_x$ |
| (b) | SYNTAX is a layer **inside** $U_x$ |
| (c) | SYNTAX takes a separate role **above** $U_x$ |

### The architectural cost of the options — a dependency analysis

The three options record **the historical decision space**; none has been eliminated. But once the two-level architecture is established, their costs are not equal:

$$\boxed{\;\text{Option (a) requires the REDEFINITION of the role of } U_x\text{ as the Dynamic Universe — the highest cost of rewriting.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Option (a) requires the REDEFINITION of the role of } U_x\text{ as the Dynamic Universe — the highest cost of rewriting.}\;}
```

The reason is categorical: in the global ontology $U_x$ is **a universe structure**; SYNTAX is **a system of rules**. In programming terms: *the running state of a program* and *the grammar of the language* are not the same object. This is not a decision but **a dependency analysis**.

### A candidate distinction — not canon

| Object | Candidate definition |
|---|---|
| **SYNTAX** | **A rule structure of admissibility / consistency** that limits which structures or configurations can carry conditions of validity |
| **$X$** | **The resolver of a given state**, which determines which regime and value is resolved in a given full phase / regime / context state |

In this candidate distinction, **it is not assumed that SYNTAX is necessarily a state-independent, context-independent or invariant grammar**. That the applicable constraints may differ according to the phase / regime context is kept open.

In the analogy of chess: SYNTAX is the rules of validity; $X$ is the engine that reads the current board and the valid context and resolves which moves are actually permitted in that state. That the application of rules such as castling, check or en passant may depend on the current state does not require **the rule structure** and **the state resolver** to be the same object. **This distinction is not canon**; it is a candidate that facilitates the decision.

This boundary must become clear — $X$ is already the object that "resolves which values are permitted" (4.1); there is therefore a risk that the two concepts overlap.

**Open debt of decision:** the final ontological / formal category of SYNTAX — whether it is a separate entity, a rule structure, or the family of upper constraints that $X$ must obey — will be closed by the architect's decision and subsequent formalization.

$$\boxed{\;\text{In the V24 writing this relation is NOT REGARDED AS RESOLVED.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{In the V24 writing this relation is NOT REGARDED AS RESOLVED.}\;}
```

---

## 25.3 Phase Collapse — a reserved ontological heading

It has been reserved as a separate ontological phenomenon.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Phase Collapse} \;\neq\; \text{ordinary phase transition}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Phase Collapse} \;\neq\; \text{ordinary phase transition}\;}
```

An ordinary transition is an event that takes place through a gate and can produce a stamp (Section 13). Phase Collapse **is not regarded as synonymous** with this. **Its details are reserved for the architect.** This document does not invent a mechanism; it records the heading and the rule of distinction.

## 25.4 Phase Rupture — a reserved ontological heading

It is a heading separate from and independent of Phase Collapse.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Phase Rupture} \;\neq\; \text{Phase Collapse} \qquad\qquad \text{Phase Rupture} \;\neq\; \text{a new phase or gate}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Phase Rupture} \;\neq\; \text{Phase Collapse} \qquad\qquad \text{Phase Rupture} \;\neq\; \text{a new phase or gate}\;}
```

**Warning on a homonymous term:** This heading **is not the same thing** as the **Big Rupture** at the global level (2.8). The Big Rupture is the global event in which the whole $U_x$ structure reaches the form of $U_+$; there, **Absolute Rupture and Absolute Dissolution express the same thing** (author's ruling). Phase Rupture, by contrast, is a phenomenon at the phase level. A shared word gives no licence to merge meanings.

**A possible phenomenal bridge:** the breaking of the flux tube under colour confinement (25.8). But:

$$\boxed{\;\text{Breaking of the colour flux tube} \;\neq\; \text{Phase Rupture}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Breaking of the colour flux tube} \;\neq\; \text{Phase Rupture}\;}
```

This is only a bridge candidate; it is not an identity.

## 25.5 The Massive Void — a reserved ontological derivation

The observation of great cosmic voids at different wavelengths, and new star formation in these regions. Under the identity c⁺ ≡ vacuum, cosmic voids are among the purest Cₘ readings of c⁺ (Section 3); this heading asks how Cₘ structure can emerge in such regions.

### A source distinction is mandatory

The archive contains **an older account** of this subject. The new derivation is not a repetition of it.

$$\boxed{\;\text{The old account of the Massive Void} \;\neq\; \text{the new ontological derivation}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The old account of the Massive Void} \;\neq\; \text{the new ontological derivation}\;}
```

The two records are not mixed with each other; the new derivation is established with its own justification. **Its details are reserved for the architect.**

## 25.6 Pauli exclusion ↔ the behaviour of the phase regime–threshold boundary — a high-priority mechanism candidate

Relating the Pauli exclusion principle to the behaviour of phenomena at the phase boundary. A possible research chain:

$$\text{phase regime–threshold boundary} \;\rightarrow\; \text{restriction of the accessible state space} \;\rightarrow\; \text{prohibition of double occupancy} \;\rightarrow\; \text{Pauli phenomenology}$$

```latex
\text{phase regime–threshold boundary} \;\rightarrow\; \text{restriction of the accessible state space} \;\rightarrow\; \text{prohibition of double occupancy} \;\rightarrow\; \text{Pauli phenomenology}
```

### Three scientific thresholds

For this line to stop being an analogy and count as a mechanism:

| # | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **1** | The Pauli result must come out **without being assumed from the start** |
| **2** | It must **agree** with antisymmetry, spin-$\tfrac{1}{2}$ and the structure of identical fermions |
| **3** | It must produce a new or previously specified **observational behaviour** |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It proposes reading Pauli exclusion as the phase boundary on which subatomic structures lie restricting the state space |
| **Why does it do it?** | If it is correct that the subatomic level lies on the shared boundary of c⁺ and Cₘ (18.0), this boundary is expected to leave a trace in quantum statistics |
| **How does it do it?** | It writes a chain from the boundary to a restriction of the state space, and from there to the prohibition of double occupancy, and sets three thresholds for the status of a mechanism |
| **What result is expected?** | A derivation of the Pauli result without assuming it, and a new observational behaviour |
| **What result is obtained?** | Unless the three thresholds are met, this line **is at the level of an analogy** |

**Model note (thought experiment of 2026-10-13).** In a thought experiment using the scale–mass phase map, neutron stars fell inside the transition band of the gravitational threshold line (the Schwarzschild-radius line); a quantitative candidate band was obtained in which degenerate fermion support ends just before this threshold (V24 §732). The note meets none of the three thresholds; the line remains in the status of a mechanism candidate. The reading "Pauli guarantees stability" has been rejected: the support collapses above the Chandrasekhar and TOV limits, and Pauli alone is not sufficient for the stability of matter.

## 25.7 c⁺ → Cₘ in the vacuum around a black hole — thought experiment

The question is whether a black hole, through gravitational effect, brings the c⁺ structure in its environment down into the matter phase. Under the identity c⁺ ≡ vacuum, this question is the same as "is matter born from the vacuum around a black hole?"

$$c^+_{\rm yerel} \;\xrightarrow[\text{strong attraction / phase threshold}]{} \; C_m$$

```latex
c^+_{\rm yerel} \;\xrightarrow[\text{strong attraction / phase threshold}]{} \; C_m
```

$$\boxed{\;\text{The black hole does NOT PRODUCE matter FROM NOTHING; it may ACCELERATE (CATALYSE) the transition of } c^+ \text{ content into } C_m \text{.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{The black hole does NOT PRODUCE matter FROM NOTHING; it may ACCELERATE (CATALYSE) the transition of } c^+ \text{ content into } C_m \text{.}\;}
```

The possible observational signature is **a residual**:

$$L_{\rm gözlenen} \;>\; L_{\rm toplanma,max} + L_{\rm spin,max} + L_{\rm depo,max}$$

```latex
L_{\rm gözlenen} \;>\; L_{\rm toplanma,max} + L_{\rm spin,max} + L_{\rm depo,max}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $c^+_{\rm yerel}$ | The c⁺ (vacuum) content around the black hole |
| $L_{\rm gözlenen}$ | The total observed radiative power |
| $L_{\rm toplanma,max}$ | The maximum power that could come from the accretion of matter |
| $L_{\rm spin,max}$ | The maximum power that could come from extraction of rotational energy |
| $L_{\rm depo,max}$ | The maximum power that could come from stored energy |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It asks whether a black hole accelerates the emergence of Cₘ matter from the surrounding vacuum, and defines its trace in the radiation budget |
| **Why does it do it?** | If c⁺ ≡ vacuum and the c⁺ → Cₘ edge exist, to test whether this edge has an observable effect in the strongest environment of attraction |
| **How does it do it?** | It adds the maximum contributions of all known energy sources; if the observed power exceeds this, it looks for a residual. A stronger signature would be the emergence of new Cₘ matter when there is no sufficient baryonic source in the environment |
| **What result is expected?** | An excess of radiation or matter that cannot be explained by the standard sources |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a thought experiment**, not canon. These signatures **are not predictions**: to be a prediction, they must satisfy the six conditions of the 0/10 → 1/10 gate (0.4) |

---

## 25.8 Colour confinement ↔ phase limit / SYNTAX — a strong derivation candidate

The chain of quark confinement, the stretching and breaking of the flux tube, and pair production.

**Two possible roles in the framework:**

| Phase | Role |
|---|---|
| c₀ | The **sustainability limit** of Cₘ configuration |
| c⁺ | Access to a new Cₘ state — the side of **pair production** (c⁺ ≡ vacuum) |

**The SYNTAX reading (candidate):**

$$\text{free colour state} = \text{INVALID} \qquad\qquad \text{colour-neutral hadron} = \text{VALID}$$

```latex
\text{free colour state} = \text{INVALID} \qquad\qquad \text{colour-neutral hadron} = \text{VALID}
```

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It reads colour confinement as a validity rule of SYNTAX and as a consequence of the phase limits |
| **Why does it do it?** | The measured phenomenology of confinement (no free quarks; pair production when the flux tube breaks) fits naturally into the language of valid / invalid configurations |
| **How does it do it?** | It counts the free colour state as invalid and the colour-neutral hadron as valid; it reads the breaking of the flux tube as the birth of new Cₘ pairs from the c⁺ (vacuum) side |
| **What result is expected?** | The derivation of the SU(3) structure and of confinement from the phase structure (20.10) |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a strong derivation candidate.** It is read together with the constraint of 18.4: calculations of pair production from the vacuum assume **a given metric** and cannot be applied to c₀. The part of the chain that remains within Cₘ is legitimate |

## 25.14 The kinematic duality postulate — full form and the Z-operator bridge

The historical duality in the archive (the same source as the record of 4.2) is preserved here in full form:

$$\Phi(c^+, C_m, c_0) = \mathrm{T}(z^+, z_m, z_0),\qquad \Phi\,e^{+i\pi} = \mathrm{T}\,e^{-i\pi}$$

```latex
\Phi(c^+, C_m, c_0) = \mathrm{T}(z^+, z_m, z_0),\qquad \Phi\,e^{+i\pi} = \mathrm{T}\,e^{-i\pi}
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\Phi$ | The historical "phase" side (not identical with $\Phi_\chi$ in 17) |
| $\mathrm{T}$ | The historical "time–geometry" side; its type is incomplete |
| $z^+$, $z_m$, $z_0$ | The historical coordinates corresponding to the three phases |
| $e^{\pm i\pi}$ | Opposite phase factors (both equal to $-1$) |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It proposes a historical duality between the phase side and the time–geometry side |
| **Why does it do it?** | In an early layer it was an attempt to unite the phases through a kinematic duality |
| **How does it do it?** | It equates the two sides and writes two phases of opposite direction in the opposite-phase form |
| **What result is expected?** | A dynamical law through the Z operator ($Z:\Phi\to\mathrm{T}$) |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a historical record.** Since $e^{+i\pi}=e^{-i\pi}=-1$, the opposite-phase form reduces algebraically to just $\Phi=\mathrm{T}$. The Z operator is not added to the current definition of $X$ as a fundamental element (23.10b) |
## 25.15 Separation of three metric regimes — g(0) / g(m) / g(+)

This is the proposal of separating three metric readings for the three phases: $g^{(0)}$ the c₀ reading (not admissible — 9.3), $g^{(m)}$ the Cₘ metric (the only legitimate domain), $g^{(+)}$ the c⁺ reading (not admissible). The proposal of separation is a design of notation; it does not bring the metric back into the outer phases.

## 25.16 The cosmological bridge X(x) ≈ ln(a)

A candidate bridge between Hubble expansion and $X$:

$$\xi_{\rm koz}(x)=\pi_{\rm koz}[X](x)\approx\ln a$$

```latex
\xi_{\rm koz}(x)=\pi_{\rm koz}[X](x)\approx\ln a
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $\pi_{\rm koz}$ | The cosmological readout projection of $X$ |
| $\xi_{\rm koz}$ | The cosmological reading |
| $a$ | Cosmic scale factor |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It ties the cosmological reading of $X$ to the logarithm of the scale factor |
| **Why does it do it?** | To look for a simple, testable bridge between the expansion history and the resolver |
| **How does it do it?** | It equates the reading with $\ln a$; it also keeps generalized forms in the archive |
| **What result is expected?** | Contact with cosmological data |
| **What result is obtained?** | **It is a candidate bridge**, not canon. Since $X$ itself is not a scalar, the archive notation "$X(x)\approx\ln a$" is written here through a reading $\xi=\pi(X)$. The point of contact is the goodness-of-fit check of 21.11 |

## 25.17 The correspondence Krylov saturation ↔ ξ_sat → ξ_sat^max

This is the proposal of mapping the saturation of the Krylov subspace of quantum evolution onto the extreme regime of the saturation reading of $X$. The archive notation "$X\to1$" is expressed in the current notation through the saturation reading:

$$\xi_{\rm sat}=\pi_{\rm sat}(X)\;\to\;\xi_{\rm sat}^{\max}$$

```latex
\xi_{\rm sat}=\pi_{\rm sat}(X)\;\to\;\xi_{\rm sat}^{\max}
```

It is a candidate bridge; numerical verification is open.

## 25.18 The axiom set of global unitarity + local Kraus

This is the axiomatic statement of the two-layer structure of 16.6. It is a model layer; it is not a fundamental element of the ontology.

## 25.19 The reading light = consistency constraint

This is the archive extension of the reading of $c$ as the consistency constraint of Cₘ (3.3): the speed-of-light limit is treated not as a prohibition on the speed of matter but as a limit of information processing / record / resolution. $v=c$ is the liminal shared boundary of Cₘ and c⁺. This reading is consistent with the core ruling; it adds no additional mechanism.

## 25.20 Structural dissolution — a scenario of constant drift

A scenario of the long-term dissolution of the framework's structures (celestial bodies, nuclei): a chain of orbital decay → instability → disintegration. The scenario is a design; it is not an observational commitment.

## 25.21 The end of the universe — record of two scenarios

The four-stage narrative in the archive (orderly end → gradual dissolution → stagnation → infinite dilution) has been aligned with the current two-pole language (2.8): the two scenarios are tied to the global poles. The end in the direction of $U_+$ is called Absolute Rupture ≡ Absolute Dissolution, and it is at the same time a new birth. An apocalyptic (eschatological) reading is forbidden (2.6).

## 25.22 The claim of a cosmic super-atom

This is the claim of reading the universe as a "super-atom"; it is the cosmic extreme of the BEC / super-atom family of analogies. It is at the level of an analogy; no real identity has been established. The BEC is not c₀ (18.5).

## 25.23 The translation matrix — readout transformation Cₘ ↔ outer phase

This is the working matrix in which concepts of the outer phases are translated into the Cₘ reading: rules for carrying the terms record / measurement / vacuum / phase boundary between the two layers. It is the working tool of the ruling "transport ≠ readout" of 13.1a.

## 25.24 Phase conductivity

This is the proposal of modelling the transfer of effects between phases in the language of "conductivity". The notation of electrical conductivity is borrowed; structural similarity is not verification (17.3).

## 25.25 The phase-synchronization mechanism

This is a candidate for realizing the c₀ ↔ c⁺ $X$-coordination (13.6) as a synchronization mechanism. The mechanism has not been written; the heading is a programme record. Synchronization here is an atemporal relation of agreement; it does not assume a physical flow of time.

## 25.26 The universe core — calculation of three scenarios

The three calculation scenarios in the archive for the universal core scale (locked to the CMB, CMB+MPC, CMB+SN) and the window of internal consistency (~$10^{47}$ light years). The scenarios are the computational body of 2.10; the numerical results are under the discipline of APPENDIX H.

## 25.27 The dynamic-attraction template

These are the template records used for dynamic attraction in relativistic stellar models (the Mestel star in special relativity; stellar models in general relativity; rotation, frame dragging). It is a context record; it is not an Evertophy derivation.

## 25.28 Readings of the single structure inside a black hole

The archive prediction records for the interior of a black hole (shadow signatures, universal Boyer–Lindquist scales) are preserved; they carry no predictive status. In the current version the status of two elements has changed: the **metric-gapless single structure** and **density not increasing with mass** (a more massive black hole is not denser; the volume grows with mass) are no longer historical project claims but **author's rulings** (Section 3). **Exact proportionality** of volume to mass, by contrast, holds in the constant-density realization ($\rho_{c_0}=\rho_*$), in which the mean density is independent of mass:

$$N_{c_0}=\frac{M}{m_P},\qquad \rho_{c_0}\le\rho_P,\qquad V_{c_0}\ge N_{c_0}\,\ell_P^3\;\propto\;M,\qquad R_{c_0}\propto M^{1/3}\ \ (\rho_{c_0}=\rho_*\ \text{constant})$$

```latex
N_{c_0}=\frac{M}{m_P},\qquad \rho_{c_0}\le\rho_P,\qquad V_{c_0}\ge N_{c_0}\,\ell_P^3\;\propto\;M,\qquad R_{c_0}\propto M^{1/3}\ \ (\rho_{c_0}=\rho_*\ \text{constant})
```

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $N_{c_0}$ | The count of core capacity in units of the Planck mass |
| $M$ | The mass of the black hole |
| $m_P$ | Planck mass |
| $\rho_{c_0}$ | The density of the single structure |
| $\rho_P$ | Planck density ($\approx5.16\times10^{96}\ \mathrm{kg/m^3}$) |
| $V_{c_0}$ | The volume of the single structure |
| $\ell_P$ | Planck length |
| $R_{c_0}$ | The radius scale of the single structure |

| Question | Answer |
|---|---|
| **What does it do?** | It fixes, according to the author's ruling, the relations of mass, density and volume of the single structure inside a black hole |
| **Why does it do it?** | These relations, which stood in the old record as "historical project claims", were taken into the core with the author's approvals of 2026-10-14 |
| **How does it do it?** | It counts capacity in Planck masses; it bounds density by the Planck density; it writes that in the constant-density realization volume grows in direct proportion to mass and the radius scales with the cube root of mass |
| **What result is expected?** | The derivation of the internal profile and of the quantitative relation with the event horizon; the prediction of shadow and scale signatures from this structure |
| **What result is obtained?** | The single structure and density not increasing with mass **are author's rulings**; exact proportionality is a consequence of the constant-density realization; the internal profile of the single structure, its relation with the horizon and its observational signatures **are open debts** (APPENDIX K.1). The shadow and Boyer–Lindquist records are not predictions |

## 25.29 The positioning of redshift

This is the record of positioning cosmic redshift on the phase map. It has the same status as the redshift bridge row of 11.7.

## 25.31 The Evertophy periodic table / a Mendeleev-style domain table

This is a proposal of presenting the phase-parameter matrix (Section 5) at the level of a domain table. It is a tool of representation; it adds no ontological content.

This is a thought experiment of transforming frequency / energy scales between phase readings; it belongs to the same family as the wavelength-factor layer of 25.9.

# SECTION 26 — PHENOMENA, OPEN PROBLEMS AND EVERTOPHY'S CANDIDATE READINGS

This section presents together, in a map of headings, established physical phenomena, theoretical open problems and Evertophy's candidate readings of them. The appearance of a heading here does not mean that modern science cannot explain that phenomenon, or that Evertophy has solved it; each row is evaluated with its own source and derivation status.

$$\boxed{\;\text{Every reading is "a candidate"; none is "solved". The whole of this section is subject to the language discipline of 0.3 and 23.9.}\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Every reading is "a candidate"; none is "solved". The whole of this section is subject to the language discipline of 0.3 and 23.9.}\;}
```

Each row in this section is **a reading, not an explanation.** The phenomenon measured by modern science is accepted as it is; the reading Evertophy proposes is placed on top of that measurement as a separate layer and is a candidate for testing (From the Author to the Reader). The appearance of a reading in this list does not mean that it has been derived quantitatively or verified. The section lists established phenomena (measured, but with a debated explanation) and theoretical open problems together; the status of each row is separate. This section makes no contribution to the counter:

$$\boxed{\;\text{Canonical numerical-prediction counter: } 0/10\;}$$

```latex
\boxed{\;\text{Canonical numerical-prediction counter: } 0/10\;}
```
